# 0002 — Open Questions

**This file answers exactly one question: *what still needs answering.*** Nothing else. It does not
say what is done, what to build next, or what a document gets wrong.

| Question | File |
|---|---|
| What is **done** | [`0003`](0003-build-plan.md)'s slice ledger, with [`0000`](0000-board.md) → *Done* as its view |
| What is **next** | [`0000`](0000-board.md) → *Do these next* — the only place that can order three tracks against each other |
| What **needs answering** | **here** |
| What a document says **wrongly** | [`0012`](0012-corpus-audit.md), plus [`0000`](0000-board.md) → *Owed — documentation debt*. A correction is not a question: nobody has to decide anything, somebody has to type |

**Why this file was restructured.** It was organised by *which session raised a question* and had
grown to fifteen session sections, so answering *what is open* meant reading all of them and diffing
them in your head. Nobody did, so the board — a self-declared **view** — quietly became the ledger and
carried 63 open items while the file named *open questions* carried none. That is the same defect
[`0012`](0012-corpus-audit.md) diagnoses in the status column, one column over. The session record is
retained in full below the ledger, unchanged; it is history and stays that way.

**The rule that keeps it true.** Every open question in the corpus appears **here, once**. A design
document's own *Open questions* section may restate an entry for a local reader; it may never hold one
this ledger lacks. When a question closes, it closes in an ADR or the owning document and is **struck
here in the same sitting** — striking rather than deleting, because a reader needs to see that it was
answered rather than dropped.

### How to read an entry

Every entry is typed, per [`adr/0043`](../docs/adr/0043-a-claim-a-measurement-could-settle-must-not-be-settled-by-argument.md):

- **M — measurable.** You can name the number that would refute it and the machine that would produce
  it. It is routed to a named spike and **no document may cite it as decided until the number exists.**
- **A — arguable.** No measurement settles it. A session may close it.

An entry with no type is an entry nobody has looked at properly, and it is the shape that let two
measured-false claims sit in 🟢 rows of the coverage map — which is now **§F** below, promoted out of
the archive and rebuilt. **§F is where you look for *what has never been examined at all***, as
opposed to §A–§E, which are questions somebody has already framed.

Three levers: raise the **Temperament** threshold, shrink the **Sight Horizon** toward its 1-Segment floor, or **let a diversion rejoin the Habit Route without re-searching** — which nothing in the corpus proposes and which is free by construction. ~~**Arguable, not measurable**~~ — **session M reversed the typing on its own question**, which is `adr/0043` turned on a session's output rather than on a document. A release does not consult it (a departing car knows its Bin), and *a parking search* under the other reading is **refused by that ADR's own title**, which is `adr/0070`'s one class of absence that counts as evidence. **Both readings resolve to the same event**, which is why it could not be typed. **What falls out is the rate, and it is what R5.6 could observe but not explain**: a shed's use rate is the **arrival** rate, where a route's is the Trip-start rate **plus every diversion** — R6.3's 1,269.51 a Tick against 32–147 affordable. *Checked at use* is therefore a far cheaper bill on a shed, by a mechanism rather than by observation. **The rung is per-Segment witnessed by paths** and the shed inherits `adr/0012`'s **shape and not its parameter: no `T`, no rotation, no proximity wake** — because a stale shed returns a Bin that exists, has capacity and is merely not the nearest, so its addition error is **bounded by the radius and already priced by the Commute Budget**, where a stale route's is unbounded. **The board's framing was right about the answer and wrong about the axis**: it asks whether the contract is one contract *"since a Parking Shed is a neighbourhood rather than a path"*, and the geometry is real but is not what decides it — **the magnitude of the addition error is**. The residual case is named rather than waved away (a **full** shed where a new road would have reached an emptier one) and accepted, with a refuting number written into the ADR's revisit trigger. `05 §3`'s *when you pay / what survives* correction is **paid**. *Original row follows.* **Settled per-Segment for the *route* consumer (96% retention against a single counter's 9%, and cheaper) — but per-Segment is the **worst rung available** under road addition, and the Parking Shed is the other consumer, scaling with Buildings rather than paths | A | session **M** | **MEASURED by S2 R5.6 for the second consumer, and it agrees — but by a different mechanism.** Per-Segment witnessed by *paths* is the only shed rung that fits: worst case **26.10% of a Tick** against per-cluster's **1,351.24%** and global's **1,638.20%**, with the global tripwire firing exactly as `plans/0010` wrote it (159,825 sheds, 255.560 ms, paid **on arrival**). **`plans/0010`'s prediction that per-cluster would suit sheds is measured false** — it is the worst survivor, 127× the paths rung, and the Arterial gesture shows 100% of its invalidation is wrong. **`CONTEXT.md` → Epoch is unblocked and the rung is per-Segment on both consumers.** What is *not* settled is the same thing it never was: **per-Segment is unsound under road addition** for sheds exactly as for routes, and routes bought their way out with a temporal answer (R5.5.4's TTL rotation) that sheds do not need. **Two consumers, one rung, two soundness stories** — and **the route half is now closed.** `adr/0012` carries the contract: the addition story is a **bound checked at use** with a proximity wake over it, and *not* a rotation. **The shed half inherits the shape and not the parameter**, which is still session M's. Worth noting the shape explains something R5.6 could only observe: a shed is consulted far less often than a route is driven, so *checked at use* is a much smaller bill there — that is a better account of *sheds need no rotation* than the one R5.6 gave. What nobody has typed is what a shed's **use** is — a parking search, or a Trip arrival |

| ~~**⚠ What a Trip's *destination* is, and the recommended generator's destination set does not exist.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-12 by a sitting, and it closed by being VOID AS POSED** — [`adr/0080`](../docs/adr/0080-phase-4-does-not-wait-on-a-trip-generator-and-a-trip-is-entered-by-command.md) and [`adr/0081`](../docs/adr/0081-the-commute-is-the-first-trip-generator-and-a-job-is-taken-by-satisficing-on-distance.md). **The fact that settled it is one this row nearly had and stopped one step short of.** The row asks *which generator has a destination set that exists today* and answers *none*; what it did not check is `06`, where **all seven** candidate generators sit in *Mechanisms with no milestone*. Phase 2 as sequenced runs 5b → 5c → 6 → 22 → 23 → 8 → 19 → 20 → 10 and **not one of those milestones produces a place a person would go**, so the question has no answer at any point in the plan and no reordering produces one. Under [`adr/0070`](../docs/adr/0070-an-unbuilt-mechanism-is-not-a-design-constraint.md) that is the void form — *given X does not exist, should Y compensate?* — and the answer is **build X**, which nothing was scheduled to do. Three consequences. **`06` gains milestone 5b-bis**, jobs and the commute, half-discharging the *Office, wages, the labour market* row. **Milestone 5b closes on tasks 1, 2, 3, 5 and 7**, because its named risk — *a single-Leg Trip model propagates* — was retired by `adr/0075` and tasks 1–3 and never depended on the generator; tasks 4, 6 and 8 move to 5b-bis, each measuring a **distribution** that cannot be honest without one. And **Phase 4 is not blocked**: a `CommandKind.Trip` puts a Trip in the Input Log on `Populate`'s precedent, so the Traveller cursor, volume attribution, Fate resolution and the Census sink `adr/0006` requires all ship now. **Two findings outlive it.** The **sampler was refused on the wrong warrant** — `0021` cites `adr/0069`, which would refuse `--trips` too; the real reason is that every measurement tasks 4/6/8 exist for is a property of the O-D distribution, and **S2 R4 already measured what a fabricated draw does** (18.52% → 128.82% detour, *"a different city"*). And **the precondition all three candidates shared was named by nobody**: there is no query from a place to nearby Buildings anywhere in `Borough.Core` — `CellResidency` indexes Layer cells, `Frontage` maps Lot→Segment one way — so it is 5b-bis's *first* task rather than the generator's implementation detail. *Original row kept below the strike, because it is the record of a question that was framed correctly and answered one field short.* **⚠ ORIGINAL:** [`0021`](0021-trips-legs-and-the-pedestrian-layer.md) decision 1 recommends **shopping** ([`adr/0067`](../docs/adr/0067-a-shopping-attempt-is-a-trip-and-a-household-tries-one-provider-per-occasion.md)) as 5b's generator and calls it *"the rarest starting position a task in this project has had"* — settled design, designed failure mode, cost already booked. **That is true of its design and false of its preconditions**, and 5b task 4 is the next task in the slice | A | **a sitting, before 5b task 4** | **Four things the generator needs, none of which exist.** There is **no Provider List** — [`adr/0066`](../docs/adr/0066-the-provider-list-is-an-intrusive-index-list-and-its-ruleset-length-is-a-cap-rather-than-an-allocation.md) says so in its own words (*"nothing in code has to change, because the Provider List has never been built"*), and how an entry is learned or forgotten is undecided while its length is stated three different ways. There is **no shop**: both shipped Rulesets declare exactly one Building kind, `dwelling`, and nothing in the loader's key set expresses selling. **The purchase mechanism throws** — `Scope.Pool`, the term that crosses an ownership boundary, is a named hole, which is why `02 §4.3`'s own bakery example is unreachable and why slice 10b was re-filed to Phase 2. And **nothing schedules a shopping occasion**; §B-1's rate is open and 5b may not choose it. **The trap is named in advance, because it is the one 5b already spotted on the other axis**: the brief refuses *"give every Household a synthetic workplace"* as a compensating position under `adr/0070`, and **"give every Household a synthetic shop" is the same move wearing a different noun.** So the sitting's real question is not *which generator* but *which generator has a destination set that exists today* — and the honest candidates are a **return-home** Trip, whose destination is a Building that demonstrably exists, or building enough of the Provider List to make shopping real, which is a slice rather than a task |
| ~~**⚠ How long is a Tick?**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-12 by a sitting, into [`adr/0082`](../docs/adr/0082-the-behavioural-clock-is-global-and-car-following-sub-steps-inside-it.md)** — *the behavioural clock is global and car-following sub-steps inside it*. **A Tick is 10.546875 s of in-world time, a Day is 24 in-world hours, `TICKS_PER_DAY = 8192` stands, and the Commute Budget is denominated at 5.6889 Ticks per clock minute** — which is the only thing **5b-bis** needed from it. `adr/0019` is **amended, not superseded**: its conclusion survives whole and its derivation is replaced, so **no State Hash moves and no baseline is re-recorded**. **The row's own framing was right and its arithmetic was the thing that broke.** It says *three answers, 73× apart*; it is **two clocks, not three** — `adr/0019`'s 0.2326 s and `02 §1.2`'s 0.144 s are within 1.6× of each other and are both car-following figures, and the calendar is 45–73× from both. **The global fine clock is refused on measurement rather than on preference**: an in-world Day is 371,420 Ticks at that resolution, so a Tick must cost 0.192 ms against S0a's **0.112 ms empty Tick** and S0b's **8.72 ms with work in it** — **108× short**, and `adr/0019`'s entire derivation predates any Tick figure taken over a city. **Three findings outlive it.** ***A degree of freedom is spent by the first document that uses it, and nothing announces the spending*** — `adr/0019` calls both exchange rates free, and the `[roads]` table spent one, in another file, years of documents later, with no edit to the ADR making it false; that is [`adr/0070`](../docs/adr/0070-an-unbuilt-mechanism-is-not-a-design-constraint.md) read backwards, reasoning from a **freedom** rather than an absence. **There are two Ticks→seconds rates wearing one name** — the host's wall-clock one, genuinely free, and the in-world one, derived — and `adr/0019`'s table has a row only for the first, **which is why it believed its headline ratio was invariant**. ⚠ **It is not, and the number moves 4.3×**: *480 Ticks ÷ 8192 = 5.9% of a life spent driving* is **1.4%** corrected (a 16.4 km crossing at 50 km/h is **112 Ticks**, not 480), so **the corpus has been assuming ~4× the standing traffic the shipped numbers produce** — re-derivation filed to `0013` and **5b-bis**, not applied here, because netting a ratio against a distribution by hand is how the in-flight band went wrong the first time. And **the argument that survived was the one listed last**: of `adr/0019`'s three reasons for declining 4096, visual honesty falls with the 65×-adrift column it rests on and queue fidelity moves to the sub-step ratio, leaving *within-Day scheduling resolution* — the makeweight — as the whole of what sets `TICKS_PER_DAY`. **One new number opened**, the sub-step ratio, in §D2 below. *Original row kept below the strike, because it framed the question correctly and is the record of two ratified numbers contradicting.* **⚠ ORIGINAL: The shipped code answers it once, the design answers it twice more, and the three are 73× apart.** `Speed.FromKilometresPerHour` derives its conversion from *"a Day is 86,400 s over `Ticks.PerDay` Ticks, so a Tick is 10.546875 s"* — so a 50 km/h car covers **36.6 Tiles/Tick** and a 5 km/h pedestrian **3.66**. [`adr/0019`](../docs/adr/0019-ticks-per-day-is-a-balance-constant-not-a-pacing-knob.md) says there are **no seconds in the library**, derives `TICKS_PER_DAY` **from** car-following resolution at **4.2 m/Tick**, and records the Tick→real-seconds mapping as owned by the host with value **"none"**. `02 §1.2` states **~0.5 Tile/Tick**. **Every authored `*_speed_kph` in every Ruleset takes the first path** — so the code runs a conversion two design documents say does not exist, **and gets the right answer**: `--trips` reports a median walk of **1.4 min at 128 m** and **18.3 min at 2,048 m**, which is 5 km/h to the digit. **This is not an outage. The code is right and the documents are not**, which is why it is a question rather than a defect | A | **a sitting, before 5b task 6** | **The question is not which speed is right — it is that one number is being asked to satisfy two independent constraints, and it cannot.** ① **Behaviour**: a day's travel must fit in a Day, or the Commute Budget, schedules and Life Stages are meaningless. A 3 km walk is 205 Ticks (**2.5% of a Day**) at 3.66 Tiles/Tick and **1.8 Days** at `02 §1.2`'s figure. ② **Interaction resolution**: a vehicle must advance a small fraction of its safe following distance per step, or Lane queues cannot form — which is the whole ground on which [`adr/0007`](../docs/adr/0007-stress-driven-simulation-detail.md) rejected *vehicles as animation*. At 146 m/Tick a car clears a 128 m Segment in **0.9 Ticks**. **They are 73× apart and both are real.** *(Appearance is **not** a third constraint, and treating it as one is the error underneath `02 §1.2` — see below.)* **The asymmetry that makes this tractable is that only ② is a *sub-model's* constraint.** Car-following is a continuous coupling; **walking is not** — `03 §3.8` already says the interpolation argument *"holds for walkers permanently"* and that local avoidance *"must not touch arrival time"*. A walk Leg is a departure, an arrival and a path, and nothing between them a finer step would reveal, so the pedestrian layer 5b is building **needs nothing from this question but the number**. So the shape of the answer is almost certainly **the behavioural clock globally, with the Microscopic Lane kernel sub-stepping inside Tick phase 4** — two nested clocks, which is what every reference game does and what `05 §1`'s *fixed sim Tick, interpolated render* already establishes the precedent for. **The measurable rider, and it is the only part a machine can settle**: sub-stepping multiplies S5's 27.4 ns-per-vehicle kernel by the step ratio, which drops the **Microscopic Cap** from `0013`'s ~532,750 to roughly **15,000** — routed to **S5**, and it is a *second* reason that Cap is unset rather than a new question. **What is arguable and must be argued**: whether `adr/0019`'s derivation survives. It ran *car-following resolution → Ticks per commute → Ticks per Day → and only then a Day length in real seconds, chosen freely afterwards*, which propagates one sub-model's requirement to the global tick rate; if the sub-model gets its own clock, the chain's first arrow is cut and `TICKS_PER_DAY` is free to be chosen for behaviour instead. That is an **amendment to `adr/0019`**, not a defect in `Speed.cs`. **Why it is in §A rather than §D**: it is not an unratified number, it is **two ratified numbers that contradict**, and the [Commute Budget](0021-trips-legs-and-the-pedestrian-layer.md) — 5b task 6, unset, hash-bearing — is denominated in whichever wins |

## B — Measurable: routed to a machine

**No document may cite any of these as decided.** Where no spike is named, the entry is owed one.

> **⚠ Every row routed to *Trip generation, `06` 5b* now reads `06` **5b-bis**, swept 2026-08-12, and
> the sweep found two rows that did not survive it.** The §A sitting moved Trip generation out of 5b
> into a new milestone ([`adr/0080`](../docs/adr/0080-phase-4-does-not-wait-on-a-trip-generator-and-a-trip-is-entered-by-command.md)),
> so nine rows here and in §D2 changed owner mechanically. **Two did not, and both were routed on an
> assumption the sitting overturned** — that shopping would be 5b's generator. *Shopping occasions per
> Household per Day* and both **Provider List** rows were pointing at a milestone that will now not
> build shopping, so they went from *owned by 5b* to **owned by nothing**. **A row's owner is a claim
> that can go stale silently**, because a re-routing sweep corrects the milestone's *name* and cannot
> see that the milestone's *content* changed underneath it — which is why the sweep is recorded here
> rather than only performed.

| Question | Owner | The number that settles it |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **Why does a city with 100% car ownership put five Vehicles on the road?** — the number that decides whether the commute generator is working at the scale the corpus assumes | **A Vehicles-in-motion census across a full Day on `rulesets/bordered.toml` and `rulesets/congested.toml` at matched Citizen counts**, which is what would separate the two candidate causes. **No milestone owns it and it is owed one** | **NEW 2026-08-23, from milestone 24 task 3's land value work**, and found while measuring something else. Both files state `[households] car_ownership_percent = 100`, so on both *a Citizen drives everywhere* ([`adr/0098`](../docs/adr/0098-a-citizen-travels-in-their-households-mode-and-mode-choice-is-undesigned-rather-than-unbuilt.md)). times the Citizens for 190 times the traffic is not a ratio**, and the shape of the Day on `congested.toml` says the generator itself is sound, so the difference is in the world rather than the pass computed zero everywhere, and *why the field was zero* and *why the pass was slow* are two findings that happened to share a run ([`adr/0073`](../docs/adr/0073-a-local-workaround-is-not-a-discharge-and-a-finding-about-shared-code-must-reach-it.md)). **What rests on it**: every figure taken on `bordered.toml` that a moving | **Is an allocation assertion's error bounded by one gen0 allocation context?** — the number that decides whether `GC.GetAllocatedBytesForCurrentThread` can carry an **exact** equality in a suite that runs in parallel | **A run of the ~~four~~ eight exact-equality allocation assertions with `GC.CollectionCount(0/1/2)` recorded either side of the measured window**, over enough full unfiltered runs to catch the intermittent — no milestone owns it, and it is owed one | **NEW 2026-08-20, from milestone 10's gate**, and it is the refuting number [`0030`](0030-save-load.md) said existed and nobody had taken. **Four discrepancies are on record and every one is under 8,192 bytes** — 5,672 and 5,696 (`ZoneRuleTriggerTests.Sweeping_allocates_nothing_after_the_first_trigger`, 2026-08-18), 6,768 (`QuantityTests.Arithmetic_on_quantities_allocates_nothing`, same day, over arithmetic that cannot allocate at all) and **7,896** (the same `ZoneRuleTrigger` test, 2026-08-19). **8,192 is the gen0 allocation context**, the per-thread buffer whose used portion that counter adds in, so the hypothesis is that a collection forced by *another* thread retires this thread's context and the counter jumps by **at most one context**. ⚠ **Four samples is a pattern and not a bound**, and this is [`adr/0043`](../docs/adr/0043-a-claim-a-measurement-could-settle-must-not-be-settled-by-argument.md) exactly: the shape of the answer is obvious and the measurement has never been run, so no document may state the bound. ⚠ **What rests on it is a *choice*, not a fix**: if the error is bounded by 8 KB per collection then the assertions can stay exact when no collection occurred and be honest when one did, which is **stronger** than a flat tolerance; if it is not bounded, the ~~four~~ **eight** assertions do not belong in a parallel suite at all and the answer is a lane rather than an edit ([`adr/0121`](../docs/adr/0121-the-commit-gate-is-the-assertion-tier-and-a-long-test-runs-post-submit-on-a-machine-that-is-not-yours.md)). ⚠ **It is intermittent, which is what makes it expensive**: milestone 10's gate ran three times over the same tree and came back **red, green, green** ⚠ **SIX FULL UNFILTERED RUNS ON A SECOND MACHINE, 2026-08-20 — 48 readings, ZERO firings, and the question is still OPEN.** Apple M4 Pro, **10 performance + 4 efficiency = 14 logical cores**, 24 GiB, macOS 26.5.2, .NET 10.0.102, Release, workstation GC, on mains at High Power under `caffeinate`. ⚠ **Not the reference machine**, and the core count is part of the reading rather than context: xUnit sizes its thread pool off `Environment.ProcessorCount`, which counts P and E cores alike, so this is a 14-way suite against the reference machine's 6-way. Each run 18m56s–19m09s over 1,779 tests. **48 readings = the eight assertion sites × six runs, and not one of them moved the counter.** **Three windows contained collections and every one read exactly zero bytes** — `1 gen0 / 1 gen1 / 1 gen2`, `13 gen0`, and `6 gen0 / 1 gen1`, all three the same `ZoneRuleTriggerTests.Sweeping_allocates_nothing_after_the_first_trigger`, which is also the test behind three of the four recorded discrepancies. **So the sufficiency half is refuted much harder than it was**: thirteen gen0 collections inside a window, and a **gen2** inside another, and the counter did not move by one byte. ***A collection inside the window is not sufficient, and it is not nearly sufficient.*** ⚠⚠ **But the half that was owed is still untested, and this run could not test it.** *A jump requires a collection* is a claim about jumps, **nothing jumped**, so 48 more zeroes say nothing about it whatsoever. ⚠⚠⚠ **And six green runs are not evidence the event is absent here** — this is the protocol's own ***one green run cannot tell "the cause was removed" from "the intermittent did not fire"*** arriving at n=6 and still biting. At a per-run firing rate `p` the chance of six clean runs is `(1−p)⁶`, so six runs exclude only `p` above **~39%** at 95% — and the reference machine's own observed rate is about **one run in three**, which this sample does not exclude at all. ***A rare event's absence is a statement about the sample size before it is a statement about the machine.*** Catching one at `p ≈ 0.25` with 95% confidence needs about **eleven** runs, and at `p ≈ 0.15` about **nineteen**. **Neither branch is supported and neither may be taken**, which is [`adr/0043`](../docs/adr/0043-a-claim-a-measurement-could-settle-must-not-be-settled-by-argument.md) holding rather than being discharged. ⚠ **The bound could not have come from this machine anyway**: allocation-context size is a GC-configuration property, both machines run workstation GC but this is a different architecture and core count, so a bound taken here is [`adr/0106`](../docs/adr/0106-a-wall-clock-budget-names-a-machine-class-and-a-thread-count-or-it-is-not-a-budget.md) one level down. **The correlation travels between machines and the 8,192 does not.** Readings in `alloc-probe.csv`, gitignored. 🔴 ⚠⚠ **THE OWED HALF IS ANSWERED, 2026-08-21, AND THE ANSWER WAS ALREADY IN THE FILE.** This row says *a jump requires a collection* is untested because *nothing jumped*. **Two jumps are in the probe's readings — 697 rows over 102 processes, archived as `tests/Borough.Tests/alloc-probe-archive/2026-08-21-cumulative-105-processes.csv` — and they fall on opposite sides of the claim**: `ZoneRuleTriggerTests.Sweeping_allocates_nothing_after_the_first_trigger` at **5,208 bytes with `0 gen0 / 0 gen1 / 0 gen2`**, and `LayerQueryTests.Answering_the_query_allocates_nothing` at **3,376 bytes with `1 gen0`** (2026-08-21, milestone 11 task 9's tier run). ***A jump does NOT require a collection*** — `GC.CollectionCount(0)` is process-wide and non-concurrent, so a zero delta means no gen0 collection completed anywhere during that window, and the counter moved 5,208 bytes anyway. **Both halves of the sufficiency/necessity pair are now refuted**: a collection is not sufficient (48 readings, three with collections, all zero) and it is not necessary (5,208 with none). ✅ **What SURVIVES is the bound, and it is the half that matters**: six discrepancies on record — 3,376, 5,208, 5,672, 5,696, 6,768, 7,896 — and **every one is under 8,192**. ⚠ **Six samples is still a pattern and not a bound** and `adr/0043` still holds, but the branch this row names as *stronger* — exact when no collection occurred — is now **refused by evidence** rather than merely unsupported: a jump occurred with no collection, so *no collection* cannot be the licence for exactness. ***The choice this row was preserving has been eliminated by the instrument it built.*** 🔴 ⚠ **AND THE META-FINDING IS THE SHARPER ONE. The 5,208 sample sat in the file unread.** It is row **300** of that archived file, so it predates 2026-08-21, and this row went on saying the necessity half was untested while the machine built to test it had already recorded the answer. ***An instrument that writes to a file nobody reads is an instrument that has not run***, and the failure is not in the probe — which did exactly what it was built to do — but in there being no step, anywhere, that reads its output. ⚠ **And the missing thing is NOT a reader, which was this entry's first guess and is wrong.** `AllocationProbe.Record` runs *before* the assertion and writes a non-zero row through immediately, so **every non-zero row in that file is a test that went red in that process**. The 5,208 was a red suite somebody re-ran, got green, and moved on from — which is the correct response to an intermittent and is exactly how the evidence was lost. ***What was missing is a pointer, not a mechanism***: nothing in the failure told the reader that the run had just written the sample the open question was waiting for. ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-21**: the eight assertions now go through `AllocationProbe.Check`, which records and asserts in one call and whose failure message names the file, the delta and the collection counts. ***A failure message that names where the evidence went is `adr/0093`'s *name a symbol* arriving at the one moment somebody is definitely reading***. 🔴 ⚠ **AND THIS ROW IS NOT THE ONLY HOME OF THE QUESTION**, which is the sharper half: [`plans/0003`](0003-build-plan.md) **queue item 13** files the same firings as a *defect* and carries a **different mechanism** — *a tiered-JIT rejit allocating on the measuring thread* — and until 2026-08-21 neither row cited the other. ***A fact with two homes is `plans/0012` Cause 1 whether or not the two agree***, and here they did not: the zero-collection sample **refutes this row's mechanism as necessary and leaves item 13's standing.** Both rows now name each other; they are deliberately **not merged**, because this one owns a *measurable* and that one owns a *defect*. ⚠ 🔴 ⚠ **AND THE READINGS WERE NEARLY DESTROYED BY ROUTINE HOUSEKEEPING, 2026-08-21.** The probe wrote to `AppContext.BaseDirectory` — `bin/Release/net10.0/` — which `dotnet clean`, a worktree prune and a fresh clone all discard **by design**. A dead worktree was about to be removed carrying **48 readings from six full runs that existed nowhere else**, which is this row's own *48 readings, zero firings* sample. ***An instrument whose output lives in a build directory is an instrument whose evidence is deleted by housekeeping***, and these readings are the slowest thing in the project to reproduce — six unfiltered runs is about four hours. ✅ **Fixed**: the probe writes to `tests/Borough.Tests/alloc-probe-archive/`, outside `bin/`, and both rescued datasets are in it under names saying what they are. ⚠ **Whether the readings should be COMMITTED is a separate question and is left open** — committing them would make this row's figures **checkable** rather than quotable, which is `plans/0012` **Cause 5**'s repair applied to a dataset instead of a sentence, and it is the first time that repair has been available. ⚠ **The next machine is item 13's and not this row's**: a run with tiered compilation disabled, which would separate the two mechanisms and which nobody has taken ⚠ **THE SET THIS ROW NAMES IS *EIGHT* AND NOT FOUR, STRUCK AND CORRECTED 2026-08-20** — and it was wrong before the machine ran rather than because of it, which is why it is corrected here rather than carried as a finding of the measurement. There are **eight** exact-equality allocation assertions, in **six files**, and they split **four and four by spelling**: `ZoneSampleTests`, `ZoneRuleTriggerTests`, `LayerQueryTests` and `BuildingResidencyTests` write `Assert.Equal(before, after)`, while `QuantityTests` and `TableAllocationTests`' three write `Assert.Equal(0, after - before)` — the same property, asserted the other way round, and **only the first spelling was ever counted**. ***A count taken by grepping one spelling of a property is a count of the spelling.*** ⚠ **It is not a tidying correction, because the undercount excluded a test already observed firing**: `QuantityTests.Arithmetic_on_quantities_allocates_nothing` is one of the four discrepancies this row opens with — **6,768 bytes, 2026-08-18** — so the row named a machine that would have left out one of its own four samples. The instrument built on 2026-08-20 records **all eight** and always did, which is why its 48 readings are *eight sites × six runs* while the prose above them still said four. ⚠ **A SEVENTH SAMPLE, 2026-08-22, taken while settling [`0003`](0003-build-plan.md) queue item 14: `ZoneSampleTests.Sampling_allocates_nothing`, **7,784 bytes**, `0 gen0 / 0 gen1 / 0 gen2`, thread 24, process 1668636.** Recorded **before** the re-run, which is what `AllocationProbe.Check`'s failure message exists to make happen, and the re-run was green. The set is now **3,376, 5,208, 5,672, 5,696, 6,768, 7,784, 7,896** — ***seven, and every one still under 8,192***. ⚠ **It adds nothing to either refuted half**: no collection, so it joins the 5,208 as a jump that needed none. **Seven samples is still a pattern and not a bound**, and this is the third distinct test site to fire, which is the only new thing in it. |

S0a priced the list **inline** — 104 bytes, 47% of the Household row, ~21% of the whole world — which prices *declared capacity*, because a column is a flat array over every slot and an unset entry costs what a set one does. Under an index list the cost follows **what Households actually know**, and the structure is only smaller if steady-state occupancy is below the cap: inline is `N × entry`, a list is `head + actual × (entry + next)`, so **at full occupancy the list loses**. The refuting number is average occupancy against the cap. **Two conclusions are suspended until it exists** — the 21%, and the Household-versus-Citizen table ordering. `adr/0066` settles the *structure* by argument and routes the *footprint* here on purpose |
| **⚠ What does a walk search cost, and where is its row?** **HALF-CLOSED 2026-08-11: the unit is measured, the row exists, and the question that replaced it is bigger.** `WalkSearchBenchmarks` on the shipped 32-Tile lattice gives **86.2 ns at 128 m → 38.4 µs at 4.10 km**, a **446× span**, because the search settles a *disc* — 2, 3, 10, 40, 149, 591 nodes at 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32 blocks, ~4× a doubling — at a flat **35–40 ns a settled node**. **So a walk search has no unit cost; it has `≈ 37 ns × distance²`**, and the 4–20 µs the ledger carried is that curve at 1.5–2.5 km. ***The multiplicand everybody named was the route count, and the lever is the distance distribution*** — halving mean walk length **quarters** the bill — **and no document in the corpus states one.** That is now the open half, it is **§A's to settle** (a Trip generator is what produces a distance distribution), and it is the second time in two slices that this subsystem's named parameter turned out not to be the one that moves the outcome. Also measured: the **severed** case is **14.9 ns flat** — the *cheapest* query in the model, ~2,600× under a 4 km walk — so the failure 5b exists to report costs nothing to report; **across the street** is 16.8 ns; and every path allocates **zero**. ⚠ Run **in-process** because a git worktree inside the repo gives BenchmarkDotNet two `Borough.Tests.csproj`; an out-of-process re-run is owed. Their product is **1.9–9.3 ms a Tick against a 15.6 ms budget at 4× — 12% to 60%** — and 4.8–46 ms at peak. **`adr/0075` makes every Leg eager**, so the whole bill lands at Trip creation rather than being spread over the journey, which is the half that makes a peak a peak. **This is not a small row**: `0013` currently sums to ≥114% at 4× *without* it. Two properties make it more urgent than the arithmetic suggests — the unit is a **guess inherited from a synthetic harness** (`0013`'s own organising column, and the corpus's standing lesson that *a unit cost is a hypothesis until a real world has produced one*), and the multiplicand comes from an O-D family `0013` line 347 already flags as **invented** until Trip generation exists. **Both halves of the row are unmeasured and the code to measure them now exists** |

The comparison the row asks for still cannot be made — but it now has one side, and **the side that exists is the one that cannot move much**, so what the Cap turns on is entirely the row below. **Do not read the supply figure as the Cap**: this corpus has a recorded habit of a number becoming a decision by being the only number in the room, and this row is now exactly that situation ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-19 — this row states the supply side in the retired **unit** and against the retired **basis**, and it is the third copy of both.** The Cap counts **Vehicles, not Segments** ([`adr/0062`](../docs/adr/0062-the-microscopic-cap-counts-vehicles-and-nothing-is-ever-evicted.md)), which is why the parenthesised Vehicle figure above is the load-bearing one and the Segment figure is a conversion at 72 Vehicles each. And it is priced against the **design speed's budget**, never against the top rung ([`adr/0096`](../docs/adr/0096-the-microscopic-cap-derives-from-the-design-speeds-budget-and-not-from-the-top-rungs.md)) — pricing at the top rung buys a stutter-free fast-forward by making every player's traffic permanently less accurate, which is `03 §3.9`'s own table read backwards. ⚠ **[`adr/0105`](../docs/adr/0105-the-target-speed-is-4x-at-a-million-and-a-rung-dilates-rather-than-being-withdrawn.md) has since fired `adr/0096`'s revisit trigger and the basis survived on one leg of two** — a rung is never withdrawn, so *the rung the binding case will not be running* is false, and the Cap stays where it is because ***a Cap and a bill are different objects***: dilation absorbs a bill, and nothing absorbs a permanently coarser city. ⚠ **And the supply figure is one core.** S5's L6 capture settles **2 threads at 1.84–1.93×** and leaves 4 bimodal on a contended machine, so the multiple is *at least 1.84× and plausibly near 4×* and must never be quoted bare — a bare *near 4×* would be [`0012`](0012-corpus-audit.md) **Cause 5** committed on this row's own owed measurement. ***Two corrections reached the ADRs and neither reached this row***, which is Cause 2 landing in the section that is supposed to be the register of what is still open. |
| **Does `03 §3.4`'s loop still close?** — and the two orphan numbers that rode with the answer | R8.5's instrument on a **variant-supplied** route set, at `06` **5b-bis**, with **both** responders live | **NEW, from session D task 5**, and it is why `adr/0046`'s static-Habit ratification was **withdrawn** rather than narrowed. R8 forbade carrying its fire rate to *"any scheme that gives a Traveller more than one candidate route"*, and [`adr/0060`](../docs/adr/0060-a-habit-route-is-a-small-set-of-variants-and-which-one-you-take-is-who-you-are.md) is that scheme. **The re-run must carry two things R8.5 did not have.** Variants disperse traffic *structurally*, so Sight's headroom to improve on a concentrated baseline is smaller — the loop closing is the question, never the effect size. And there are now **two responders on one signal**: Sight per crossing, Aggravation per `N` journeys, which is the shape that oscillates. **Two orphan numbers are entered here at last** — R8's **14.08% diversion fire rate**, whose consumer changed when [`adr/0061`](../docs/adr/0061-a-diversion-rejoins-by-local-descent-and-a-rejoin-is-never-a-search.md) removed the search behind a diversion, and Temperament's **92.28% damping**, measured against one herd when there are now two to damp. Both were measured on the deleted structure and **were rows nowhere**. **⚠ S2 R7 wrote the direction for both on 2026-08-11, and they do not share a sign — which is the reason this row must not be discharged as one item.** The **14.08%** is an **upper bound**: on the tree, the congestion Sight fires at was manufactured by route degeneracy rather than by demand — 90.87% of the carriageway *empty* at 13% of holding capacity — so dispersal unloads exactly the arcs that were firing and a variant-supplied set fires at most as often. The **92.28%** goes the other way and is the one figure of the three that dispersal makes **unquotable**: damping is a ratio *against a herd*, the tree manufactured the largest herd this model can produce (every Citizen on a pair holding the **identical** route), and `adr/0060` shrinks that herd **by construction** — so the number is defensible read as *what damping can do at most* and unsupported read as *what damping will do*. **A mechanism's efficacy measured against a maximal disturbance is not evidence about a small one**, so this half needs the re-run above and not a sentence |

Each has a pass/fail written down: **spillback** with force-promotion **disabled** — does the upstream Segment block; **hysteresis** with lossy demotion enabled — does the jam persist after the volume that caused it is gone; the **whole suite** for the virtual queue, which is `§5.1`'s admission rule turned onto a *representation* rather than a scenario; and **queueing** for divergence against Ticks since the threshold crossing. **The last one is a claim this session made and withdrew** — `adr/0007`'s VDF argument is about *saturation*, and *age* is a different axis supported by nothing. **`03 §3.3`'s force-promotion decision was filed as arguable and that typing is wrong**: the number exists and the machine is named, so a session may not close it — the second thing task 0's method caught after the pass had finished |

The refuting number is the count under a real draw at 1M; if it exceeds what S5 says fits, `adr/0007`'s scaling clause is what fails, not the Cap's value |
| ~~**What the Lane model costs**~~ **ANSWERED 2026-08-11 by S5, and published the same day** → [`spike-results`](../docs/spike-results.md) → *S5*, [`0019`](0019-s5-lane-kernel.md). **~325,000–330,000 Vehicles a Tick a core** with Overlaps, ~379,000–381,000 without, and **~4,510–4,580 Microscopic Segments in 15.6 ms** at 72 Vehicles each. **Two of five tripwires fired and both reach sentences rather than designs**: `adr/0016`'s transplanted 400,000 is **not reproduced** (T1) and its *"constant of a `memcpy`"* is **26–29× a bare walk** (T3). **The structure survives entirely** — flat in `O(n)`, no spatial index, zero allocation — and **the gap is `adr/0003`'s arithmetic**: three integer divisions a Vehicle a Tick, two of them removable for 1.63–1.75×. `0013` has the row it never had. **⚠ L5 then withdrew T1 the same day**: `IntegerMath.FloorDiv` was evaluating a modulo on every call, so **every `Fixed.Div` in the project was two 64-bit divisions**, and correcting it — **bit-identical**, 1,060 tests green, no baseline moved — takes the Lane model to **~533,000–570,000** and *above* `adr/0016`'s 400,000. **So the gap was never `adr/0003`'s arithmetic**, the §D2 row this answer opened is **retired the day it was written**, and what is left owed is the `performance` capture and **what the correction is worth at every other division site in the simulation**, which S5 cannot speak for | ~~**S5**~~ **run; owes the `performance` re-capture** | **NEW, from session D task 0, and the pass's largest finding.** `adr/0016`'s affordability rests on one external number — Eickhoff's *"roughly 400,000 individually simulated cars on a single core"*, *"the number that makes microscopic traffic affordable at all"* — taken off a **float-based Rust engine**, where `adr/0003` forbids us floats entirely. **[`0013`](0013-tick-budget.md) has no row for the Microscopic tier at all**, not even *unbuilt*, so the half of the movement subsystem that is not routing is priced nowhere while the half that is carries 60–67 of the ledger's ≥114 points. S5 also prices the two claims that ride with it: *O(n) with the constant of a `memcpy`* and **promotion cost** — materialising Lane queues from in-flight Trips, which `adr/0016`'s own revisit trigger names as a condition and gives no machine |

Same shape as every other unit cost the corpus has had to re-measure: *a unit cost is a hypothesis until a real world has produced one.* Note `adr/0005`'s neighbouring figure has already gone this way — *"a Citizen record is roughly 40 bytes"* against **S0a's 85.98 MiB at 1M**, ≈90 B/Citizen across the tables, which `03 §2.1` admits is stale and the ADR still states |

The refuting number is **peak pedestrian density per block face at 1M against the density at which walking speed falls**. The corpus already names the one context where it fails — a transit platform, `03 §3.7`'s recorded revisit trigger — which is an argument that the general claim is a *measurement* rather than a truism |
| **Trip object count "roughly triples"** — mean Legs per Trip | Trip generation, `06` **5b-bis** | **NEW, from session D task 0.** `adr/0008` states it as a sizing consequence and the Trip table is to be sized on it. Nothing generates Trips, so it has never been counted; it is an input to table sizing and to [`0013`](0013-tick-budget.md) both, and the corpus's own record on guessed multiplicands is in the *Routing* row of that ledger |
| **Does parking scarcity degrade as a gradient?** — and what a shed query costs per arrival | Trip generation, `06` **5b-bis**, plus parking | **NEW, from session D task 0.** `adr/0009` chose graceful degradation *"over a hard failure … because it converts a cliff into a gradient"*, and the refuting number is the **walk-Leg length distribution as shed occupancy approaches 1**: a jump is a cliff, whatever the mechanism intended. R5.6 measured the shed's **invalidation** (per-Segment witnessed by paths, 26.10% of a Tick against a global rung's 1,638.20%) and never the **query**, which `adr/0009` pays *on arrival* and names as its own first revisit trigger ✅ **BOTH HALVES ANSWERED 2026-08-19, milestone 7 task 8, and they are answered by two different machines rather than the one this row assumed.** **The gradient holds.** `ParkingScarcityTests` on [`rulesets/scarce.toml`](../rulesets/scarce.toml) cuts `[[building]] parking` 8 → 1 over four whole Days at 4,000 Citizens and prices the walk at every Building door: the walk-Leg length distribution moves **monotonically and with no jump**, p50 **0.0 → 0.9** clock minutes, p90 **0.6 → 1.9**, max **0.9 → 3.1**, against **4.8** clock minutes to walk across the whole shed at 400 m. ***A jump is a cliff whatever the mechanism intended***, and there is no jump — so `adr/0009`'s central claim survives the one reading that could have refuted it. ⚠ **The occupancy column does not do what this row expected and the finding is worth more than the answer**: it saturates at **83.0%** and will not climb, because spaces *held* peak at `parking = 5` and **fall** as capacity shrinks further — a Trip that cannot park is refused, and a refused Trip is a car that needs no space. ***A shed cannot be filled by shrinking it, because the refusal that scarcity causes removes the demand that would have filled it.*** **The query cost is measured separately and is in [`0013`](0013-tick-budget.md)**, not here — a quiet re-take on the reference machine, and it replaced a PROVISIONAL figure that was about **five times** too high. Full record in [`0031`](0031-parking.md) task 8. |
| ~~**What fraction of Trips fail on Commute Budget with a shed that was stale about an addition?**~~ **VOID 2026-08-19, and it was void the day it was written** | Parking, `06` **milestone 7** | **OPENED AND CLOSED IN ONE ENTRY, milestone 7 task 8.** It is [`adr/0083`](../docs/adr/0083-a-sheds-use-is-the-arrival-query-and-a-stale-shed-is-wrong-by-a-bounded-walk.md)'s own **first revisit trigger**, [`0031`](0031-parking.md) task 8 carried it as a fourth reading *available for free*, and it had never been a row here — so it is filed and struck in the same act, because ***a trigger nobody wrote down is a trigger nobody will fire***. ⚠ **The reading cannot be taken, and not because it is hard.** `World.TryChooseParking` calls `ParkingShed.Nearest` **fresh on every arrival**; no shed is stored anywhere in the `World`, no shed outlives the query that built it, and a structure that does not persist cannot go stale. The trigger describes a **materialised** shed, which `adr/0083` deliberately did not build and which `0031` explicitly forbids this milestone from building. ***A revisit trigger written against a design is not a revisit trigger against the build that design chose*** — [`adr/0093`](../docs/adr/0093-a-description-of-the-build-is-where-to-look-and-never-what-you-found.md) arriving at a **ratifier** rather than at a doc-comment. **It becomes live the day a shed is materialised**, which is also the day `[parking] shed_keeps` stops being hash-neutral — so the two reopen together and D1's cap row names the same event. |

The measurement is owed, and `05 §5` still leans on the other answer |
| **Does deriving a waiter's requirement cost less than the Bin write it accompanies?** | Slice 7's evaluation counters, plus an **S0b-class capture** with a Ruleset in force | **NEW, from [`adr/0063`](../docs/adr/0063-a-wait-list-wakes-on-the-bins-state-and-a-shortfall-is-derived-rather-than-stored.md), and the ADR declines to declare it affordable.** The drain used to read one field per waiter examined; it now calls `RuleEngine.Requirement`, which is a partial `Check` — the band plus the term walk for the one blocking Bin. The drain stops at the first uncovered waiter, so the common case is **one derivation per Bin write**, on the hot path of every `Deposit` and `Withdraw`, in the subsystem that is already the largest priced row in [`0013`](0013-tick-budget.md). Against **82.84 ns** an evaluation synthetic and **552 ns** in a real 1M world, the refuting number is the per-write cost with the derivation against without. **If it is material the honest fix is the stored column returning with an explicit invalidation on reload** — this decision reversed on a number, which is the correct way to reverse it |
| **Whether Bin Rule evaluation fits the Tick budget at 1M** | **S0b** | Slice 7 task 9 measured the unit and published the wire inverted: **82.84 ns an evaluation**, flat to 1.8% across two orders of magnitude, so **15.6 ms holds ~188,000 per Tick**. What it cannot settle is the multiplicand. Against `0002`'s own **450 Rule Instances per 1,000 Citizens** and `02 §4.3`'s rate of 8, a 1M city spends **~60% of a Tick on evaluation alone**. ~~**Since superseded by a whole-Tick measurement — 67% at 4×**~~ **and superseded again, this time by a measurement taken in a city rather than in a fixture** ([`0011`](0011-rule-engine-bins-and-rules.md) finding 42). Task 10a put a Ruleset in a 1M world for the first time: **~6.4 ms a Tick, 41% at 4×, at a measured 11,586 due**. The synthetic whole-Tick unit was **2.8× too low** and the guessed multiplicand ~5× too high, so the 67% was **right by cancellation** — the worst way for a number to be right. The wire is now *fewer than ~28,000 due Rules per Tick at 4×*, which against the 450 needs a **mean rate above ~15.9 Ticks** where the old wire needed 4.8, and `02 §4.3`'s bakery runs at 8. **What is still owed is unchanged and is now the only thing owed: the multiplicand.** 11,586 is measured for a Ruleset that models no city, so it settles nothing about a real one — that is **10b**'s, and S0b's for the rest of the Tick |

---

## C — Arguable: a session can close it

Grouped by the document that would carry the answer, because that is the sitting it belongs to.
An entry here is not scheduled; [`0000`](0000-board.md) decides when a session runs, and the standing
rule is **when something concrete is blocked on it, not because it is available.**

### `01-player-experience.md`

- 🔴 **Is Terraform a seventh verb?** **OPEN, *arguable*, filed 2026-08-22 by `plans/0038` decision 2**
  ([`adr/0142`](../docs/adr/0142-height-does-not-ship-until-terraforming-does-because-terrain-without-a-price-is-a-wall.md)).
  [`adr/0021`](../docs/adr/0021-the-map-is-bounded-procedural-and-terrain-never-enters-a-tick.md) states
  *"Terraforming is a player verb, priced by cut-and-fill haul distance"*, and **`01 §2` has six** — Zone,
  Connect, Service, Govern, Demolish, Inspect — under the sentence *"the list is short on purpose"*.
  ⚠ **`01` does not mention terraforming anywhere at all**, so this is not a verb the section considered
  and rejected; it is one the section has never met. ***An ADR may assert a verb, and the document that
  owns the verb list is the only thing that can add one.***
  ⚠ **The corpus has counted this list once already and got a correction from it**:
  [`adr/0022`](../docs/adr/0022-land-is-a-stock-the-city-spends.md)'s 2026-08-12 amendment asked whether
  clearing forest needed one and recorded that
  [`adr/0091`](../docs/adr/0091-clearing-land-is-bought-rather-than-taken-and-demolish-is-the-sixth-verb.md)
  had made Demolish the sixth — so the count is maintained and a seventh is a change to it rather than an
  addition to a table. **It blocks nothing today and it gates the height field**: milestone 24 stores no
  height precisely because terraforming is what turns terrain from a wall into a price, and neither the
  verb nor the milestone exists. **Whoever answers it also answers whether `06`'s newly split terraforming
  row can be placed.**

### `02-simulation-model.md`

- 🔴 **Does the land value target pass stagger?** **OPEN, *arguable*, filed 2026-08-22 by milestone 24
  task 3.** `MapLayers.SetLandValueTargets` walks **every live Cell row** on one Tick in 256, and
  [`plans/0013`](0013-tick-budget.md) now measures that Tick at **6,578 ms on `rulesets/bordered.toml`**
  — **421× the 15.6 ms budget** — after a 13.4× fix to the query underneath it. ⚠ **No further work on
  the query closes it**: the pass is 1,048,576 noise queries and the budget allows about **15 ns each**.
  ***The remaining defect is the pass and not the query.*** `02 §10`'s own rule is *`O(1)` at the write
  site per Tick, `O(n)` **staggered**, whole-world at end of run*, and a whole-world sweep is the one
  shape it names as wrong; staggering to `1/256` of the Cells a Tick gives **~4,096 queries ≈ 25 ms**.
  🔴 **It is a design change and not an optimisation** — *when* a Cell retargets moves the State Hash,
  and [`adr/0044`](../docs/adr/0044-the-map-layer-diffusion-cadence-is-the-designers-number-not-the-profilers.md)
  makes the cadence **the designer's number rather than the profiler's**, which is exactly the number a
  stagger changes. ⚠ **The alternative is not "leave it"**: the pass was free only while the Cell table
  was empty, which was true of eight shipped Rulesets and is true of none once Sealing writes.
  **Blocks: milestone 24 task 3 landing without making the assertion tier unusable.**

  ✅ ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-23 — IT NO LONGER BLOCKS, AND THE ENTRY ABOVE REASONED FROM A DIVISION THAT
  DID NOT HOLD.** *No further work on the query closes it* was concluded from 1,048,576 queries against
  a 15 ns budget, and it assumed each query had to do the work it was doing. **It did not.**
  `LineSourceQueries.Level`'s first pass finds the nearest Street by resolving two node handles and
  projecting a point for every Segment in the window, and only afterwards asks whether any of them
  carries a Vehicle — ***so the expensive half of a traffic query is the half that never looks at
  traffic.*** Where nothing within range carries Vehicles the whole query is **provably** zero, and
  `TrafficPresence` lets it skip pass one: **7,353 ms → 80 ms on `rulesets/bordered.toml`, 85×, and
  hash-preserving** ([`0013`](0013-tick-budget.md)).
  **So the question stands and its consequence has gone.** A whole-world sweep is still the shape
  `02 §10` names as wrong and *when* a Cell retargets is still the designer's number, but 80 ms on one
  Tick in 256 does not make the assertion tier unusable — **the stagger can now be decided on its
  merits rather than under a performance gun.**
  🔴 ⚠ **And the 85× is measured on a world with five Vehicles in it**, which is [`§B`](#b--measurable-routed-to-a-machine)'s
  open question — `congested.toml` peaks at 937. ***What the pass costs in a city with traffic is
  UNMEASURED***, and it is the traffic that decides it, so this entry must not be closed by quoting the
  80 ms at a busy world.
  ⚠ **A third thing was found and is not this question's to settle**:
  [`adr/0044`](../docs/adr/0044-the-map-layer-diffusion-cadence-is-the-designers-number-not-the-profilers.md)
  **contradicts itself** about who owns a stagger — Consequences bullet 1 leaves *the stagger's phase*
  to a profiler, bullet 3 calls the offsets hash-bearing and the designer's. A per-Cell stagger moves
  **only phase and never a period**, so the two bullets sort this exact change opposite ways. Whoever
  answers the question above amends the ADR first, or answers it from whichever half they happened to
  read.


- ⚠ **Should land value swing with rush hour?** **OPEN, *arguable*, filed 2026-08-20 by milestone 9
  task 5** ([`adr/0127`](../docs/adr/0127-the-land-value-target-never-stops-moving-so-the-question-is-what-the-lag-rests-around.md),
  [`0034`](0034-the-land-value-target-and-the-composed-layers.md)). **The measurable half is done and
  this is what is left of it.** Desirability's noise term reads a Segment's volume **at the instant it
  is asked**, and land value is the only part of the composition with memory — so the target moves on a
  **daily orbit**: zero noise at midnight, a peak at eight, and 185 of 262 Cells moving on a typical
  cadence sample. Measured, bounded and trend-free; the question is whether it is the right **field**.
  ***A land value that rises overnight and falls at eight is a traffic meter with a lag on it***, and
  the other reading — that land value should reflect *typical* conditions, so the noise term wants a
  time-average rather than an instant — has no number that refutes it, which is why it is here rather
  than in a spike. ⚠ **Not urgent, and the reason is `adr/0125`'s**: nothing reads land value, so the
  first consumer is the first thing that can be wrong about it. **Trigger: milestone 13, the price
  surface**, the same trigger the weights' real ratifier carries. ⚠ **Do not close this by tuning
  `land_value_tau`** — damping a daily cycle with a lag constant hides a modelling choice behind a
  designer's number, and the modelling choice stays.

- ~~**What "full" means for a Bin with no capacity.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-10 by
  [`adr/0065`](../docs/adr/0065-a-bin-holds-a-long-and-unbounded-names-a-ceiling-whose-approach-is-a-defect-rather-than-a-refusal.md)**
  (session **N**, [`0018`](0018-session-n-the-bin-the-pool-and-the-economy.md) task 4). **A Bin's level
  and capacity are `long`; *unbounded* is `long.MaxValue`; *full* stays uniform with no special case;
  and an approach to any ceiling is `adr/0006`'s magnitude clause rather than a headroom refusal.**
  Two things the entry as filed had wrong.
  **It was filed as a semantic question and the answer was a width.** *Is full a state an unbounded Bin
  can be in* is unanswerable while the ceiling is `int.MaxValue`, because the honest answer depends on
  whether the ceiling is reachable — and at 360,000 Households a city-aggregating money Bin overflows a
  signed 32-bit level at **5,965 units per Household**. The corpus had **already chosen `long`**:
  `Money` is a `long` and `HouseholdTable` holds `Column<Money>`, so a payment into a Bin narrowed 64
  bits to 32 and nobody had noticed. The decision is the Bin agreeing with a type the project already
  had.
  **The determinism half `adr/0031` flagged was never in danger** and needed recording rather than
  deciding: the code has always written `capacity − level` through `HeadroomAt`, never
  `level + delta > capacity`, so nothing overflowed and nothing inverted.
  **It closed a live latent defect on the way**: `RuleEngine.Requirement`'s `floor × |net|` is an
  unbounded `int` product, and on overflow a negative requirement makes the drain wake a waiter it
  cannot satisfy and then *increase* its own budget. Unreachable only because the sole declared Readout
  is `occupancy`.
- ~~**Does a wait list wake on the arrival or on the state?**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-10 by
  [`adr/0063`](../docs/adr/0063-a-wait-list-wakes-on-the-bins-state-and-a-shortfall-is-derived-rather-than-stored.md)**
  (session **N1**, [`0018`](0018-session-n-the-bin-the-pool-and-the-economy.md) task 1), and
  implemented in the same pass. **The wake budget is the Bin's `level` — or its `headroom` — and the
  requirement is derived at the drain; `RuleInstance.shortfall` is deleted.** Three things the entry
  as filed had wrong are worth keeping, because each was a consequence of the framing rather than of
  the answer.
  **It was two bugs held as one question, and that concealed the answer.** The budget was one write's
  delta and the requirement was computed under a Ruleset that may since have been reloaded — two
  independent defects on two axes. Neither candidate this entry named was the fix: *re-derive the
  shortfall* corrects the requirement while still comparing it against a single arrival, and `3 > 1`
  holds however the 3 was obtained, so **the candidate the ledger favoured fixes nothing about the
  trickle**; *decrement as arrivals accumulate* fixes the budget by writing a saved, hashed column on
  every deposit for every waiter, where moving the budget to `level` fixes it with no writes at all.
  **`pool` was not the trigger, and the entry said it was.** The defect was live in the committed
  golden baseline the whole time: the golden session reloads into `rulesets/minimal-tuned.toml` at
  Tick 128, that file's one changed number is `restock`'s output amount 1 → 2, and a producer with a
  headroom deficit of 2 drawn down one unit at a time is never woken. Found by building `adr/0033`'s
  satisfiability invariant, which had been specified in three documents and implemented in none.
  **And the state was already illegal**, so this was never a choice between two behaviours — a
  consumer asleep beside a Bin holding what it needs is `adr/0033`'s *no Rule is asleep with all
  inputs satisfiable*.
  **Slice 8's second face is discharged with it** (`plans/0015` finding 3): nothing stores a
  requirement, so nothing can hold one against a Ruleset that has been reloaded, and `adr/0015`'s
  acceptance test reaches the shortage path without a whole-world wake pass on a keystroke.
  **One measurable is left behind and is routed to §B**: whether the derived requirement costs less
  than the Bin write it accompanies.
- ~~**Whether a Bin's capacity is a property of the Bin as built or of the Ruleset in force.**~~
  **CLOSED 2026-08-10 by
  [`adr/0064`](../docs/adr/0064-a-bins-capacity-is-a-property-of-the-ruleset-in-force-and-an-over-full-bin-drains-rather-than-clamps.md)**
  (session **N**, [`0018`](0018-session-n-the-bin-the-pool-and-the-economy.md) task 3). **Capacity is
  `derived AND rebuilt` from the Ruleset in force, keyed on `(kind, Resource)`; an over-full Bin is
  left to drain.** Three things the entry as filed had wrong, kept because each came from the framing
  rather than from the answer.
  **It named two positions and the answer was a third.** *As built* and *in force* were held as the
  whole space, and both assume capacity is **state**. It is a function, so the question was never
  *which value does the row hold* but *why is there a row at all* — the same move `adr/0063` made on
  `shortfall` four hours earlier, and the session could name no property distinguishing the two
  columns.
  **The second-order question's second horn does not exist.** This entry said leaving a Bin over its
  new ceiling *"breaks `Invariant.BinLevelIsWithinCapacity`"*. Id 14 lives at exactly two **write-site
  guards**, in `Deposit` and `Withdraw`; there is no standing whole-world check. An over-full Bin has
  negative headroom, refuses every deposit at the engine's own affordability test, accepts every
  withdrawal, and **drains back under its ceiling on its own** — so the choice was never between
  destroying Goods and violating an invariant, and the option that won was already implemented.
  **The argument that decided it is not about reload.** Filed here as a hot-reload defect, and a
  reload is design-time and rare. What settles it is the **patch**: a shipped Ruleset change under a
  live city would leave Buildings raised before and after it permanently different in their ceilings,
  identical in every declared respect, with the cause recoverable from no state the world holds.
  **Two obligations leave with it**, and they ride with the implementation in
  [`0003`](0003-build-plan.md)'s hash-moving queue rather than becoming ledger entries of their own: a
  loader refusal making `(kind, Resource)` a key — which closes a defect that is live today, since
  `Fit` builds one Bin where construction built two — and an end-of-run check that the derivation was
  actually run. (`plans/0015`, finding 4.)
- ~~**How many Households a Building holds.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-10 by
  [`adr/0068`](../docs/adr/0068-a-buildings-occupancy-is-declared-by-its-kind-and-an-over-capacity-building-evicts.md)
  and [`adr/0069`](../docs/adr/0069-placement-is-a-mechanism-of-its-own-and-construction-houses-nobody.md)**
  (session **N**, [`0018`](0018-session-n-the-bin-the-pool-and-the-economy.md) task 2). **Occupancy is
  declared per `[[kind]]` and is `derived AND rebuilt` from the Ruleset in force; an over-capacity
  Building evicts the overflow into the Pool by a draw under its own `purpose_tag`; and `02 §5.2` step 2,
  Household placement, is a mechanism of its own, so construction houses nobody.** **This entry was
  wrong in four places and every one of them was checkable**, which is why they are kept.
  **It was filed here *and* in §B, and the two were not disagreeing about the type.** It is three things:
  a **defect** (two populators disagreeing, owed to code), a **shape** (arguable, closed here) and a
  **value** (a number under `adr/0052`, which is §D — §B holds claims a measurement could *refute*, and
  *how many families fit in a flat* is a choice). Both filings named a **number**, and the subject was a
  **mechanism**.
  **`adr/0025` covers it, and this entry says it does not.** The disqualifier used here — *"density is a
  cap on what may be built … this is how many families fit in what was built"* — is refused by that
  ADR's own section heading, **Capacity, not quality**, whose first sentence is *"Density says how many
  Occupants a Lot may carry."*
  **"No Rule reads its length" has been false since slice 7 task 10a.** `Readout.Occupancy` is the only
  declared Readout and `rulesets/minimal.toml` bands `consume` on it.
  **And "it breaks nothing today" broke the committed golden baseline.** The wake defect task 1 shipped
  a fix for exists because *"a producer whose deficit is 2, drawn down by the **occupancy-1** Buildings a
  Zone Rule creates, is withdrawn from one unit at a time and never woken"* — at occupancy 3 it does not
  occur. **The sentence and the defect were in the same ledger.**
  **What actually settles the equilibrium is `World.Place` having exactly one caller**, at construction.
  The number settles nothing while the only door into a dwelling is its own construction.
- **Where private capital comes from.** A regenerating pool is legible but arbitrary; deriving it from
  profits and savings is causally honest and adds a loop that could deadlock a struggling city.
  Probably derived, with a floor.
- **Multiplicative rather than additive utility** in `§5.4`'s choice model. SILO multiplies, so zero on
  any component yields zero total — *no amount of cheapness compensates for zero reachable jobs*
  expressed structurally rather than as a penalty.
- **`§3` (Resources) is stale**, not open: `adr/0031` replaced the Goods taxonomy with Resource
  families and nobody rewrote it. A correction — [`0012`](0012-corpus-audit.md)'s, not this file's.
- ~~**What a Zone Rule's `sample` is denominated in — and therefore what `§5.7` means by pacing
  growth.**~~ **CLOSED the same day it was raised**, by
  [`adr/0059`](../docs/adr/0059-a-zone-rules-sample-is-a-revisit-period-so-the-ruleset-states-a-duration.md):
  a Zone Rule authors **`revisit_ticks`, a duration**, and the engine derives
  `sample = ceil(Lots × interval ÷ revisit_ticks)`. **It needed no ratifier** — the default is
  `TICKS_PER_DAY`, derived — so §D **lost a row rather than gaining one**, the first time that has
  happened. Implementation is [`0014`](0014-zone-rules-and-the-sweep-family.md) **task 11**, sequenced
  after slice 8. **Full body, including S0b's τ collapse and the developer-per-Zone-Rule argument, in
  the archive below** → *Closed entries*.

### `03-agent-architecture.md`

- ⚠ **Does anything bound the walk *to* the car, and is a journey that begins with an unbounded walk
  priced correctly? — NEW 2026-08-19, from milestone 7 task 7's first run.** ***Arguable.*** The walk
  **from** the car is bounded by construction: `World.TryChooseParking` picks from a ball of
  `[parking] radius_metres` around the **destination's** door, so it cannot exceed a walk across the
  shed — **4.8 minutes** at 400 m. The walk **to** the car is bounded by nothing:
  `TripEngine.Itinerary` sets `waypoints[1]` from `World.HeldParkingAddress`, which is where the
  **last** journey left the car, and relates it to `waypoints[0]` not at all. `--parking` on
  `rulesets/congested.toml` at 4,000 Citizens over 4,096 Ticks reports **0 of 3,330** arrival walks
  past the ceiling and **57 of 3,330** departure walks past it, the longest **16.6 minutes**.
  ***A walk more than three times the shed's own diameter is not a shed walk.***
  - **The obvious explanation is refuted and that is why this is a row rather than a fix.** A failed
    morning journey would strand a Citizen away from their car — and `--evidence` on the same city
    reports **2,364 completed and zero failures of any kind**. The tail exists in a city where every
    Trip succeeds, so whatever produces it is upstream of any failure and has not been named.
  - **Two questions, and only the second is arguable.** *What produces it* is a fact somebody can go
    and look at, and is work rather than a question. *Whether it should be bounded* is the design
    question: [`adr/0009`](../docs/adr/0009-parking-is-modelled-supply-never-search.md)'s graceful
    degradation is stated entirely about the **arrival** — *"the shed widens, the walk Leg lengthens"*
    — and says nothing about the departure, which the build has since made the longer of the two.
  - ⚠ **It is not obviously a defect.** A person who parked at work and slept at home really does have
    a walk ahead of them, and refusing to model it would be the *unbuilt mechanism as a design
    constraint* trap ([`adr/0070`](../docs/adr/0070-an-unbuilt-mechanism-is-not-a-design-constraint.md)).
    What is genuinely open is whether the **Commute Budget** should be spending against it, since
    [`adr/0095`](../docs/adr/0095-a-commute-budget-is-three-rungs-and-only-the-last-one-refuses.md)'s
    three rungs are percentiles of a **free-flow, foot-only** distribution taken before either flanking
    Leg had a cost at all.
  - **Blocked on nothing and blocking nothing today.** Milestone 7 task 8's run is the machine that
    would size it, and this row is what that task should read first.

- ⚠ **Where a route lives at 1M — NEW 2026-08-14, and the premise is a *sharing rate* rather than a
  memory figure.** [`adr/0047`](../docs/adr/0047-routing-never-keys-on-the-district.md) moved route
  storage out of the travel-time matrix and sent the routes to *"the route cache"*; 5c task 4 built
  that cache and measured what a key on **pairs** buys over a key on **travellers**. It is the share of
  commutes that share a node pair with another commute: **17.78% at 4,000 Citizens, 7.52% at 16,000,
  and falling** as the paved extent grows with the population. That is
  [`adr/0012`](../docs/adr/0012-routing-intent-lives-in-the-agent.md)'s own *"the benefit cannot be
  settled at all until Trip generation exists"* — settled, and small. ***A key that merges almost
  nothing is a key chosen for a property the traffic does not have.***
  - **The access pattern is the second half.** A commute is a **once-per-Day cyclic scan** — every
    employed Citizen departs once a Day, in an order `CommuteRoster` fixes — which is the pattern LRU
    is provably worst on. Measured at 16,000 Citizens against a **21.30%** ceiling: LRU **2.83%**,
    Random **3.79%**, MRU **19.54%**, refuse-to-displace **22.41%**. `RouteEviction` now defaults to
    MRU. ⚠ **Random fails here and succeeds in the textbook because the textbook cache is fully
    associative** — a four-way set churns out under a random victim too.
  - ⚠ **No memory figure belongs in this entry and an earlier version of it carried three.** They were
    built by extrapolating a route-length **maximum** where memory scales on a median, multiplying by
    an employment ratio measured off `rulesets/minimal.toml` — a file whose own header says it models
    no city, and whose `[[building]] jobs = 8` on the dwelling kind gives 0.96 jobs per resident — and
    conflating the **cache working set** (distinct pairs over a Day) with the count of routes that must
    **exist at once** (in-flight Travellers, which `spike-results` puts at 37,000–111,000). All three
    are withdrawn. ***An extrapolation is a claim about a mechanism, not about a curve***: foot route
    length is capped absolutely by the Commute Budget — 50 minutes at 5 km/h is 4.17 km, about 32
    blocks — and the fitted √population curve ran straight through that ceiling.
  - **measurable, and the machine is named.** The distribution that decides it is a **car** route
    distribution: by car the Budget's ceiling reaches 41.7 km against a 19.2 km city at 1M, so it does
    not bind and route length is a property of the map instead. **No drive Leg exists until 5c task 5**,
    which is where the median, p90 and tail are to be taken. Until then this question has a premise and
    no arithmetic.
  - **The candidates, so the sitting does not start from scratch.** Per-**Citizen** storage
    ([`adr/0060`](../docs/adr/0060-a-habit-route-is-a-small-set-of-variants-and-which-one-you-take-is-who-you-are.md)'s
    Habit), which gets 100% on a commute and ⚠ **only on a commute** — `adr/0067` picks a shopping
    provider *per occasion*, and `06` names seven Trip generators, so per-Citizen storage scales with
    population × purposes and only the commute slot ever hits. **Hierarchical** over the routing
    partition. **Microscopic-only** storage with Statistical Legs served from the matrix, ⚠ which
    couples storage to `adr/0007`'s *fidelity is a property of place* and would make a Citizen's
    footprint depend on where it is standing. Or **no storage at all** for Statistical Legs — compute,
    attribute volume, discard — which `adr/0041` did not refuse, since what it refused was a
    *District-granular, cycle-driven* distribution and this would be per-Segment and exact.
  - ⚠ **What must not be reopened on this.** `adr/0047` retired the District next-hop table on **four
    grounds and not one of them was cost or a hit rate.**
  - **Nothing is blocked today.** The cache pays **23.5×** on the city it can cover — 0.525 µs served
    against 12.349 µs searched at 4,000 Citizens.
- ⚠ **What fraction of Citizens work — NEW 2026-08-14, and it multiplies every population-scaled
  estimate in the routing cluster.** `[[building]] jobs = 8` on the **dwelling** kind gives 0.96 jobs
  per resident (`0002` §D), and the shipped Rulesets produce an employment-to-population ratio of
  **31–33%** in a run. Real-world comparison, offered by the user rather than derived here: US
  employment-to-population is **~60% of the civilian population aged 16+ and ~48% of everyone**
  including children — and a Citizen in this simulation is everyone, at ~2.78 per Household. **The
  ratio is currently an artefact of a file that says it models no city**, and it was used as a design
  input three times on 2026-08-14 before anybody looked at it. **arguable** where it is a design target
  and **measurable** where it is a consequence of `[[building]] jobs`; the two must not be confused,
  which is `adr/0070` — a number is not a mechanism.
- **`§5`, the traffic model — the most detailed unargued design in the project**- **`§5`, the traffic model — the most detailed unargued design in the project**, and now under a
  Microscopic Cap that binds far harder at 1M. So is **`§2`, the Citizen model**.
- ~~**Audit rate and escalation policy.**~~ ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-16 by session E**
  ([`adr/0108`](../docs/adr/0108-repeated-divergence-is-a-bug-report-and-the-queue-decides-how-long-a-promotion-lasts.md)).
  **The escalation policy is that there is none** — `03 §4` invariant 3's queue guard bounds a
  promotion, permanent promotion is refused, and the record's consumer is `§5.1`'s suite, which `§5.1`
  had named since the first commit. **The rate's *unit* is settled** — a coverage period, not a sample
  size (`adr/0059`) — and **its value moves to §D2 as a gap**, not to this list: it is a number, so it
  is `adr/0052`'s and not this section's.
- **Freight weighting** — whether freight contributes to Segment Stress identically to commuters.
  Coupled to lane distribution, since a weighting only matters if freight is mechanically distinct.
- **Does traffic distribute across parallel Lanes at all?** If lane choice is made only for the next
  turn, a car queues behind a bus with three empty Lanes beside it. Not wrong at the road level,
  possibly wrong at the lane level, and **visible**. It is dwell time specifically; stops are free.
- **The arithmetic of a Disaster response.** How many units dispatch, from where, and what *contained*
  means as a stopping condition. Nobody has argued it.
- **What happens when transit wait exceeds the Commute Budget for a whole corridor.** Transit scarcity
  is a gradient everywhere else; this is the one place it could fail hard, and hard failure is the
  thing this design says to suspect.
- **Audit every displayed figure against *if an indicator would change when the simulation is
  optimised, it is not a trajectory*.** Gridlock was one instance and has been severed. The pass has
  never been run.
- ⚠ **`§3.7` makes walking free of congestion permanently, so a compact city is a strategy that
  bypasses the traffic model entirely — and nothing built charges for compactness.** *Raised by the
  user 2026-08-14, out of the job-search box measurement.* **A** on the intent, **M** on the threshold;
  owner is a session, after a Ruleset that models a city. The refusal is real and **counts as evidence**
  under [`adr/0070`](../docs/adr/0070-an-unbuilt-mechanism-is-not-a-design-constraint.md): `§3.7` does
  not omit pedestrian capacity, it **declines** it with an argument (*"pedestrian networks do not
  saturate at this scale"*) and makes it permanent (*"walking has one fidelity, permanently"*,
  *"pedestrians never contribute to Stress"*). So this waits on no unbuilt mechanism and no milestone
  will change it. **The arguable half**: whether a city small enough to walk *should* cost the player
  anything, given that compact cities are a legitimate and admired real outcome — `NO VERDICT` says the
  game must not punish a shape, and `PLAYER GOVERNS` says the player may choose it. **The measurable
  half is largely already measured and may dissolve the question**: at the design density a city's width
  is `sqrt(population ÷ 3700)` km, so walkability breaks near **64,000 Citizens**, where the city
  reaches the 4.17 km ceiling — and `JobSearchBoxTests` reads locality at **100.0%** up to 40,000 and
  **11.7%** at 160,000, bracketing it from the other side. **Two riders, and the first is the one to
  check first.** ⚠ **3,700/km² is circular** — it is an *output* of the 1M target (§D, and `0012`'s
  *Cause 5* registry), so the 64,000 threshold restates the map rather than corroborating it; the
  build-derived bracket 2,738–5,136/km² moves it to **48,000–89,000**. ⚠ **The density cap is Ruleset
  content, not engine**, so denser housing raises the threshold and *"no Ruleset that models a city
  exists yet"* — which makes the honest form of the question **at what population does a compact city
  stop being walkable, under a Ruleset that models one**, and that Ruleset is the blocker rather than
  the argument. **What makes it a row now rather than later**: the first-hour target is **10,000
