  Citizens**, comfortably inside the walkable band, so the traffic model — the most elaborate system in
  the design — engages nowhere in the period the player forms their impression. That is a **pacing**
  claim rather than a balance one, and it is the half no counterweight would fix. **The only built
  counterweight to compactness is pollution concentration**. ⚠ *(This read **land value and desirability
  are named holes that throw** until 2026-08-20; milestone 9 built both, so they are counterweights that
  exist — but **nothing in the city reads land value yet**, so what changed is the field and not the
  pacing claim.)*

### `04-economy-and-goods.md`

- ⚠ **What a Policy's standing setting is keyed on.** **NEW 2026-08-18, from milestone 10 decision 5**
  ([`adr/0118`](../docs/adr/0118-govern-fits-the-four-fields-and-the-hard-part-is-that-it-writes-to-the-ruleset-rather-than-to-the-world.md)).
  ***Every verb before `Govern` wrote to the world; `Govern` writes to the Ruleset in force.*** `Zone`,
  `Connect`, `Trip` and `Populate` are one-shot mutations on world entities. **A tax rate is a standing
  setting** — set at Tick 100, still in force at Tick 100,000 — so it is **saved, hashed state**, and its key
  is a `RuleId`: a dense **positional** index into the Ruleset file, whose *name* exists only in
  `Borough.Formats` ([`adr/0048`](../docs/adr/0048-the-ruleset-is-validated-where-it-is-parsed-and-only-integers-and-strings-cross-into-the-core.md)).
  ⚠ **Replay is safe and the live case is not, and they must not be collapsed.** `TickInput.RulesetHash` is
  populated every Tick and the reload runs at the top of Phase 0, so a logged command always resolves
  against the file the player was looking at. But a designer inserting a `[[rule]]` under
  [`adr/0015`](../docs/adr/0015-all-tuning-data-is-hot-reloadable.md) shifts every later index, and while
  `RulesetDegradation` re-arms the world's Rules, ***a saved setting cannot be re-derived***. **An identifier
  that is safe inside a table is unsafe as a Policy's key.** Two candidates and neither is chosen: a hash of
  the Rule's authored **name**, which `adr/0048` puts in `Formats`; or an explicit authored `[[policy]]`
  identity, which is `adr/0015`'s own direction of making the stable thing **content**. **Blocking nothing
  today** — milestone 10 builds no `Govern` command — and live the moment one is built.
- ⚠ **What happens when a player issues a command the treasury cannot pay for.** **NEW 2026-08-18, from milestone 10 decision 3**
  ([`adr/0116`](../docs/adr/0116-the-treasury-opens-empty-and-a-founding-balance-is-a-ratio-this-milestone-holds-neither-side-of.md)).
  [`adr/0035`](../docs/adr/0035-infrastructure-is-priced-by-what-it-consumes.md) §3a specifies the unfunded state
  **completely** — *the Rule that could not draw waits and subscribes to the Bin that was short* — and
  ***the first ten minutes are made of commands, not Rules***. `Simulation.ApplyConnect` throws on a malformed
  payload or returns silently when nothing changed; there is **no refusal path, no return value, and nowhere for
  *you cannot pay for this* to go**. A Rule waits; **a command cannot wait, so it must be refused, and a refusal
  is a stop** — in exactly the region `01 §3` promises there is none. ***Specifying what happens when a Rule
  cannot pay is not specifying what happens when the player cannot.*** ⚠ **Undesigned rather than unbuilt**
  ([`adr/0070`](../docs/adr/0070-an-unbuilt-mechanism-is-not-a-design-constraint.md)): no document states it, and
  it is in **neither** of [`06`](../docs/06-roadmap.md)'s two inventories — second sighting of that gap after mode
  choice, and the sibling table there says in its own header it cannot be complete. **The likely shape is not a
  treasury mechanism**: the shell asks `Core` whether an action is affordable *before* it reaches the Input Log,
  which is a **sim/render boundary** decision (`05 §2`) and puts a query on the hot/cold axis — so this may end up
  under `05` rather than here. **Blocked on nothing and blocking nothing today**, because no command has a price;
  it becomes live with the first one, which is `adr/0035`'s construction cost.
- ~~**`§7` (Jobs) is stale by construction.**~~ **Grilled 2026-08-15** →
  [`adr/0104`](../docs/adr/0104-a-skill-tier-is-earned-by-attendance-and-the-credential-stays-a-wall.md).
  **A city produces its own skilled workers**, Sorting becomes the *fast* channel, the credential stays
  a **wall** at 2 → 3, and the segments stay **three**. The section's other three staleness defects were
  corrections rather than decisions and are fixed in place: it described employment with **no mention of
  wages** (`adr/0026`), it had **Households** choosing workplaces where employment is a **Citizen**'s
  (5b-bis), and it gave a labour-starved Business **one** lever where `adr/0026` gives two — *pay more,
  or be smaller*. ⚠ **What is still open in `§7` is the row below**, which the sitting did not reach.
- **Labour as an input Bin, against `adr/0026`'s jobs.** `02 §4.1` dissolves *only produces if staffed*
  into a labour input Bin filled by arriving commute Trips, and session B loaded it further by folding
  **experience** in as a per-worker deposit multiplier. `adr/0026` has jobs as a Household↔Business
  relationship, so the Bin is a *second representation of the same fact*. `§7` (Jobs) is stale twice
  over and is where it lands. **An economy session, not `02 §4`'s.**
- **How construction consumes Materials** — drawn down over Days or in one transaction. Raised in
  priority by `adr/0022`: Materials imports are the growth brake, and this decides how sharply it bites.
- **Whether Outside Connection prices drift**, and on what. Unified with the Hinterland, so if anything
  drifts it all drifts together. Drawdown is the only non-arbitrary candidate found so far.
- ~~**`§3` (Movement) and `§6` (how a shortage becomes an unhappy person) have never been grilled.**~~
  **`§6` was grilled 2026-08-15** → [`adr/0102`](../docs/adr/0102-a-housed-departure-is-a-comparison-the-household-re-runs-not-a-threshold-it-crosses.md),
  [`adr/0103`](../docs/adr/0103-a-need-is-where-a-frequent-private-failure-accumulates.md). **Steps 6 and 7
  rewritten, the Need set settled at four, and no number opened.** ⚠ **Its own residue is that steps 1, 2
  and 5 are untouched** — the District Pool draining, the price rising to the import ceiling, and the
  Provider List cursor — and all three rest on mechanisms that do not exist (`Scope.Pool` throws, there
  are no prices, there is no Provider List in `src/`), so they are `adr/0070` territory rather than
  ungrilled prose. **`§3` (Movement) is still untouched.**

### `05-technical-architecture.md`

- **`§6` threading, the whole of it** — which thread runs `step()`; who owns and publishes the
  transform history (`adr/0037` left this downstream); whether hot-path results need **generation
  tagging** (`adr/0002` leaves the row open); whether cold queries drain from a queue or are called
  between Ticks; the `adr/0033` wait-list check; the `§1.1`-versus-`§6` parallelism contradiction
  (the phase table states *permission*, `§6` states *implementation*, neither says so); **Phase 4 Move
  is marked parallel and `§6` never mentions it**; and `adr/0034`'s point-of-use noise queries moving a
  spatial search into Decide. **Phase 2 — it was recorded as gating *"milestones 10 and 11"*, and ⚠ **both halves are wrong**: there has never been a milestone 11, and old-10 is now **8**, which session K found unblocked. **Owner: session R** (`06` → *Obligations no milestone can hold*).**
- **`§2`, the sim/render boundary and the snapshot format.** Sharpened by the threading answer:
  `visible_agents` is a cross-thread query, so format and buffer lifetime are one question.
- ~~⚠ **Does a save carry a verified State Hash?**~~ ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-18 by milestone 8 task 10 —
  it does, and the premise the question rested on was false.** `adr/0087` asks for a hash *"computed on
  the background thread from the copy"*; task 6 recorded that as unbuildable because `HandleColumn.Fold`
  folds the target row's monotonic id and a handle's bytes do not contain one. True of **a column** and
  false of **the file** — `Rows.cs:72-73` declares `id` and `generation` as `Saved`, so the id is in the
  copy, in another table's block. ***A value absent from a column's own bytes can still be present in the
  copy.*** So the clause is **honoured rather than overturned**, the header is nine fields and 60 bytes,
  and `05 §4` invariant 6 is a property of **every load**.
  [`adr/0112`](../docs/adr/0112-the-saved-set-is-the-hashed-set-so-a-save-can-compute-its-own-state-hash.md),
  amending `adr/0087` and `adr/0111`. ⚠ **The finding outlives the answer**: the false claim was pinned
  by a **test**, `A_fold_over_the_bytes_is_not_the_state_hash`, which is still green and was always about
  something else. ***A test name is a description of the build*** (`adr/0093`), and **a negative test is
  the most quotable kind, because it reads as a closed door.**
- ~~⚠ **Is `Borough` the name?**~~ ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-18 with the user in the room, by splitting the
  question.** `Borough` is the **codename** and is frozen; a **public name** is a separate decision, is
  deferred, and has no deadline. The magic number is a codename in a file header, so
  [`0003`](0003-build-plan.md) §*The name*'s trigger is ***discharged rather than met*** — the only
  player-visible strings are two file extensions that nothing dispatches on. Full record, including the
  boundary between the two and the three filters that turn out not to apply to a public name, is in that
  section.
- **Is the travel-time matrix saved or rebuilt on load?** Leaning rebuild — a cache, not state.
  Consistent with wait lists being rebuilt.
- **A save migration path for the kernel radius**, which is a shape `adr/0015` has no word for. The
  radius is world-creation-fixed because a Cell is stored in kernel units, but unlike `TICKS_PER_DAY`
  nothing is *lost*: one full re-diffusion repairs the map. `adr/0015` offers *reload freely* and
  *refuse*; this wants a third answer, **migrate**. If a third member appears it is `adr/0015`'s
  two-category split that reopens, not `adr/0044`.
- **A save migration path for a Chunk size change.** Chunk size is on the *cannot be retrofitted* list
  and nothing describes what happens if a profile later says it should move. **Its own session.**
- ~~**Does the World hold the current Tick, or does the Simulation?**~~ **CLOSED the day it was filed**,
  into [`adr/0058`](../docs/adr/0058-the-tick-is-state-so-the-world-holds-it-and-the-hash-folds-it.md):
  the World holds it, as a saved and hashed column on a one-row table, and the invariant tiers stopped
  taking a Tick at all — so the bug class is *unrepresentable* rather than fixed. **What closed it was
  not the argument in this row.** The entry led with *hash-bearing by relocation* as the obstacle, and
  that was the wrong lead: the only recorded hashes are three baselines in this repository and
  `GoldenHashTests` names the command that regenerates them, so a re-record is a commit and not a
  compatibility event. `adr/0052`'s caution is calibrated for a number somebody **chooses**. **Two things
  that were not on this row turned out to be the content.** Adding a table changes the *composition*, so
  the golden `README`'s own step 3 applied and `World.HashSeed`'s version byte is bumped to `02` — the
  procedure caught what the entry had not. And folding the clock **kills within-run flatness**: every
  hash sample now differs from the last, so *"an idle Tick changes nothing"* had to move to a
  clock-excluded fold, where it says what it always meant. Cross-run bisection is untouched. **The rest
  of this row stands and is what is still open**, which is why the text is kept rather than deleted:
  `_phase` and `_inForce` are still on the Simulation, in the same position the Tick was, and
  `_inForce`'s save semantics are `§7`'s unargued format half. ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-17 by milestone 8
  task 4** ([`0030`](0030-save-load.md) D5): **all five fields on the `Simulation` dissolve** rather than
  joining the World. `_phase` is `Commit` at every save because `adr/0087` takes the copy at the end of
  phase 7, and **a value with one possible value is not state**; `_inForce` **is** the Ruleset content
  hash and is a header field, so saving it too would be a second copy of a header entry; `_opened` is
  `true` in every save on `_phase`'s own argument; and `_reloads` and `_degradation` were **already
  documented as per-run rather than per-world in their own remarks**, which is where the walk found three
  of the five answers. ⚠ **Dissolving out of the save is not dissolving out of the loader** — a fresh
  `Simulation` has `_opened = false`, so the first step after a load would take its Ruleset through the
  **opening** branch rather than the **transition** one and record no provenance trail entry, which is
  the trail's own bug class at the moment it was designed for. *Original entry follows.*
- **Does the World hold the current Tick, or does the Simulation?** Today the Simulation does — `_tick`
  is a private `ulong` on it — and **it is therefore in no `[Table]`, in no field declaration, and so
  neither hashed nor saveable.** *(`_phase` and `_inForce`, the Ruleset in force, sit beside it in the
  same position, which is why this is one question rather than three.)* **Three mechanisms have now paid
  for the answer being *no*, which is what promotes it from a shrug to an entry.** Slice 8's
  `World.Adopt` had to take the Tick **as a parameter** to compute an arming stagger. Slice 9 then added
  three checks over the Event Wheel and **all three are relative to a *now* the wheel cannot reach
  except through its caller** — the double-arm refusal rests on time being monotone, the period bound on
  the caller passing a **truthful** Tick, and `Unlink` on the phase order. And the cost of that was not
  theoretical: the end-of-run tier had been stamping every violation **`Tick 0`** in both 100,000-Tick
  acceptance runs, because a fresh `Simulation` over an already-run world reports `_tick` = 0, and
  **nothing caught it for as long as no invariant read the stamp.** A number that must be passed in
  correctly by every caller is a number the World should probably own. **Two things make it a real
  question rather than an obvious repair.** `Simulation._tick` is the **next** Tick to run rather than
  the last one run, and moving it moves that convention into the hash's blast radius — so it is
  *hash-bearing by relocation*, which `adr/0052` has no row for. And **invariant 6, the Factorio test,
  needs the Tick to survive a save** and does not exist yet, so the mechanism that would force the
  answer is the one mechanism nobody has built. **Where it belongs:** with `§7`'s format half rather
  than on its own, since *what is saved* and *who owns it* are the same sitting. Raised by
  [`0016`](0016-the-event-wheel.md) → *What building it found*, item 5.
- **`§1`, `§7`'s format-and-migration half, `§8`, `§10` remain unargued.**

### Routing — session M's cluster

⚠ **SWEPT 2026-08-13 against [`adr/0047`](../docs/adr/0047-routing-never-keys-on-the-district.md), and
four of this cluster's five bullets were dead.** Three are struck below — the representative funnel, the
player-drawn District, and *the network runs out of routes* — and the fourth, the cost unit, had already
been struck by `adr/0071`. **One survives, and the ADR says so by name**: `adr/0047` closes ledger
entries 10, 11, 13 and 15 together and records that ***decision 8 does not — it turned out to be a
different question***. So what is left of session M's routing cluster is **the Commute Budget's
granularity**, plus one entry that was never in it. *The sweep is recorded rather than merely performed,
because the failure it corrects is not that anybody was careless: `adr/0047` listed the entries it
closed, in itself, and closing them in the ledger is a **separate act** nobody was assigned.*

~~Beyond the blocking entries in section A — of which **the diversion policy is now the one to answer
first**, because it is the only one with a measured overshoot behind it and because two of its three
levers are Ruleset numbers section D already lists as unset:~~ **The diversion policy closed
2026-08-10 into [`adr/0061`](../docs/adr/0061-a-diversion-rejoins-by-local-descent-and-a-rejoin-is-never-a-search.md)**
— a Rejoin is local descent and never a search. What remains:

- ⚠ **What makes a city in this simulation congest at all?** **NEW 2026-08-14 from 5c task 6, and
  ⚠ REWRITTEN THE SAME DAY — the entry's own headline was the thing that was wrong.** The
  volume-delay function is built, correct and exercised. What it is inert on is a question of
  **scale and trip rate**, and the four missing terms are named below with a multiple against each.
  It stays *arguable* under `adr/0043` because what is open is a design question about demand; the
  measurable half has now been taken **twice**, and the second taking is the one to read.

  ⚠ **The first taking was wrong in four independent ways, and only one of them was a wrong
  measurement.** They are set out rather than struck, because each failed differently and the set is
  worth more than the correction.

  **1. Every occupancy figure was 5.6× too high.** `TripEngine.AdvanceTravellers` handled each
  Traveller once per call and went on to the next slot, so a drive crossed **one Segment a Tick**
  whatever the crossing cost, and the sub-Tick carry could only ever *add* delay. Measured: a journey
  quoted at **2.10** Ticks took **11.66**. That is `adr/0041`'s *a vehicle crosses about one Segment
  per Tick* — stated by that ADR as following from `TicksPerDay = 8192`, retired by `adr/0094` —
  living in a **second site**, which task 6 did not check while repairing the first.
  ***A premise that expires retires every site resting on it, and finding one of them is not finding
  them.*** Fixed 2026-08-14 (`StatisticalTravelTimeTests`), and ⚠ **the floor had been *masking* the
  volume-delay function rather than exaggerating it**: a free-flow crossing is 0.22 Ticks, so while
  every crossing was rounded up to one, BPR had to multiply the true cost by more than 4.6 before
  anything downstream could see it. The capacity sweep's ×1.0088 at 400 Vehicles an hour and ×1.8949
  at 200 are now **×1.1925** and **×7.0869**. ***A floor under a quantity is a dead zone in every
  function of it.***

  **2. The peak dial's ×1.0000 was an observation window, and the dial works.** The sweep stepped
  **512 Ticks from Tick 0 on a 2,048-Tick Day**. At `commute_peak_factor = 192` the departure window
  is `ceil(2048/192)` = **11 Ticks**, so departures happen on Ticks 0–10 — which is before anybody
  holds a job, because `[jobs] revisit_ticks` is 1,024. It measured a burst of nobody. **The tell was
  in its own table and was read as a result**: vehicle-Ticks *fell* 23,760 → 5,003, and concentrating
  a fixed number of commutes cannot reduce their total. ***A dial that moves a rate is refuted only
  by a window longer than the period it moves it over.***

  **3. The flat 0.44 is the busiest Segment, and the busiest Segment is a coincidence.** Peak `v/c`
  0.44 at 4,000, 16,000 and 64,000 Citizens alike is a **maximum over ~10⁶ Segment-Ticks** at a mean
  near 0.02 — which is the tail of a low-mean Poisson and is flat because such a tail barely moves.
  The **mean**, which is what BPR prices, was never printed by anything. This entry coined
  ***a peak that a mean does not follow is a coincidence, and a volume-delay function prices means***
  and then rested its own headline on the peak.

  **4. And `√population` sizes the supply without sizing the demand.** This entry's headline —
  *the same number sizes both the demand and the supply* — is **measured false**. Supply grows as
  `P`; demand grows as **`P` × journey duration**, and duration grows with the city's linear extent.
  Load therefore **grows**, at about `P⁰·³⁴`:

  | Citizens | Segments | peak in flight | per Segment | mean `v/c` | modal commute |
  |---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
  | 4,000 | 227 | 23 | 0.101 | 0.0110 | — |
  | 16,000 | 779 | 121 | 0.155 | 0.0168 | **1–2 min** |
  | 64,000 | 3,023 | 844 | 0.279 | 0.0303 | — |
  | 256,000 | 11,952 | **5,004** | **0.419** | **0.0454** | **4–8 min** |

  *(One-hour rush hour, 3,600 Vehicles/hour Street, peak in flight over the second Day's window.
  **4.15× over a 64× population**, which is not flat and is not linear either.)*

  **The rush-hour sweep, measured over a whole Day at 16,000 Citizens**, and it is the shape of the
  correction rather than a lever:

  | `commute_peak_factor` | window | in-world | peak in flight | busiest `v/c` | delay |
  |---:|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|
  | **3** (shipped) | 683 T | 8.0 h | 19 | 0.43 | ×1.0053 |
  | 12 | 171 T | 2.0 h | — | 0.43 | ×1.0053 |
  | 24 | 86 T | 1.0 h | 121 | 0.65 | ×1.0269 |
  | 48 | 43 T | 30 min | **222** | 0.65 | ×1.0269 |
  | 192 | 11 T | 7.7 min | — | 1.09 | ×1.2079 |

  ⚠ **In-flight scales exactly with the departure rate — 19 → 222 for a 16× narrower window — and
  the busiest Segment moves 4 → 6.** The dial does precisely what it says and buys almost nothing,
  because what it raises is the mean and what was being read was the tail. **This is finding 3 and
  finding 2 arriving together**, and it is why the sweep had to be re-run rather than re-argued.

  ### The four terms, each with a multiple

  | missing | today | worth |
  |---|---|---:|
  | **Population at target** | 256,000 measured, 1,000,000 designed | ~1.6× |
  | **Employment** | **32%** of Citizens hold a job | ~3× |
  | **More than one Trip a Day** | the commute is the only generator; `06` lists **seven** more with no milestone | 3–4× |
  | **A Day with a shape** | one departure, uniform over a window | ~2× and it makes the others legible |

  Against a shortfall of roughly **20×** to a mean `v/c` near 0.8, ***the arithmetic closes*** — and
  every term in it is a **demand** mechanism the design already intends. None is a road.

  ⚠ **The fourth term is the one this entry had not named, and it is not the peak factor.** *(Raised
  by the user 2026-08-14, after the sweep above.)* `commute_peak_factor` sets a window's **width** and
  the departures are **uniform inside it**; there is one journey a Day and no return. A commute in any
  real city is **bimodal** — out in the morning, back in the evening — so what is missing is the Day's
  **shape**, not its concentration. Two things follow that the width dial cannot supply. It is worth
  ~2× on Trips outright, which this entry previously dismissed in those terms (*"doubling the trips
  doubles 0.032 to 0.064; the gap is not a factor of two"*) — **true, and a 2× is a fifth of a 20×
  stack rather than a rebuttal, which is what dismissing a term one at a time costs**. And it repairs
  the reason there is no `peak_offset`: this file records that *when* within the Day a peak falls is
  unobservable because nothing asks the time — ⚠ **which stops being true with two peaks, because the
  interval between them is observable without any absolute clock**. ***A phase is unobservable and a
  gap between two phases is not.*** The refusal to build the evening leg is `CommuteEngine`'s own and
  is real — *a return journey makes a Citizen's day a schedule* — so this is `adr/0070`'s **build X**
  and not a question about compensating for its absence.

  ### What this leaves open, and what it closes

  **Closed**: whether the volume-delay function can be exercised at all. It can — at 16,000 Citizens
  with a 7.7-minute rush hour the busiest Segment reaches `v/c` **1.09** and **×1.21** on a **3,600
  Vehicle/hour** Street, with no absurd capacity anywhere.

  **Open, and it is a design question rather than a measurement**: which of the four terms this game
  wants, in what order. ⚠ **And a pacing claim that no counterweight fixes** — at the first-hour
  target of **10,000 Citizens** the city is ~2.5 km across and a drive is **1.4 in-world minutes**, so
  it genuinely cannot congest, and *that is correct*: a town of 10,000 has no traffic jams. The most
  elaborate system in the design engages nowhere in the period the player forms their impression,
  which is `§3.7`'s *walking is free of congestion* bullet in this same section arriving from the
  vehicle side.

  ⚠ **Consequences for 5c task 8, and they are hard preconditions.** A long run **cannot ratify
  `[traffic]`'s three numbers on a generated city** — the curve is evaluated only where it is nearly
  flat — and it must run against `rulesets/congested.toml`, whose 400 Vehicles/hour is a demonstration
  and not a city. **Read the mean and never the peak**: a peak over a low mean is a Poisson tail, and
  reporting it as congestion is what this entry did for a day.

  ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-15 by building the world, and it took an afternoon.** `ConnectedCityCongestionTests`
  lays a **dumbbell** — two zoned districts joined by **one** Street corridor, every Segment placed by
  `CommandKind.Connect` — and at the **shipped** 3,600 Vehicles an hour it peaks at `v/c` **65.1%**
  against a generated lattice's **43.4%** on the same Ruleset. Four times the district on the same one
  corridor, density held fixed, puts the priced world at **1,074%** where its free-flow control sits at
  **130%**. So the hard precondition above stands exactly as written — *a long run cannot ratify these
  numbers on a generated city* — and what it was missing is that it named the wrong replacement: not a
  longer run, not a lower capacity, **a differently-shaped city**.

  ⚠ **The finding that outranks the fixture: *the volume-delay function is a loop and not a formula*.**
  Congestion slows a Vehicle, a slower Vehicle dwells on its Segment longer, longer dwell **is** higher
  volume, and higher volume is more congestion — so past `v/c` = 1 it feeds itself, and 130% becomes
  1,074% rather than 1,074% being what BPR returns for 130%. Every quotation of BPR in this corpus reads
  it as a static curve. This is `03 §3.2`'s *use it only where it is strong* arriving with a number, and
  it is the argument for the Microscopic tier from a direction nobody took it from.

  ⚠ **NEW, and it is a gap in the build rather than a question: `CommandKind.Populate` and
  `CommandKind.Connect` are mutually exclusive at world creation.** `RoadGenerator.LayInto` **throws** on
  a world that already has Segments — it is a world-creation pass and says so — and
  `SyntheticCity.PopulateInto` calls it unconditionally, before it builds anything. So a player-shaped
  network cannot be populated in either order, and the fixture above grows its own population by copying
  that method's three loops. ***There is no door in the build through which a player-shaped network gets
  a population, because the only populator is welded to the only generator.*** Filed here rather than
  worked around silently (`adr/0073`); the workaround is in the test and the repair belongs to whoever
  next needs a Connect-laid city, which under `adr/0090` is every city a player will ever have.
  ✅ **REPAIRED 2026-08-15 — [`0003`](0003-build-plan.md)'s hash-moving queue, item 9, filed and built
  the same day.** `SyntheticCity.PeopleInto` is the populator's people half on its own, the fixture's
  copy is gone, the verb count did not move, and **1,438 tests are green with no baseline re-recorded**.
  ⚠ **The gap it was blocking is closed; the readings it was blocking are not** — the rows in §D above
  name this world as their ratifier and somebody still has to run it and call the numbers right or wrong.
  ⚠ **And building it found the weld was hiding an incomplete clamp**: at zero Lots the Building count
  divided by zero, which `PopulateInto` structurally could not reach, because `Subdivide` always lays at
  least one Lot. ***A mechanism with one caller has only the edge cases that caller can produce, so
  opening a door widens the input domain before it widens anything else.*** *Original filing note:* it
  had an owner as of 2026-08-15 — [`0003`](0003-build-plan.md)'s hash-moving queue, item 9 — and
  the promotion is what the second copy of the populator bought — a workaround filed once is a note, and
  a workaround that becomes a duplicate mechanism is a queue item. ⚠ **Two things that entry carries and
  this one did not.** Skipping `LayInto` is **not** the repair: `Subdivide` is keyed on
  `StreetGrid.Blocks`, which is `WorldTiles ÷ block_tiles + 1` — a property of the **map** and the
  Ruleset, never of what is laid — so the land half is wrong for a Connect world **twice, and only the
  first failure throws**. And the repair **moves no hash**, because every fixture and the golden session
  populate an empty graph; under [`adr/0100`](../docs/adr/0100-moving-the-state-hash-costs-nothing-until-somebody-is-carrying-a-save.md)
  that is neither an argument for doing it nor against.

  ~~⚠ **And a number to check before anybody re-tunes it.**~~ **STRUCK 2026-08-15 — the key it is about
  no longer exists.** [`adr/0101`](../docs/adr/0101-a-commute-is-two-journeys-and-the-days-shape-is-a-property-of-the-job.md)
  retired `commute_peak_factor` on 2026-08-15 and replaced the whole departure model: the Day's shape is
  now emergent from a Shift start band on the employing kind, so there is no peaking multiplier to
  re-tune and no `plans/0012` **Cause 5** to examine. ***A filing outlives the thing it is about, and
  nothing retires it when the mechanism goes*** — this note was written on 2026-08-14 and was moot within
  a day, which is `plans/0012` **Cause 2** (a decision whose consequences do not reach every document
  that rests on them) landing on a *filing* rather than on a decision. Struck rather than deleted,
  because the reasoning is sound and transplants to any future peaking key. *Original note follows.*
  ~~`commute_peak_factor = 3` was chosen from~~
  S2 R7's *morning peak of 2–3×*, which R7 measured on **in-flight Travellers**; the key is defined on
  **departure rate**. Those are the same number only if journey duration is constant across the
  comparison — and duration is exactly what the advance-loop repair moved by 5.6×. Possible
  `plans/0012` **Cause 5**, unexamined, filed here rather than acted on.

  <details>
  <summary><b>Superseded: the entry as it stood on 2026-08-14 before the re-measurement</b></summary>

  Kept rather than deleted, because three of its four errors are in the **statistic** rather than in
  the arithmetic, and a reader who only sees the correction learns none of them.

  **A three-dial sweep at 16,000 Citizens on `rulesets/minimal.toml`**, each dial authored Ruleset
  data, each run twice (with and without `[traffic]`):

  | dial | Segments | busiest one-way | `v/c` | vehicle-Ticks | loaded ÷ free |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | shipped | 769 | 4 | 0.44 | 23,760 | **×1.0000** |
  | `lots_per_segment` 5 → 32 — **5.3× less road per Building** | 145 | 5 | 0.54 | 11,364 | **×1.0000** |
  | `commute_peak_factor` 3 → 192 — **an 8-hour rush hour down to 8 minutes** | 769 | 5 | 0.54 | 5,003 | **×1.0000** |
  | **both** | 145 | **10** | **1.09** | 2,393 | **×1.0000** |

  ⚠ **Laying less road makes the city *smaller*, not busier**, and that is the first result worth
  keeping — it survives the re-measurement untouched. `SyntheticCity` sizes the paved extent to hit a
  **Lot** count, so cutting roads per Building shrinks the map, shortens every commute and takes total
  traffic **down 2.1×** while taking `v/c` up 0.1. ***Every supply dial this simulation exposes moves
  demand with it.***

  ⚠ ~~**And the last row is the sharp one: `v/c` 1.09 is genuinely over capacity and the run is still
  byte-identical.**~~ ~~Mean load there is **0.032 Vehicles per Segment per Tick** — the road is empty
  about 97% of the time — so a busiest-Segment reading of 10 is a **momentary coincidence of arrival
  times, not a jam**.~~ **The diagnosis is right and it was applied to the wrong row**: it is the
  *whole table* whose peaks are coincidences, including the ones this entry read as evidence.

  ~~**So the binding constraint is trips, not road.**~~ **Half right.** Everybody makes exactly one
  journey a Day, and that is one of four terms rather than the constraint. ~~**The network is bigger
  than the fleet at every setting of every dial.**~~ **False as stated** — it is bigger than the fleet
  at every setting of every *supply* dial, which is what was swept.

  ⚠ **The evening leg is not the answer either** — `CommuteEngine`'s refusal is real and is
  `adr/0070`'s *build X*. ~~**Doubling the trips doubles 0.032 to 0.064. The gap is not a factor of
  two.**~~ **The gap is not a factor of two and no single term was ever going to be**, which is what
  dismissing them one at a time concealed.

  ⚠ **A fourth dial closed the loop, and it is the supply side: `street_capacity_per_hour`.** At
  16,000 Citizens over 512 Ticks, varying nothing but the Street's capacity — ⚠ **and every row of it
  was taken under the 5.6× advance-loop defect; the corrected sweep is finding 1 above**:

  | Vehicles/hour | Vehicles a Segment holds at capacity | busiest `v/c` | `--traffic`'s two panels | loaded ÷ free |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 3600 (shipped) | 9.22 | 0.44 | identical | **×1.0000** |
  | 900 | 2.30 | 2.61 | identical | **×1.0000** |
  | 600 | 1.54 | 3.91 | identical | **×1.0002** |
  | **400** | **1.02** | **5.86** | **differ** | **×1.0088** |
  | 200 | 0.51 | 15.63 | identical (every block `@`) | **×1.8949** |

  ⚠ **The demand side cannot be moved and the supply side can, so `rulesets/congested.toml` cuts the
  road rather than raising the traffic** — 400 Vehicles an hour, which by Little's Law is a Segment
  holding **1.02** Vehicles where 3,600 holds 9.2. ***You have to make the Street absurd before a
  generated city will congest it.*** ⚠ **The first clause is now false** — the demand side moves, by
  a measured 12× on one dial — and the sentence in bold survives only for a city of **16,000 with an
  eight-hour rush hour**, which is the configuration nothing in the design asks for.

  ⚠ **The middle rows are a finding about the *instrument*, not about the city**, and this half
  stands: between 3,600 and 600 the bill moves and the two panels do not, because the extra dwell is
  real and **shorter than a Tick**. ***A per-Tick snapshot cannot see a sub-Tick delay***, so the bill
  and the picture are driven by different thresholds and an instrument showing only one of them is
  silent over most of its range.

  </details>

- ~~**The representative funnel.**~~ **CLOSED by [`adr/0047`](../docs/adr/0047-routing-never-keys-on-the-district.md), struck here 2026-08-13** — and it was closed **twice, in opposite ways**, which is why it is worth reading rather than deleting. R8 refuted the *mechanism*: the funnel **does not bind**, and excluding it — and then a four-Segment convergence zone around it — gives readings identical to the printed digit, so the binding term is the **tree upstream** and *multiple access nodes per District was the wrong fix*. `adr/0047` then deleted the *object*: there is no District-granular route, so there is no representative. **The ADR rejects both repairs this bullet was circling** — multiple representatives, and a Segment-granular tail on a District-granular route — on the same ground, that they repair the funnel, which is not what concentrates the traffic. *Original bullet follows.* Under either surviving path-source rung, every Trip into a District passes through one node, so its Stress is an artefact of the partition rather than a property of the city. Same defect class `03 §3.9` rejects for the Microscopic Cap and `adr/0041` rejects for volume, arriving a third time by a different door. `plans/0010` decision 11.
- ~~**A player-drawn District still changes the city**~~ **CLOSED by `adr/0047`, struck here 2026-08-13, and it is the bullet that ADR was most directly written to kill.** Routing does not key on the District, so moving a boundary moves what **pools** and never what a Traveller drives — `CONTEXT.md` → District has carried that sentence since. ⚠ **The dependency ran the other way too and is also discharged**: `adr/0047` names *"`06` still has player-drawn versus automatically derived Districts open, blocking milestone 5c"*, and that question **was already closed** — `02 §2.1` settles it as ***both***, automatic by default and player-adjustable, which [`plans/0010`](0010-s2-routing.md) records in terms (*"It is not open"*). So 5c was carrying a gate that was discharged on two independent grounds: the question was answered, **and** the ADR severed the dependency on it. *Original bullet follows.* …through the path source rather than through volume — both surviving rungs key on the District, so moving a boundary moves a representative and changes the Segments a Traveller drives. `plans/0010` decision 10.
- **The Commute Budget's granularity.** A matrix entry is wrong by 11.32% (6.73 Ticks) against a true
  query. Whether that is free or disqualifying depends on a granularity nothing states. **R6 is owed
  the same question about its cache key and the two should be answered once.**
- ~~**The cost unit for routing.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-11 by [`adr/0071`](../docs/adr/0071-travel-time-is-sub-tick-and-q16-16-is-a-scale-rather-than-a-meaning.md)**
  — travel time is **Q16.16 Ticks** and speed is **Q16.16 Tiles/Tick**, each its own type over an
  `int`, and **`05 §121` is amended in place**: Q16.16 is a *scale*, not a meaning. Closed by argument
  rather than routed to R7, correctly under `adr/0043` — the two candidate spellings are *the same
  bits*, so no machine could distinguish them and nothing was owed to one. **What settled it was in
  neither candidate**: `05 §121` and `02 §2` have contradicted each other since before the spike ran,
  one forbidding the representation of a speed the other mandates, and `Units.cs` resolved it silently
  in `02`'s favour. Applied literally the old sentence rounds a walking pace of 3.66 Tiles/Tick to 3.
  The residual risk is named in the ADR and is **not** this question: a volume-delay function could
  drive a jammed arc's cost toward the format's four-Day ceiling, which wants a saturating ceiling
  with a stated meaning once the VDF exists. *Original body:* R0 routes in Q16.16 Ticks and had to — a Tick is ~10.5 in-world
  seconds and a vehicle crosses about one Segment per Tick, so whole-Tick costs make A\* minimise *hop
  count* while appearing to route on time. But `05` says Q16.16 is for sub-Tile positions and nothing
  else. An integer count of a fixed fraction of a Tick measures identically, so **no number rests on
  this**; whether the core acquires a second Q16.16 meaning is the corpus's decision. Owed by R7.
- ~~**The network runs out of routes, not road**~~ **CLOSED, struck here 2026-08-13, and it took two ADRs because it was two claims.** The **diagnosis** stands untouched and is one of the four grounds `adr/0047` rejects the next-hop table on: *one tree per District means one route per (node, District) pair in the entire model*, and a tree holds **one** route to a place where a cache holds many, so **only one of the two can disperse traffic at all**. The **structure** that produced it is deleted by `adr/0047`, and the positive answer is [`adr/0060`](../docs/adr/0060-a-habit-route-is-a-small-set-of-variants-and-which-one-you-take-is-who-you-are.md) — a Habit is `k` variants, so the population disperses **before** any congestion response. ⚠ **What does *not* close with it is the evidence**: every figure in this bullet was measured **on the deleted tree**, so none of them may be quoted as a property of the shipped design. The live successor is §B's *does the route cache actually work* — the cache rests entirely on Trip repetition, and that is a number no spike can produce. *Original bullet follows.* — 87.25% of traffic on 1% of the carriageway, 90.87% of it empty, at 13% of holding capacity with capacity confirmed realistic, because one free-flow tree per District means one route per (node, District) pair in the whole model. Decision 11 on a different axis, and produced by R8 rather than argued.
- ~~⚠ **A failed Trip must demote the option that produced it.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-13, in the sitting
  that entered it — into [`adr/0097`](../docs/adr/0097-a-reach-failure-is-counted-on-the-citizen-and-a-stock-failure-is-not-remembered-at-all.md)
  for the half that is buildable, and VOID under [`adr/0070`](../docs/adr/0070-an-unbuilt-mechanism-is-not-a-design-constraint.md)
  for the half that is not.**
  **The sentence was one debt covering two failures**, and separating them settled it. An option fails
  because it is **full** or because it is **unreachable**;
  [`adr/0067`](../docs/adr/0067-a-shopping-attempt-is-a-trip-and-a-household-tries-one-provider-per-occasion.md)
  answered the stock half on **2026-08-10, three days after the debt was filed**, with a cursor —
  ***an answer that arrives after a debt is filed does not find its way to the ledger***, which is
  [`0012`](0012-corpus-audit.md) **Cause 2** in the direction the sweep does not look. The reachability
  half is `adr/0097`: **counted on the Citizen**, reset on employment, read by nothing yet, consumer
  named as milestone **19**'s Departure and deliberately not designed. ~~**No `adr/0052` number
  opened**, which is `adr/0059`'s direction a fifth time.~~ ⚠ **Building it on 2026-08-17 opened one
  after all, and the way out was to make it inert rather than to ratify it.** A count that must
  *saturate* has a saturation point, and any **reachable** one decides when attribution stops being
  exact for a consumer nobody has designed — `adr/0070`'s forbidden move — so *no width* and *a
  saturating width* could not both hold as the ADR wrote them. `CitizenTable.ReachFailures` is a
  `ushort`: ~32,000 Days of saturation against a 562-Day campaign, so it is a **wrap guard** and the
  bound stays 19's. ***A number can satisfy `adr/0052` by being unreachable rather than by being
  ratified***, and only one of those two survives a consumer arriving. See
  [`0028`](0028-evidence-the-accumulators.md) → *Task 3*.
  ⚠ **Two findings outrank the decision.** `adr/0047`'s loop **is not reachable in the build**: the job
  pass keys its candidate draw on the **Tick**, so every occasion draws different Buildings and the
  defect is live as a **recurring search bill** — ~32.5 µs a Dijkstra, ~5,400 of 10,000 Citizens, for
  ever — rather than as a loop. The loop needs *deterministic* re-evaluation, which is **5c**. And
  **`02 §9` was already violated and nobody had noticed**: its rule is *every aggregate figure must be
  able to name its constituents*, and 5b-bis's `jobs beyond budget` is an aggregate with no entity
  reference anywhere behind it — so *distance rather than supply separates them* was reportable in
  aggregate and true of nobody in particular. **The search bill is routed to
  [`0013`](0013-tick-budget.md)** under [`adr/0073`](../docs/adr/0073-a-local-workaround-is-not-a-discharge-and-a-finding-about-shared-code-must-reach-it.md);
  ***a byte on a row does not repair a bill***. *Original entry follows.*
  ⚠ **A failed Trip must demote the option that produced it.** **NEW to *this ledger* 2026-08-13, and
  to nothing else.**
  > ⚠ **CORRECTED WITHIN THE HOUR, AND THE CORRECTION IS THE MORE USEFUL HALF OF THE ENTRY.** This bullet
  > was first written as *"nothing in the corpus owns this… the sentence exists exactly once in the whole
  > corpus."* **That is false, and the corpus's handling of this debt was in fact exemplary.**
  > [`adr/0047`](../docs/adr/0047-routing-never-keys-on-the-district.md) filed it *owed to `adr/0017`*;
  > **`adr/0017:44` records it** as a written note ending *"Recorded here rather than invented, because
  > picking the mechanism is a design decision and this note is not one"*;
  > [`0012`](0012-corpus-audit.md)`:627` carries it as a **discharged** audit item saying the mechanism
  > *"is a design decision and belongs to a session"*; and [`0021`](0021-trips-legs-and-the-pedestrian-layer.md)
  > decision 5 argued it and made a recommendation. **Four documents, each doing its job.** The author of
  > the false sentence reached it by reading a `grep` truncated by `| head` and reporting a conclusion
  > rather than a location — ***a description of the build is where to look and never what you found***
  > ([`adr/0093`](../docs/adr/0093-a-description-of-the-build-is-where-to-look-and-never-what-you-found.md)),
  > committed while writing up a finding **about** `adr/0093`, in the same sitting. **The tool was the
  > proximate cause and the rule is the lesson**: a search result is a place to look, and a *negative*
  > search result is not a finding at all until the search is known to have been complete.
  > **What survives is the one true residue, and it is why this bullet stays**: the question is
  > **not in this file**, whose own opening rule is *every open question in the corpus appears here,
  > once*. A design question owned by *"a session"*, sitting correctly in four documents and in no
  > ledger, is invisible to the only file that answers *what needs answering*.
  **The defect is a live infinite loop in the choice model.**
  [`adr/0017`](../docs/adr/0017-agents-satisfice-they-never-optimise.md) re-evaluates *"immediately
  on a failed Trip"* against the same travel-time matrix, which still says the same wrong thing — so a
  Household can choose, fail, re-evaluate and **choose the same unreachable option for ever**. At a
  worst-case entry error of **77.62 Ticks** that is not an estimate, it is a different Trip. ***Finer
  granularity shrinks the error and does not close the loop; only the demotion does*** — which is why
  this cannot be discharged by §D2's partition-size row, and why the two must not be read as one
  question. **Arguable**, and genuinely so: what is owed is what a demotion *is* — how long it lasts,
  whether it attaches to the option or to the estimate, and whether it is observable — none of which a
  measurement settles. `02 §9` wants the diagnostic anyway. Owner: **a sitting**, and it is the only
  thing in this cluster that is still one.

### Never grilled at all

- **`adr/0005`–`0009` and `adr/0012`** — six decisions written from research and never argued: the
  fidelity tiers, the Event Wheel's own rule, walking, parking, routing intent. **All six gate Phase 2**
  (sessions **D**–**J**), and `0006` gates milestone 4.
- **`00-vision`'s pillars have never been challenged directly.** They are inherited from `plans/0001`
  and assumed by everything. The document's only remaining exposure.
- ~~**`01-player §1` (core loop), `§3` (first ten minutes)**~~ **— both closed 2026-08-12 by session P**
  ([`0025`](0025-the-player-model.md)), eighteen decisions and `adr/0090`–`0093`. **`01-player §4` (two
  hours, and twenty) is what remains**, and it carries a real problem with no answer: **268 km² of
  individually-placed service Buildings is a governability problem** — a **playtest** question that must
  not be grilled.
- **`06`'s Phase 2 ordering (K)**, which stays last — re-deriving it before `03 §5` is grilled is
  writing it twice.
- **Phase 3 is undesigned, not unplanned.** Rendering has never been designed and has no document to
  argue; every other phase is backed by `02`–`05`. Session **L**, itself blocked on S1 and S3.

### Unowned — no document covers it

- **What is health actually for?** Established as Attended and nothing more. Every other Service has a
  place in a causal chain; health has none, and a Service with no chain ends up as a desirability bar.
  The design has no illness, no mortality and no aging (`adr/0010`), so the obvious mechanism is
  foreclosed and something else has to justify it.
- **Is recreation only Amenity?** If Amenity is the whole answer, parks are the cheapest Service in the
  game and that should be stated deliberately rather than by omission.
- **On what axis do Service variants differ?** Deferred every time it has come up. Capacity alone makes
  variants a ladder the player graduates up, which is the error `adr/0025` refused for Density. Settle
  the axis before any variant is authored, even though the catalogue is Ruleset content.
- **Is car ownership endogenous?** Ownership is a persistent Household state with a purchase price and
  a per-Day running cost, and mode is a Provider List attribute. What is open is the *decision* —
  bought when habitual Trips are bad without one, sold under pressure — which would make ownership an
  **outcome of urban form rather than an input**. The counter is that it is a feedback loop, and this
  design has needed hysteresis in three places to stop that shape oscillating. The poverty interaction
  is unresolved: an unemployed Household sells the car, freeing money and removing reach, so **the
  absorbing state tightens by the act of surviving.**
- **Should a raw table door be reachable without the world's derived indices?** ⚠ **Filed 2026-08-17
  by milestone 8 task 1, which found the consequence rather than the question.** `BuildingTable.Create`
  creates a row; `World.CreateBuilding` (`World.cs:1189-1191`) creates the row **and** adds it to the
  Cell residency index. Both are reachable, they are one line apart in the same assembly, and the
  cheaper one leaves a derived structure stale with **nothing able to report it** — `CellNext` is
  `(derived AND rebuilt)` and `BuildingResidency`'s head, tail and count are not columns at all, so the
  gap is outside the State Hash twice over. It was live in `GoldenFixtures.Build()`, **the world every
  committed baseline is recorded from**, and the rebuild audit caught it on its first run.
  ***A door that maintains an index and a door that does not are indistinguishable at the call site,
  and the one that does less is the one with the shorter name.*** The repair candidates are all
  structural rather than diligence — make the table door `private protected` so the world's is the only
  way in, have the rebuild audit run over every fixture, or fold the residency arrays so the hash can
  see them — and they differ in what else they cost. **Arguable**: no number refutes any of them.
- **Where does a departing actor's balance go?** ⚠ **Filed 2026-08-19 by milestone 10 task 4, which
  found it by building the check that reports it, and widened by task 4b the same day — it has two
  subjects, a Household and a Business, and one answer would settle both.** `World.DestroyHousehold` frees the row with whatever
  is in `Money` and `Savings`, so a Household dying rich destroys conserved money — and
  `Invariant.MoneyIsConserved` now says so, loudly, the first time it happens. ⚠ **Task 4c rewrote the
  mechanism and left the question exactly where it was**: those two columns are deleted, a balance is a
  money Bin ([`adr/0114`](../docs/adr/0114-a-balance-a-rule-can-fail-on-is-a-bin-and-a-bins-owner-is-discriminated.md)),
  and `DestroyHousehold` now wakes that Bin's waiters and frees its row — so the money still leaves the
  world, by a second route, reported by the same invariant. ***A change of representation moves where a
  leak is written and never whether it leaks.*** It did make the loss **legible** for the first time: a
  freed Bin is a blame target disappearing, so a Rule sleeping on that balance is woken rather than left
  waiting on a slot nobody will ever write to again — which is why the free is a `WakeAll` and not a
  `Free` alone. Nothing in the corpus
  states a destination: `adr/0054` covers **eviction**, where the Household survives and keeps what it
  owns, and the only sink `CONTEXT.md` → Money admits is the **Outside Connection**, which is milestone
  **11**. So the answer today is *there is nowhere for it to go*, which makes this `adr/0070`'s
  **unbuilt** class with a date on it rather than a decision anybody is free to take now. ⚠ **It has no
  production caller and that is not reassurance** — the one caller is `GoldenFixtures.Build()`, and the
  fixture is why the invariant fired on day one. What has to be settled is whether a departure is a
  **gate crossing** (the money leaves the city and the supply of record falls with it, which is
  milestone 11's machinery used for a second purpose) or an **inheritance** (the balance moves to
  another Household or to the treasury, which needs a rule saying which). **Arguable**: no measurement
  distinguishes them, and both conserve.
  ⚠ **The Business is the same question with a different subject and a different accident.** Task 4b
  gave a demolished Building's Business the **worker** branch's treatment rather than the Household's —
  unlisted, row kept, premises handle severed — because there is no pool for a Business and freeing the
  row would take its balance out of the world. That conserves money and it leaves **a row nothing will
  ever free**, which is `adr/0006`'s shape. It is unreachable today (no pass creates a Business, so the
  loop is empty in every world the simulation builds on its own) and it is a leak the moment milestone
  **13** places one. ***Two actors were given different eviction rules by two milestones, on the
  strength of what each happened to have somewhere to go to*** — which is a reason about the build's
  furniture rather than about what should become of an actor the city stops housing, and it is why this
  row now has one question and two subjects rather than two rows.
- **Should the loader refuse a money Bin on a `[[building]]`?** ⚠ **Filed 2026-08-19 by milestone 10
  task 4.** [`adr/0113`](../docs/adr/0113-a-business-is-an-occupant-with-its-own-balance-and-a-building-never-holds-money.md)
  says a Building never holds money, and it is a decision with teeth — the clause is
  [`adr/0025`](../docs/adr/0025-density-is-a-cap-and-it-trades-land-for-materials.md)'s Cohort
  prohibition applied to the container, because `BuildingTable.OccupantHead` is the head of a **list**
  and a Bin on the Building is a sum over however many are in it. **The loader does not enforce it**: a
  `[[building]]` may declare a money Bin today and the only thing refused is a `capacity` key on one
  (`RulesetLoader.cs:1275-1290`). ***A decision whose whole content is a prohibition is worth nothing
  until something refuses.*** It is filed rather than built because it belongs beside **task 4b**, which
  is what gives a Business somewhere legitimate to hold a balance — a refusal shipped before the
  alternative exists refuses a shape with no replacement. **Arguable.**

---

## D — Numbers chosen and never ratified

Recorded on the day each was chosen rather than after it has been repeated until it reads as settled
([`0003`](0003-build-plan.md) rule 6). `CLAUDE.md`'s constants table is the same list in the code's
words; when they disagree, this file is the one that says *nobody ratified it*.

> ⚠ **What this section structurally cannot hold, found 2026-08-16 by session T** ([`0027`](0027-session-t-the-target-speed.md)).
> Its entry condition is [`adr/0052`](../docs/adr/0052-a-hash-bearing-number-is-chosen-with-a-named-ratifier-or-not-at-all.md)'s:
> **hash-bearing or world-creation**. The **target speed** — the number the entire Tick budget and every
> percentage in [`0013`](0013-tick-budget.md) is denominated in — is neither, because `CONTEXT.md` →
> Speed is categorical that the simulation cannot observe the tick rate and *no speed setting can change
> any outcome*. So it was never eligible for this table, it is not a wrong sentence so
> [`0012`](0012-corpus-audit.md) could not hold it either, and its only home in the corpus was one cell
> of `0013`'s *How this file gets less embarrassing*. It sat in `CLAUDE.md`'s constants table as
> *"15.6 ms at 4× speed"* for the life of the project: stated, cited, denominating everything, never
> chosen.
>
> ***A number that changes no state has no home in a ledger organised by state.*** It surfaced only when
> `06` built a table organised by **who owes what**, which is an argument for that table existing.
> **The value turned out to be right and its standing was not**, which is precisely the failure mode
> this file's own motto names — *an unratified number is more dangerous than an open question* — reached
> by a route the file cannot see. **No new rule is proposed**: `adr/0052`'s scope is correct and
> widening it to *every product number* would make this table unreadable. What is recorded is the blind
> spot, beside the two `06` already carries.

> **Triaged on 2026-08-08, because the list had eighteen rows and had never lost one.** That record —
> zero struck, ever, against `0012`'s 39 of 51 — was read as *numbers never get fixed here*, which is
> half right and hid the useful half. **Five of the eighteen were not unratified numbers at all**:
> one is a convention with no fact of the matter, three are provenance stamps on measurements with a
> named owner and a scheduled fix, and one is a defective derivation, which is a correction rather
> than a number. Of the thirteen that remain, **seven are unset**, and an unset number is a *gap*
> rather than a *debt* — it blocks work and cannot corrupt it, because nothing accretes on a value
> that does not exist. **The debt is the six that are in use.** They are the only rows here where
> waiting costs something, and they now sit in their own table so that fact is legible.
>
> **The standing rule the triage produced, now
> [`adr/0052`](../docs/adr/0052-a-hash-bearing-number-is-chosen-with-a-named-ratifier-or-not-at-all.md):**
> a **hash-bearing** or world-creation number may not be chosen without a *named* ratifier and a
> trigger written down beside it on the day it is chosen — a spike id, a session letter, a quantity,
> never a category. It does **not** require ratifying before choosing, which is often impossible and
> worse than the disease. `adr/0044` is why the rule exists: the Layer cadence was picked by argument,
> cited across three documents as settled, and had to be measured back out.

### D1 — In use and unratified. **This is the debt.**

The code reads these today, so work accretes on them and every day one stays wrong is a day of
accretion to undo. Ordered by what it costs to change them late.

| Number | Where it stands | What ratifies it |
|---|---|---|
| **Fertility's pollution weight** — `w_p` in `base fertility − w_s·Sealing − w_p·pollution` | **UNSET**, tuning, hash-bearing. **NEW 2026-08-22, `plans/0038` decision 1b**, from [`adr/0141`](../docs/adr/0141-fertility-composes-with-weights-and-only-one-of-them-is-a-number-anybody-chooses.md). ⚠ **It is ONE row and the decision could have opened two** — `w_s` is **derived** from an endpoint, since a Cell at Sealing 1024 has every Tile built on and therefore no farmland, so the Sealing term is `base × Sealing / 1024` and has **no key**. ***A coefficient with an endpoint is not a tuning knob***, which is [`adr/0059`](../docs/adr/0059-a-zone-rules-sample-is-a-revisit-period-so-the-ruleset-states-a-duration.md)'s shape. ⚠ **The weights are load-bearing rather than cosmetic**: unweighted, a Tile count of 0–1024 outweighs a pollution stock measured at *about 12 in kernel units under a strong source* by roughly **85:1**, which is an artefact of the representation | **Named ratifier: milestone 24's long run**, on a world with **varied terrain** and at least one emitting industrial source. **Quantity: a farm Cell's yield against its distance from a plume.** ⚠ **The refuting reading is stated in both directions** — a farm beside heavy industry whose yield is **unchanged** means it is too low, and a farm that dies from a neighbour's plume **alone**, with the ground unsealed, means it is too high. **Reopens** if Sealing stops being a count of Tiles, which would move `w_s`'s divisor and change what `w_p` is being balanced against |
| **How long a Household keeps looking before it gives up** — `[placement] gives_up_after_days`, **120** Days | `rulesets/bordered.toml`, the only shipped file that states it. **NEW 2026-08-21, milestone 11 task 7**, from [`adr/0130`](../docs/adr/0130-the-pools-bound-is-a-duration-and-the-unhoused-channel-ships-with-the-gate.md). Hash-bearing and hot-reloadable. ⚠ **It is a DURATION and the occasion count derives from it**, not the reverse — `adr/0059` one level down: authoring a count would make how long a family waits move whenever somebody retuned `[placement]`'s cadence. **120 Days is four months, and it is `00-vision.md`'s own figure** — *"Considered 20 dwellings over 4 months"*, read as a Household at exactly this bound on the day it left. ***That is a number borrowed from an illustration, which is the weakest provenance in this table***: the vision was describing a readout, not choosing a bound, and nothing has measured whether four months is how long a family in this city would actually keep looking | **Machine**: the acceptance run, `06` milestone **11 task 9**. **World**: a world where **arrivals outpace housing**, which is authorable because arrivals are Command-driven at 11 and needs no autonomous producer. **Quantity**: the Pool's size at steady state. ~~and the two refuting observations are stated — a Pool that grows monotonically over the run means the bound is too long to be a sink, and a Pool that empties while dwellings stand vacant means it is too short to be a search.~~ 🔴 ⚠ **THE FIRST REFUTATION IS WITHDRAWN 2026-08-21 by milestone 11 task 9 — it fires on a correct build** (`plans/0035` **F31**). Measured on `bordered.toml` at **244 Days** with the shipped 120: the Pool grew monotonically for the whole run to **11,653**, still climbing ~50 a Day, *while the bound worked exactly as designed* — mean wait **30 Days**, longest **122**, **4** of 11,646 over it. ***A duration bound produces a size bound only after a settling time of its own order***, and the observation as written carried no settling term. **What the run established instead is what the number MEANS**: the Pool is a queue, so its size is inflow × mean wait and the duration caps the mean wait — **the Pool's ceiling is `inflow × gives_up_after_days`**, which on `bordered.toml` is roughly **forty thousand Households** and takes years of game time to approach. Both shipped files agree: `crowded.toml` at a 2-Day bound predicts ~1,370 and measures **1,458**, settled by Day **4**. ⚠ **This neither refutes nor ratifies 120** — it makes 120 legible, which is what a designer needs in order to argue with it. The **second** observation stands unfired. ⚠ **The committed acceptance test cannot reach this**: it runs on the file whose bound is 2 Days, because that is the only one observable inside a working loop. ***A test sized to watch a mechanism cannot also characterise the number it is tuned by.*** ⚠ **A third observation is owed and is NOT reachable at 11**: whether four months *feels* like a reasonable wait is a judgement about a played city, and `adr/0043` does not reach it. **Trigger to reopen: milestone 16**, when acceptance ships and a second bound on refusals runs first-to-trip beside this one — a Household that refuses fifty dwellings will hit that one first, so this number's meaning narrows on that day |
| **`[[building]] arrivals_per_day`** — a gate's throughput ceiling | **12 Households a Day**, tuning, hash-bearing, and **in one Ruleset only** — `rulesets/bordered.toml`, milestone 11 task 3. ⚠ **MOVED HERE FROM §D2 ON THE DAY A FILE STATED ONE**: it was a *gap* while no kind existed and it is a **debt** now that one does. **It bounds arrivals and nothing else** ([`adr/0088`](../docs/adr/0088-the-price-of-a-far-hinterland-is-paid-in-your-own-traffic.md) amended twice, `plans/0035` decision 9) — the second ceiling, the Access Point's Segment capacity, is So `foot_crossing_every` is **inert at every shipped city and live at exactly one authored configuration**, which is a *third* status rather than a sharper reading of the second — the dial has a working range, no city this project generates sits in it, and the only file that does is a demonstration and says so in its own header. |
| **The three free-flow speeds and the three capacities** | `street_speed_kph = 50`, `arterial_speed_kph = 90`, `walk_speed_kph = 5`; `street_capacity_per_hour = 3600`, `arterial_capacity_per_hour = 12000`, `foot_path_capacity_per_hour = 1000`. **Hash-bearing** — free-flow is `(derived AND rebuilt)` from the Ruleset in force per `adr/0064`, so retuning a speed moves the standing city rather than fitness for this simulation** — nothing yet converts a road speed into a commute anybody notices, so no wrong answer is currently reachable. **The capacities are weaker than the speeds** and should not be read as equally sourced: they are textbook lane throughputs, ~~nothing reads them at all~~, and they are stored as whole Vehicles per Day precisely so that an unbuilt consumer does not get to dictate a representation (`adr/0070`). **Named ratifier: 5c's travel-time matrix** for the speeds — the first thing that produces a commute time a person can call implausible — and ~~milestone 21's `walk_speed_kph`'s ratifier is 5b, not 5c, and the ordering was wrong the day it was written.** A walk Leg's cost is `distance / speed` over the foot subgraph ([`adr/0008`](../docs/adr/0008-walking-is-a-simulated-leg.md), [`adr/0075`](../docs/adr/0075-a-leg-is-a-plan-and-a-traveller-is-a-cursor.md)) and needs **no travel-time matrix, no route cache and no VDF** — which is exactly the property that makes 5b buildable before 5c. So the first thing that produces a walk time a person can call implausible is **milestone 5b**, one milestone earlier than this row says, and it arrives with a Commute Budget to score it against. The two road speeds are unaffected and stay with 5c. *The general shape is worth keeping: three numbers were bundled into one row because they share a source, and they were then given one ratifier because they shared a row — which is `plans/0012`'s **granularity** defect, a status coarser than the claims it covers.* ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-19 — *nothing reads them at all* is false, and the capacities acquired their consumer without this row noticing.** The volume-delay function landed in **5c task 6** ([`adr/0099`](../docs/adr/0099-a-legs-cost-is-a-plan-and-a-drive-is-priced-segment-by-segment-as-it-is-met.md)) and **not** in milestone 21, which is the Lane kernel: `Ruleset.CapacityFor` fills `RoadSegmentTable.CapacityPerDay`, `LoadOf` divides a Segment's live volume by it, and `TripEngine` prices every drive Leg through `LoadAt` — so a capacity is read **on the hot path, on every Tick a Vehicle enters a Segment**, and the clause filing these three as safe-because-inert is spent. **Both speed ratifiers have run as well**: `TravelTimeMatrix` turns `street_speed_kph` and `arterial_speed_kph` into a commute time a person can call implausible, and `walk_speed_kph`'s ran a milestone earlier at **5b**. ⚠ **What running them did not do is ratify a capacity.** A generated city's paved extent scales with the population it serves ([`adr/0090`](../docs/adr/0090-the-generator-makes-land-and-the-player-makes-every-road.md)), so ***the same number sizes both the demand and the supply***, `v/c` peaks at **0.44** at 4,000, 16,000 and 64,000 Citizens alike, and the denominator is never approached at any population this project can generate. The capacities' ratifier is therefore the **under-provisioned `CommandKind.Connect` world** the `[traffic]` row below names — [`adr/0052`](../docs/adr/0052-a-hash-bearing-number-is-chosen-with-a-named-ratifier-or-not-at-all.md)'s 2026-08-15 amendment, *a ratifier names a machine and a world*. ⚠ **And the value column above is no longer the whole set**: `rulesets/congested.toml` states `street_capacity_per_hour = 400`, a ninth of the 3,600 the other four shipped files carry, and says in its own header that it is a demonstration rather than a city — so the figure here is the *majority* value and not the only one. ***A number filed as unratified because nothing consumes it acquires its consumer in silence***, and the only instrument that catches it is somebody opening the symbol — [`adr/0093`](../docs/adr/0093-a-description-of-the-build-is-where-to-look-and-never-what-you-found.md) on a description of the build that was right when it was written and was never re-read against it. |

That is the **sixth** sighting of a dial inert at the shipped configuration, after `foot_crossing_every`, the job-search box, `v/c`, and this milestone's own two. ***A radius and a cap are not redundant dials***: a cap bounds the **work** and binds where supply is dense, a radius bounds the **walk** and binds where it is sparse, and a kept set that never fills never triggers the early exit — so they are refuted by different readings and must not share a ratifier. ⚠ **The figures are deliberately not quoted here.** They are task 3's and unpublished at the time of writing, and a number lifted out of a working session into a ledger arrives with no clause saying what it measures — [`0012`](0012-corpus-audit.md) **Cause 5** committed in the act of recording a finding about it. What binds under scarcity is the **cap**, in the row below. Figures owed to [`0031`](0031-parking.md) task 8; quote its sentence, never these digits. |

| ~~**The commute peak factor**~~ ⚠ **RETIRED 2026-08-15 by [`adr/0101`](../docs/adr/0101-a-commute-is-two-journeys-and-the-days-shape-is-a-property-of-the-job.md), and it is the *third* time a row here has lost a quantity rather than gained a value** — after the Zone Rule's `sample` and `revisit_ticks`' misclassification. The key authored a departure window and the engine derived the morning peak from it; a commute is now **two** journeys anchored on the **job's** Shift start hour, so the peak is a **reading** — what the profile comes out as, given where the jobs are and how long people work. ⚠ **The row's whole history is the argument for retiring it**: its stated refuting number had to be re-derived **twice** (a peak that could not be estimated at the available population, then a dial invisible to an instrument reading a tail), and the reason is now legible — ***a dial that states its own answer cannot be ratified by measuring the answer***. What replaces it is two **bands**, below, whose ratifier is the profile's *shape* rather than any number either band contains. ⚠ **Its last unexamined worry is void rather than answered**: the possible `plans/0012` Cause 5 under the value — S2 R7 measured 2–3× on **in-flight Travellers** while the key was defined on **departure rate** — no longer has a key to be wrong about, and the corpus should not carry it forward as though the new bands inherited it. They are not derived from R7 at all. *Original row follows.* `commute_peak_factor = 3` in all three shipped Rulesets, shipped 2026-08-12 with 5b-bis task 5 ([`adr/0081`](../docs/adr/0081-the-commute-is-the-first-trip-generator-and-a-job-is-taken-by-satisficing-on-distance.md)). **Tuning, hot-reloadable, hash-bearing** — it decides which Tick of the Day each Citizen departs on, so it moves every Trip id the State Hash folds. The **window is derived** from it (`window = ceil(TICKS_PER_DAY / factor)`, `JobRuleset.CommuteWindow`), the factor is not | **It is the closest a §D row has come to not being a free number at all.** Under a uniform departure window of `W` Ticks the instantaneous departure rate is `TICKS_PER_DAY / W` times the daily average, so the **peaking multiplier and the window are one quantity seen from two sides** — and **S2 R7 measured the multiplier** (a morning peak of 2–3× on in-flight Travellers) while nothing in the corpus has ever measured a window. So the Ruleset states the side with evidence and the engine derives the other, which is `adr/0059` a fourth time. **3 implies an eight-hour departure window**, which is wide for a rush hour and is exactly what 3× means: narrowing it without moving the factor is impossible, and moving the factor asserts a peak height nobody has measured. ⚠ **What is genuinely unmeasured is the *shape*** — a uniform window reproduces a stated height exactly and a tapered one would be a curve invented at the write site, so this row is about a height and a second row would be needed for a profile. **No `peak_offset` exists and the absence is deliberate**: nothing in the simulation asks what time of day it is, so *when* within the Day the peak falls is unobservable, and a number with no consequence is `adr/0052`'s prohibition rather than its subject. **Named ratifier: task 8's long run, reporting peak pedestrian density against the Day mean** — which is 5b's own task 4, the measurement the whole re-scope was about, and it refutes this directly: a measured peak that is not ~3× the mean means the factor is wrong. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-13 by 5b-bis task 8, and it is the stated *refuting number* that changes rather than the value.** *A measured peak that is not ~3× the mean means the factor is wrong* **cannot be measured on any city this project can run.** `peak ÷ day-mean` is the product of the structural factor and `max-of-N ÷ in-window mean`, and the second term is 1 only in the limit of large counts — at 1,000 Citizens about **four** people are walking at any instant inside the window, and the largest of 116 samples of a mean-4 count is about **10** by arithmetic that has nothing to do with cities. The first form of the test asserted the stated ratifier and **failed at 10 against 3**. ***A maximum is a statistic of the sample as well as of the thing sampled***, so a peak asserted against a mean is an assertion about the population it was taken at. **The ratifier is now the *quiet fraction***: departures uniform over `TICKS_PER_DAY ÷ factor` Ticks put nobody in flight for `1 − 1 ÷ factor` of the Day — a **proportion over every sample** rather than a maximum over them, so it converges as the run lengthens instead of growing with it. Measured at 4,000 Citizens over 50,000 Ticks: **64% against a predicted 67% at factor 3, and 0% against 0% at factor 1** — two rungs, because one is a fixture and two are a relationship, and the factor-1 control is what makes it a test of `JobRuleset.CommuteWindow` rather than of the shipped file. The peak itself survives as a **one-sided** assertion (`peak ≥ factor × mean`), because every sampling term inflates a measured maximum and none deflates it. **Not refuted, and refutable for the first time.** ⚠ **This is `adr/0043` from a new angle**: the claim was correctly typed *measurable*, a machine did produce the number, and the number still could not settle it — because the named quantity was not estimable at the population available. **Reopens** if any mechanism ever reads the time of day, since that is what would make an offset observable. ⚠ **MEASURED 2026-08-14 over whole Days, and the dial does exactly what this row says while buying almost nothing.** Sweeping the factor at 16,000 Citizens on a 3,600 Vehicle/hour Street, peak Travellers in flight over the second Day's window runs **19 → 121 → 222** at factors 3, 24 and 48 — a **16× narrower window giving a 12× higher in-flight count**, which is the departure-rate multiple and is the row's own arithmetic coming out right. **The busiest Segment moves 4 → 6.** That is this row's *a maximum is a statistic of the sample as well as of the thing sampled* arriving on a **second** quantity: what the factor raises is the **mean**, and a Segment peak at a mean near 0.2 is a Poisson tail. ***A dial that moves a mean is invisible to an instrument reading a tail.*** The earlier ×1.0000 for this dial in §C was an **observation window** — 512 Ticks of a 2,048-Tick Day, sampling a factor-192 burst on Ticks 0–10, before anybody holds a job. ⚠ **And a possible `plans/0012` Cause 5 under the value itself, unexamined**: S2 R7 measured 2–3× on **in-flight Travellers** and this key is defined on **departure rate**, which are the same number only if journey duration is constant across the comparison — and duration is exactly what 5c's advance-loop repair moved by **5.6×** on 2026-08-14. ⚠ **The stated reopening trigger is too narrow, and the user named why on 2026-08-14.** *No mechanism reads the time of day* is what makes an **offset** unobservable; it does not make a **shape** unobservable. A real commute is **bimodal** — out in the morning, back in the evening — and ***a phase is unobservable and a gap between two phases is not***, so a Day with two peaks needs no clock and no offset while this row's uniform window structurally cannot express one. What is missing is therefore the Day's **shape** rather than its concentration, and it is a mechanism (`adr/0070`'s *build X*, and `CommuteEngine`'s own refusal of a return journey) rather than a value of this number. §C's SSC entry carries it as the fourth of four missing demand terms |

shipped Rulesets, shipped 2026-08-15 with [`adr/0101`](../docs/adr/0101-a-commute-is-two-journeys-and-the-days-shape-is-a-property-of-the-job.md). **Tuning, hot-reloadable, hash-bearing** — they decide which Tick of the Day every commute departs on, so they move every Trip id the State Hash folds. **Five numbers replacing one**, and the trade is deliberate: `commute_peak_factor` was one
