**triangular**, and the warrant was not invented: a return is `start + shift`, the sum of two uniform draws, which *is* triangular — so the mechanism had already produced that shape at one end of the Day and the morning was the end that lacked it. A Shift length is drawn in **Ticks** rather than in the whole hours it is authored in, because five whole-hour lengths quantise every return in the city onto five Ticks. ⚠ **The reopening trigger has fired and did not reopen these numbers.** It was *a second Building kind*, and one was built as a test-local variant: the empty middle of the Day **is** land use, it closes with no engine change and no new number, and the finding is a fifth quantity's worth of design rather than a correction — ***a Day's quiet end is bounded by `latest start + longest Shift`***, so a *later* second kind fills the afternoon and wakes the night, and only an **earlier** one adds midday traffic. **Reopens** now on a shipped Ruleset that models a city, which is the first one whose land use is a decision rather than a demonstration |
| **`[households] car_ownership_percent`** — what share of Households keeps a car | **100**, stated by `rulesets/congested.toml` alone since 2026-08-14 ([`adr/0098`](../docs/adr/0098-a-citizen-travels-in-their-households-mode-and-mode-choice-is-undesigned-rather-than-unbuilt.md), 5c task 5). **Hash-bearing**: it decides who drives, which decides what a commute costs, which decides who takes which job. **100 rather than a partial rate** because that file exists to put Vehicles on Segments, and a partial rate would make the demonstration a measurement of the rate instead of of the function. The other three shipped files omit the table, and **the absence means nobody drives, reached by omission rather than by a defaulted key**, because zero is inside the range of legitimate answers and a placeholder there could not announce itself. ⚠ **There is no derivation to find and the search is recorded as having failed** — tau's precedent is that the cheapest way to satisfy `adr/0052` is to find the derivation, and here every candidate is a real-world calibration input rather than a quantity this simulation produces. What the corpus can supply is a **bracket**: US household vehicle availability is about 91% and an urban core is far lower, so the range is wide and the interesting question is which end of it a city-builder wants | ⚠ ~~**5c task 8's long run**~~ **THAT RATIFIER HAS RUN AND COULD NOT FIRE, 2026-08-15**, and it failed for a *weaker* reason than the row below it, which is worth keeping separate. Its stated refuting readings are about **reach** — `jobs beyond budget` never leaving zero, or the three Budget rungs collapsing into *fast* — and a generated city can produce those. What it cannot produce is the thing that makes the rate mean anything: **on a city that never congests, driving has no downside at all**, so any rate is as good as any other and the run is not measuring the quantity it names. ***A ratifier can be defeated by the world without being defeated by the instrument.*** **Named ratifier: the same under-provisioned `CommandKind.Connect` world as the row below** — the first city in which owning a car costs something — with the reach readings kept unchanged, because they are still the two that refute it. ⚠ **That world exists as of 2026-08-15** (`ConnectedCityCongestionTests`), and this row is the one it serves **less** well of the two: the fixture puts a real price on a drive, so a rate is now answerable, but the city it prices is a demonstration with one corridor in it and a rate read off that is a rate for that city. ***A world that makes a number refutable is not thereby a world that makes it right*** ⚠ **THE READING WAS TAKEN 2026-08-15 AND BOTH NAMED REFUTING READINGS FIRE — and they refute the wrong thing, which is a result about this row rather than about the rate.** On the dumbbell ladder at the shipped 3,600 Vehicles an hour over two Days, `jobs beyond budget` is **0** and `moderate` and `unsavoury` are **0** at every rung — 4×4/4,000, 6×6/9,000, 8×8/16,000, 10×10/25,000 — with `fast` running 3,630 / 8,294 / 14,436 / 22,698. **The cause is not the rate.** At 100% ownership every commute is a **drive at 50 km/h across a city at most 4 km wide**, and [`adr/0095`](../docs/adr/0095-a-commute-budget-is-three-rungs-and-only-the-last-one-refuses.md)'s three rungs are percentiles of a **foot-only** distribution — which `CLAUDE.md`'s own Commute Budget row states in as many words, four sentences from where this row's ratifier was written. ***A refuting reading named against one consequence cannot refute a number whose live consequence is a different one.*** This rate is **inert on reach and load-bearing on congestion**, and congestion is what the world was built to expose: at 10×10 the priced and free-flow runs differ by **51.6%** in occupancy while agreeing to the Citizen on who is employed. **So the row does not close, and what is owed is a *different pair of refuting readings*** — ones denominated in what a drive costs rather than in how far it reaches. ⚠ **Do not read the fired readings as a refutation of 100%**: they say the instrument was pointed at the wrong consequence, which is `adr/0052`'s amendment (*a ratifier names a machine and a world*) needing a third clause — ***and a quantity***. Asserted in the direction measured by `ConnectedCityCongestionTests.The_clamp_catches_a_tail_and_does_not_bind_routinely`, so a world that stops collapsing the rungs says so rather than drifting |
| **`[traffic] alpha_percent`, `beta`, `clamp_percent`** — BPR's two parameters and the ceiling on what it reads | **α 15%, β 4, clamp 400%**, stated by `rulesets/congested.toml` alone since 2026-08-14 ([`adr/0099`](../docs/adr/0099-a-legs-cost-is-a-plan-and-a-drive-is-priced-segment-by-segment-as-it-is-met.md), 5c task 6). **Hash-bearing** — they decide what a loaded Segment costs, which decides how long a Trip takes, which moves every downstream count. ⚠ **Sourced, not ratified, and `adr/0052` keeps those apart**: 15% and 4 are BPR's textbook figures and 400% is **recovered arithmetic** — S2 R8.0 published *an arc at the clamp costs 39.4× free-flow* and `1 + 0.15 × 4⁴ = 39.4` exactly, so the clamp is backed out of its own published delay. **S2 is synthetic and ran R4's uniform origin–destination draw, the longest-trip distribution available**, so a figure carried out of it bare is carried out of a different city (`plans/0012` **Cause 5**). **β is bounded by arithmetic rather than by taste** — a clamped ratio of 4 to the eighth is 65,536, exactly Q16.16's whole range — so the loader refuses above 6, and that bound is not a ratification | ⚠ ~~**5c task 8's long run**~~ **THAT RATIFIER HAS RUN AND COULD NOT FIRE, 2026-08-15.** Load came out at **0.0018 / 0.0048 / 0.0110 Vehicles per Segment per Tick** at 4,000 / 16,000 / 64,000 Citizens on `congested.toml`, growing as roughly `P^0.66` — so ~**0.07** at 1,000,000, against a Segment holding **1.02** there and **9.2** at the shipped capacity. **BPR is only ever evaluated where it is nearly flat**, at every population this project can generate. ⚠ **The cause is structural and no length of run touches it**: [`adr/0090`](../docs/adr/0090-the-generator-makes-land-and-the-player-makes-every-road.md) gives the generator land and the player every road, so `CommandKind.Populate` sizes the lattice from the population it serves and ***the same number sizes both the demand and the supply*** — `v/c` peaks at **0.44** at 4,000, 16,000 and 64,000 alike. Congestion is a property of a network's **shape**, a Ruleset can scale a lattice and cannot bend one, and the only producer of shape in this build is `CommandKind.Connect`. **Named ratifier: a long run over a city whose Streets were laid by `CommandKind.Connect` and deliberately under-provisioned** — a machine *and a world*, which is [`adr/0052`](../docs/adr/0052-a-hash-bearing-number-is-chosen-with-a-named-ratifier-or-not-at-all.md)'s 2026-08-15 amendment and the finding this row produced. ⚠ **THAT WORLD NOW EXISTS**: `ConnectedCityCongestionTests`, written 2026-08-15 — a dumbbell of two zoned districts joined by **one** Street corridor, every Segment laid by `Connect`, at the **shipped** 3,600 Vehicles an hour rather than `congested.toml`'s 400, because a reading taken at 400 cannot tell *the player under-built the road* from *the file made the road absurd*. At 4,000 Citizens it peaks at **65.1%** against the generated lattice's **43.4%** on the same Ruleset, and at 16,000 on a district four times the area — same density, same one corridor — the priced world peaks at **1,074%** where its free-flow control peaks at **130%**. **The numbers are refutable now, which is the precondition and not the ratification**: what remains is a reading somebody takes on that world and calls right or wrong. ⚠ **And it produced a finding that outranks the fixture — *the volume-delay function is a loop and not a formula*.** Congestion slows a Vehicle, a slower Vehicle dwells on its Segment longer, longer dwell **is** higher volume, and higher volume is more congestion; past `v/c` = 1 that feeds itself, which is why 130% becomes 1,074% rather than 1,074% being what BPR returns for 130%. The corpus has only ever quoted BPR as a formula. This is `03 §3.2`'s *use it only where it is strong* arriving with a number, and it is the argument for the Microscopic tier from a direction nobody took it from. **The refuting readings are unchanged and stated in both directions** — loaded and free-flow occupancies coming out byte-identical means the parameters are decorative on any city a player will build, and a clamp that binds routinely means the curve has stopped discriminating where the game is played and it is the *clamp* that wants moving rather than α. ⚠ **`congested.toml`'s own numbers are not ratified by a run built to exercise them** — `street_capacity_per_hour = 400` puts **1.02** Vehicles on a Segment where the shipped 3,600 puts 9.2, so it is a rung chosen to make the mechanism visible and not a city. ⚠ **THE READING WAS TAKEN 2026-08-15 AND NEITHER REFUTING READING FIRES, so α, β and the clamp survive their first real world.** The ladder, at the shipped 3,600 over two Days: peak `v/c` **65.1 / 97.7 / 1,074.3 / 2,767.0%** at 4×4, 6×6, 8×8 and 10×10, against clamped shares of the Segment-Ticks that carried anybody of **0.00 / 0.00 / 0.04 / 0.52%**. **The *decorative* refutation fails**: loaded against free-flow occupancy is 6,784/6,784, 19,475/19,472, 44,853/43,703 and **122,725/80,978** — byte-identical at the bottom rung and **+51.6%** at the top, which is the two-ended reading this row asked for. **The *binds routinely* refutation fails by a wide margin**: at 10×10 the peak is **6.9× the clamp** and the clamp still takes **0.52%**, so it is bounding a quartic's tail without touching its body, which is the job it was recovered from S2 R8.0's 39.4× delay to do. ⚠ **Read the clamp share as a steep trend rather than as a level** — 0.00, 0.00, 0.04, 0.52 is ~13× a rung against a 2.6× peak — so what is measured is *not routine here*, never *never routine*, and ***a refuting reading that does not fire is a reading of the world it was taken in***: this world is four rungs of one corridor. ⚠ **And the reading produced something larger than the row.** The loaded and free-flow runs report the **same employed count and the same rung counts, to the Citizen, at every rung**, while their occupancies diverge by half. That is [`adr/0046`](../docs/adr/0046-a-driver-routes-on-habit-sight-and-temperament-never-on-current-cost.md) working as decided — ***congestion is a cost paid and never a cost avoided*** — and it is the 2026-08-15 corpus sweep's *the traffic model has no feedback term* arriving with a number instead of an argument. It is now an **equality assertion** in `ConnectedCityCongestionTests`, so the day a driver model closes `03 §3.4`'s loop the fixture breaks and says which loop closed. **Still formally unratified**: what has happened is that both stated refutations were attempted on the world this row named and neither landed, which is the strongest thing a `adr/0052` row has yet been able to report |

### D2 — Unset. **A gap, not a debt.**

Nothing is built on any of these, because there is nothing to build on. Each names the machinery it
waits for, so that *not yet* stops reading as *neglected*. ~~**Two of the seven are being chosen right
now** and are the ones to watch — they are the test of the rule above.~~ **Both were chosen, on the
same day, and both moved to D1 with a named ratifier — so the rule above has now been tested twice and
held twice.** What is worth keeping from the episode is that *neither number turned out to need a
choice*: tau is `TICKS_PER_DAY ÷ cadence` and the stagger is the Rule's own rate, so both were
**derived** and the argument was about where to look rather than what to pick. `adr/0052` asks for a
named ratifier and does not ask for an arbitrary number, and the cheapest way to satisfy it is to find
the derivation.

> ⚠ **Two more left on 2026-08-15, and the third test of the rule is the one that failed.** The
> `[traffic]` trio and `[households] car_ownership_percent` were *stated* by `rulesets/congested.toml`
> on 2026-08-14 and went on sitting in this table for a day, **each describing itself as a debt in its
> own cell** — so the first lesson is unglamorous: ***a row moves between these tables only when
> somebody moves it***, and a cell that has noticed its own status has not thereby changed table.
> **The second is the one worth carrying.** Their named ratifier — 5c task 8's long run — **ran, and
> could not fire**: a generated city's paved extent scales with the population it serves, so `v/c`
> peaks at 0.44 whatever the population and BPR is only ever evaluated where it is nearly flat. The
> ratifier named an **instrument** and not a **world**. That is
> [`adr/0052`](../docs/adr/0052-a-hash-bearing-number-is-chosen-with-a-named-ratifier-or-not-at-all.md)'s
> 2026-08-15 amendment — ***a named ratifier names a machine and a world*** — and it is a **worse**
> failure than the formality that ADR was written against, because a formality announces itself and
> this one is discharged the moment the instrument runs.

> ⚠ **Four rows in this table wait on one mechanism under four names, and three of them are adjacent.**
> Found 2026-08-15 by the corpus sweep behind [`06`](../docs/06-roadmap.md)'s *driver model* row.
> **Temperament** waits on *the routing model*; **`k`** on *a `k`-shortest path source, which does not
> exist*; the **Rejoin crossing budget** and the **Aggravation threshold** on *the traffic model*. Those
> are one thing — [`adr/0046`](../docs/adr/0046-a-driver-routes-on-habit-sight-and-temperament-never-on-current-cost.md),
> [`adr/0060`](../docs/adr/0060-a-habit-route-is-a-small-set-of-variants-and-which-one-you-take-is-who-you-are.md)
> and [`adr/0061`](../docs/adr/0061-a-diversion-rejoins-by-local-descent-and-a-rejoin-is-never-a-search.md)
> settle it between them, `plans/0026` says in as many words *"No Habit, Sight or Temperament"*, and
> **no milestone holds it**: 21 is the Lane kernel and 22 is Stress. The ratifier cells now say so
> identically, which is the whole repair — ***a shared blocker written four ways reads as four
> independent waits***, and a reader counting what is owed counts four small things where there is one
> large one. **The rows are not merged**: each is a distinct number with distinct evidence, and
> `adr/0059`'s retirement is the precedent for what merging costs. ⚠ **It is the same mechanism that
> closes `03 §3.4`'s loop**, so this is not only a bookkeeping tidy — 5c prices congestion on entry to a
> Segment and routes on free flow, which `adr/0046` refuses by name, and **congestion is therefore a
> cost paid and never a cost avoided**. Four unset numbers and the model's only feedback term are one
> absence.

Same **bundling** error too: `§3.5`'s bullet 3 pairs *deterministic* with *fixed-cost* exactly as `02 §5.7` paired cost with pacing, and the constant delivers the cost claim while silently setting the period that **bullet 1** depends on. ***A constant sample makes the audit's only stated benefit inversely proportional to the size of the city's road network.*** So a Ruleset states a duration and the engine derives `sample = ceil(Segments × interval ÷ coverage_period)` — `adr/0059`'s shape for the **fifth** time. ⚠ **Unlike every previous sighting there is no forced default**, and the reason became statable one Day before this row: [`adr/0101`](../docs/adr/0101-a-commute-is-two-journeys-and-the-days-shape-is-a-property-of-the-job.md) gave the Day a **shape**, so a rotation *"selected deterministically by tick"* whose period is commensurate with `TICKS_PER_DAY` visits every Segment at the **same phase of the Day for ever** and is structurally blind to a junction that fails at peak — which is the whole phenomenon `03 §3.6` describes. **The period must precess against the Day**, a property of the pair rather than of either, refused at the loader. **Named ratifier, in all three parts** ([`adr/0052`](../docs/adr/0052-a-hash-bearing-number-is-chosen-with-a-named-ratifier-or-not-at-all.md) as amended, plus `adr/0098`'s *a machine, a world **and a quantity***): **machine** — `adr/0106`'s reference class, one core; **world** — a `CommandKind.Connect` city containing a low-volume turning failure, because 5c task 8 measured that ***the same number sizes both the demand and the supply*** in a generated one; **quantity** — **detection latency** in Ticks, refuted **upward** against `§3.5`'s own standard that the player must not be the detector, and **downward** on the Cap share taken from stress-promotion |

Neither exists, so nothing is built on either | **NEW 2026-08-15, from [`adr/0104`](../docs/adr/0104-a-skill-tier-is-earned-by-attendance-and-the-credential-stays-a-wall.md).** ⚠ **Only *one* boundary needs a cut**, because `CONTEXT.md` → Skill Tier gives experience 1 → 2 outright and makes 2 → 3 a **wall** — so schooling *influences* both and *gates* only the top. **The second number is a ratio rather than a value** — the file states the schooled rate and the engine derives the other, which is `adr/0059`'s shape and is why this is one row and not two. ⚠ **A threshold on a Need is refused everywhere else and licensed here**, and the licence is in `CONTEXT.md`'s own words: a tier is *"a **category boundary** … because a category is not a quantity"*, where [`adr/0102`](../docs/adr/0102-a-housed-departure-is-a-comparison-the-household-re-runs-not-a-threshold-it-crosses.md) refused a cut over a **continuous** outcome the same day. ***A cut is dishonest over a gradient and honest over a category.*** **Named ratifier: 20's long run**, and the refuting readings are named in both directions — **no** tier-3 adults in a city with working schools means the cut is too high, and the **same** rate in a city with poor schools means it does nothing |

What is unset is the **composition**: how what stands on a Lot weights the land value under it. **Look for the derivation before reaching for a value**, on tau's precedent and `adr/0059`'s — the ADR's whole argument is that the price is already computed by the city, so a weight authored beside it would be a second number that could contradict the first in silence. **Named ratifier: the first play session in which a player clears something**, and the two refuting observations are stated — a price the player cannot predict off the land value overlay is too composed, and a `Demolish` that becomes the primary verb is a price that does not bite. ⚠ **It is the second thing in the corpus blocked on the land value target**, which nothing sets today |
| **The Lane kernel's sub-step ratio** | The Lane kernel, which is `06` **milestone 21** | **NEW 2026-08-12, from [`adr/0082`](../docs/adr/0082-the-behavioural-clock-is-global-and-car-following-sub-steps-inside-it.md)**, and hash-bearing: it decides where a Vehicle is, so it moves the State Hash. **It exists because a Tick is a *behavioural* unit and car-following needs ~45× finer**, and a global fine clock is refused on arithmetic — 371,420 Ticks an in-world Day puts the measured Tick **108× over budget**. **The band is derived and only the point inside it is free**: Treiber's Δt ≤ 0.5 s is the ceiling, a ratio of **21**, and `adr/0019`'s own comfort figure of 12% of following distance is **45**. **Named ratifier: S5**, re-run with the kernel sub-stepping — it already owns the 27.4 ns-per-Vehicle unit this multiplies. **Its storage class is deliberately not decided**, because the Lane kernel is unbuilt and choosing a parameter's disposition for a consumer that does not exist is `adr/0070`'s forbidden move; it is settled where the kernel is built. ⚠ **It is the largest single lever on the Microscopic Cap and the direction is bad**: S5's ~569,000 Vehicles in 15.6 ms becomes **~12,600 at 45 or ~27,100 at 21**, against a demand side that S2 R2 puts near **186,600** — 7–15×, heavily caveated because R2's uniform draw is the longest-trip distribution available. **This is a *second* reason the Cap is unset and it is the one that moves it most**; what would fail if the gap is real is `adr/0007`'s *"it scales"* clause, not the Cap's value. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-13 by [`adr/0094`](../docs/adr/0094-a-day-is-2048-ticks-because-ticks-per-day-is-a-sampling-rate-and-not-a-length-of-life.md): the band is now 84–180.** A Tick spanning 42.19 in-world seconds must integrate four times more sub-steps inside it at the same Δt, and the vehicle population in flight does not move to compensate — `arrival rate × journey duration` is invariant in in-world terms. **The reach-for-the-ratio-before-the-Day instruction survives and `adr/0019`'s reach-for-the-Day one is now backwards.** ⚠ **CORRECTED the same day by [`adr/0096`](../docs/adr/0096-the-microscopic-cap-derives-from-the-design-speeds-budget-and-not-from-the-top-rungs.md): the *7–15×* and *27–58×* gaps in this row are both withdrawn, and this row is where the first one was written.** **186,624 is S2 R2's synthetic *fleet*, not a stressed count** — `plans/0013` says so in the table cell — so it was never the Cap's denominator and the ratio was never a ratio. **And the supply side was quoted at 15.6 ms while the clock moved**, which double-counts, since the budget scales on the same ladder: at the **design speed** the figure is **~25,400 Vehicles on one core**, identical to what the old clock gave at *its* top rung. ***A caveat attached to a number does not travel with it***, and `plans/0013` had predicted this failure in writing, in the paragraph under the table the number was taken from |
| **The Lane kernel at 2 and 4 threads** | **S5**, re-run — it owns the one-core unit | **NEW 2026-08-13, from [`adr/0096`](../docs/adr/0096-the-microscopic-cap-derives-from-the-design-speeds-budget-and-not-from-the-top-rungs.md).** Not a number to choose — a **measurement owed**, and the largest unclaimed multiple on the Microscopic Cap's supply side. **Every figure the Cap has ever been quoted from is one core**, deliberately, because S5 recorded that *"the kernels that will be parallelised are decided by"* a later spike and no later spike has run. The Lane kernel is a plausible scaler: a Lane's pass is over its own queue, `05 §4` lint 4 requires thread-count equivalence anyway, and S5 measured that pass at **17–29× a bare walk**, which is the signature of **compute** rather than of streaming — so `spike-results`' bandwidth curves (1.83× on six desktop cores, 3.75× on twelve M4 Pro threads) are about a different kind of kernel and must not be borrowed. **Two rungs, not a sweep**: 2 and 4 answer the only question here, which is whether a compute-bound queue pass behaves like those curves at all. Near-linear to 4 and the Cap's supply side is ~4× every published figure; flat at 2 and the one-core numbers are final and the fallback tier below stops being a probability. ⚠ **RUN 2026-08-14, and it half-closes — the *flat at 2* branch is refuted and the *near-linear to 4* branch is not yet earned.** Eight captures ([`spike-results`](../docs/spike-results.md) → *S5 L6*): **2 threads is 1.84–1.93×**, stable, so a compute-bound queue pass **does** scale near-linearly and the bandwidth curves this row forbade borrowing were indeed about a different kernel. **The one-core numbers are therefore not final and the fallback tier below Microscopic does not become an obligation on this evidence.** ⚠ **4 threads is bimodal — ~2.52–2.70× or ~3.67–3.87×, nothing between — and is *owed* rather than measured.** The pass needs four pinned cores clear at once, so one busy core sets the wall clock for the whole rung, and the minimum-over-repetitions estimator that exists to remove interference **degrades exactly at the widest rung**. ***The more parallel the thing being measured, the less a busy machine can measure it.*** **The dominant co-tenant was another session in this tree** — `Borough.Tests` at ~1018% CPU against desktop media at 32% — so the re-capture's first control is *nothing else running in this repository*, ahead of the desktop, the governor and the three-in-one-window rule. **Until it runs, the supply-side multiple is *at least 1.84× and plausibly near 4×*, and the 4× must not be quoted bare** — this row is the registry entry for that. **A second result came free**: `05 §4` **lint 4** has its first evidence in this project's life, **0 disagreeing rows of 294,912 at every rung in all eight captures**, holding *by construction* because the Lane pass is wholly Lane-local — ⚠ **not a discharge**, since lint 4 is about `step()` over the whole world and this is its easy case |

So there is **no refresh cadence, no hash-bearing number, and R4.6's break-even does not select an algorithm** (`adr/0046`). **The first row ever struck from this section**, and it had been ratifiable since R8.5 reported — the list was not being read. Re-opens only if `03 §3.4`'s loop is later shown not to close. **⚠ QUALIFIED by S2 R7, and the word *RATIFIED* is provisional until one sentence is written.** R8.5 ran on a **District-granular free-flow tree**, and [`adr/0047`](../docs/adr/0047-routing-never-keys-on-the-district.md) has since **deleted that structure** — using R8's own concentration column as one of the four grounds. R8 states the limit itself: its fire rate *"is a property of District-granular routing and must not be carried to any scheme that gives a Traveller more than one candidate route"*, which is precisely the scheme `adr/0047` chose. **The likely direction is safe and probably strengthening** — more candidate routes means more places for a jam to redistribute to, so self-correction should close at least as easily — **but that is an argument nobody has made, and this row currently rests on *the named ratifier ran and did not refute*, with no statement that the run's structure was subsequently removed.** Under `adr/0052` a ratifier that measured a deleted structure has been cited rather than applied. **Two neighbours inherit the same defect and are not rows here**: R8's 14.08% diversion fire rate, which is the multiplicand behind R6.3's 1,269.51/Tick, and Temperament's 92.28% damping. **⚠ NARROWED by session M, which is a second qualification and not a discharge of the first.** What R8.5 ratified is **static under congestion**: `03 §3.4`'s loop closes on the local layers, so nothing recomputes a Habit *because a road got busy*. It never ratified static under **topology change**, and could not have — R8.5 ran no edits, and a road being built is not a cost signal. `adr/0012` now states the topology half separately and it is **not** static: bounded, checked at use, parameter `T` above. So *RATIFIED* stands for the claim the ratifier actually tested and the **scope is written down instead of assumed** — which is the same defect as the `adr/0047` qualification, found twice on one row by two different routes. ~~**The `adr/0047` qualification is untouched and remains open**~~ **DISCHARGED 2026-08-11 by S2 R7**, which wrote the sentence this row had been waiting for since it was qualified: the direction is **up**. Self-correction works by traffic *leaving* an over-used Segment, more candidate routes is more places for it to go, and variants disperse the population **before** any congestion response, so the loop closes at least as easily on a variant-supplied route set as on the tree. **The one effect running the other way does not reach it** — `adr/0061`'s rejoin can fail, at R6.4.2's 19.14% below the Horizon-3 cliff, but a Traveller whose rejoin fails **has still left the Segment**, so a failed rejoin costs the journey and not the relief. **This discharges the qualification and restores nothing**: the withdrawal above stands on its own ground, and *the loop still closes* was never the claim that was withdrawn. **What R7 found doing it is that the three qualified figures do not share a sign** — see the neighbours row in §B |
| **`T`, the Habit staleness bound** | Trip generation, `06` **5b-bis** | **AMENDED by session D task 4, on cost and on standing rather than on value.** [`adr/0060`](../docs/adr/0060-a-habit-route-is-a-small-set-of-variants-and-which-one-you-take-is-who-you-are.md) made a re-formation compute **`k` candidate routes rather than one**, so `T`'s drain is `k` times more expensive than when the bound was chosen — the two numbers are coupled and were entered separately. And **`T` is now the only recompute left anywhere in the routing model**: everything else adapts by *switching* between candidates that never change (`adr/0061`), so this is the single line on which route computation still happens at all, which raises what the `P(stale)` trigger is watching. No derivation was found and the ratifier is unchanged. **NEW, from session M**, and hash-bearing. *How long after a player builds a bypass the city starts using it*, counted in Ticks but **spent at Trip start** — a Habit older than `T` is recomputed the next time its owner travels. **Named ratifier: the first `06` 5b run that produces a steady-state `P(stale)` and a Trip start rate**, which is what turns `T` from a period into a bill; the value is then bounded by *the wake-missed fraction must clear within a span a player will sit through* and selected inside that band by the first playable build. **Reopens** if `P(stale)` comes back near 1, which would mean the bound is not bounding — `adr/0012`'s **`P(stale)` trigger**, which `d` shares, because session M collapsed the two setters onto one bit and one drain. Note it is **not** a rotation period and R5.5.4's 0.40/Tick does not transfer: that was a 412-entry cache and this is a per-Citizen store |
| **`d`, the addition wake radius** | ~~S2 **R6.4.3**~~ — **THE RATIFIER HAS RUN** | **NEW, from session M**, and hash-bearing — it decides which Citizens learn about a new road promptly, so it moves routes and therefore the hash. **Named ratifier: R6.4.3**, which sweeps `d` and reports wake fan-out against the ground truth of *which routes would actually change*, per R1.7's method. **The refuting number is `P(stale)`, not a fan-out and not a miss rate.** The miss is survivable because `T` backstops it — the whole reason belt-and-braces was chosen — and the fan-out is only *marks*, because session M's second decision is that **the wake sets a bit and does not recompute**: age and proximity set the same bit and both drain at Trip start. So `d` fails exactly where `T` fails, through one shared quantity, and R6.4.3 measures the `d` half of it on the graph alone. **Reopens** if the wake is dropped — in which case the number ceases to exist rather than becoming unratified — or if the reverse index's **per-insert** maintenance cost turns out to exceed what the wake buys. **⚠ R6.4.3 RAN and did not refute — `d` = 0** ("`P(stale)`" peaks at **31.73%** at `d` = 16 Cells and reads **16.89%** at `d` = 0; 2 km of radius nearly doubles fan-out to buy eight points of catch). **But the round found something the ratifier was not designed to see, and it is worth more than the value**: the wake **tests proximity of the wrong object.** Where a *stored* path runs says nothing about whether a new road helps it — the routes that change are those whose *recomputed* path uses the new Segment — so the miss **does not close at any radius** (36 missed at `d` = 0, still 20 at `d` = 16). That is R5.4's addition asymmetry arriving at the wake, and it gives R1.7's 309-of-429 a structural explanation at last. **`d` = 0 is therefore ratified as *the cheapest rung of a mechanism whose ceiling is low*, not as a well-chosen radius**, and the open question it leaves belongs to `adr/0012`: whether a proximity wake is the right instrument at all, given `T` is what actually backstops the miss. Also measured: **`C` is dominated by rounding** — of 202 changed routes only 53 improve by more than 1%, and the wake catches 66.03% of those at `d` = 0 and never reaches 80% |
| ~~**Sight Horizon**~~ **RETIRED by session D task 4 — derived at 1 Segment, there is no value to choose** | — | **The second row ever to leave this section by being *derived away* rather than filled**, after the arming stagger, and the third counting `adr/0059`'s deletion. The floor was already graph geometry; task 4 found the **ceiling is the same number**. A driver *has* its own route and can read `N` live arcs along it; it has **no** route down an alternative beyond the first arc, because nothing searches — so at `N = 1` the comparison is symmetric and above 1 it is not, biasing diversion without bound by an artefact of what the driver holds. **R8 observed the effect and did not name the cause** (*"the horizon stops behaving like a monotone knob"*, with a saturated arc at 39.4× free-flow under BPR `β = 4`). **The other parameter this name was wearing survives as its own row** — the **Rejoin crossing budget**, `adr/0061`, unset. *Original row follows.* ~~Floor **derived at 1 Segment** from the Road Graph (`adr/0046`); the value above the floor is tuning.~~ **⚠ S2 R6.4.2 found this is two parameters wearing one name.** Rejoin success cliffs **19.14% → 85.74% at Horizon 3**, identically on all five O-D rungs, because rejoining a route means going round a block and a block on this graph is three Segments. R8.1's floor of **1** is *noticing a choice*; **3** is *recovering a route you have left*. Nothing in the corpus separates them, and `adr/0046` sets neither |

One `W` per `[[life_stage]]`, forward-only over `[N, N+W)` Days so the authored countdown keeps its meaning as a **floor**, with its own `purpose_tag`. **The contrast with the arming stagger two rows up is the point**: the Tick *within* a Day is unconstrained, so uniform is the only draw that stays spread and there was nothing to pick — **a width in Days is a free parameter**, and that is exactly the difference between a number that owes a ratifier and one that does not. **Named ratifier: the first build in which `[[life_stage]]` exists and a founding generation reaches its first transition at 1M.** The refuting number is **peak armed rows drained on a cascade Tick** — `W` must be wide enough that a founding wave's cascade fits the Tick budget — and the value is then selected inside that band by the first playable build, on slice 10's pacing precedent. **Reopens** if the demographic echo it deliberately preserves turns out to read as a bug rather than as history |

| ~~**The crossing cost**~~ **SET 2026-08-12 at 30 s and moved to D1**, where it is an unratified number in use rather than a gap — 5b-bis task 3 created the `[trips]` table it lives in. *Original row follows.* — what it costs on foot to get to the other side of a Segment | Trip generation, `06` **5b-bis** | **NEW, from session F** ([`adr/0074`](../docs/adr/0074-side-of-street-is-a-property-of-the-access-point-not-of-the-graph.md)), and hash-bearing — it changes a walk Leg's cost, therefore the Commute Budget, therefore a Trip Fate, therefore the city. **It exists because side of street was kept rather than surrendered**: the alternative was two footway edges per Street, which triples the graph and loses one Epoch and one search. The term applies exactly when two **Addresses** share a Segment and differ in side, and is silent elsewhere — so **its blast radius is the across-the-street case and nothing else**, which is unusually small for a hash-bearing number. **Look for the derivation before reaching for a value**, on tau's precedent: a crossing is a real duration — a signal cycle, or a gap in traffic — and the shape of a derivation would be *half a signal period*, which is a property of the junction rather than a preference. **Named ratifier: the first 5b run that reports the walk-Leg cost distribution with the term at zero and at a candidate value**, since what the number has to be is *large enough that a shop opposite is not the same as a shop next door, small enough that it does not dominate a Leg made of blocks*. **Reopens** if rung 3 is ever built — a footway on one side only makes crossing conditional rather than constant, and the term would then have a place to be refused |
| ~~**The Commute Budget**~~ **SET 2026-08-12 at 20 clock minutes and moved to D1**, where it is an unratified number in use rather than a gap — 5b-bis task 4 is what made it choosable, because the distribution it is a percentile of now exists. *Original row follows.* | Trip generation, `06` **5b-bis** | **NEW, from session F**, and hash-bearing — it is the line between a Trip that completes and a Trip whose Fate is *exceeded commute budget*, so it decides which Buildings decline. `CONTEXT.md` → Commute Budget has always described it and no row has ever held it. **Session F settled its *unit* and refused a knob**: it is **clock minutes, one currency across modes, with no per-mode weight**, because a weight would make the scored quantity differ from the displayed one — SC4's unlearnability failure, which the Budget exists to prevent ([`adr/0008`](../docs/adr/0008-walking-is-a-simulated-leg.md)). **That refusal is what keeps this to one number rather than one per mode**, and it is the second time a §D row has been made smaller by an argument rather than larger. **Named ratifier: the first 5b run long enough to produce a Trip-cost distribution**, since the Budget is a percentile of that distribution and is meaningless before one exists; the band is *tight enough that geography bites, loose enough that a working city does not fail its own commutes*, and the value inside it is a feel decision selected by the first playable build, on slice 10's pacing precedent. **Reopens** if `03 §6`'s car-ownership question is ever closed toward a choice, since a Budget that is one number cannot express *I would drive but not walk that far*. **⚠ AMENDED 2026-08-12 by 5b-bis task 3, and the amendment is that the gap is now *stated in a file* rather than merely absent.** `[trips]` exists and `commute_budget_minutes` is its **one optional key**, against `[placement]`'s rule that every key inside a present table is required — because the two numbers in that table are unset in different senses: a crossing cost may be *chosen with a named ratifier*, and a percentile of a distribution cannot be chosen at all before the distribution exists. **An omitted key therefore states a city with no ceiling** — nothing is refused for its length, `TripFate.ExceededCommuteBudget` is reachable and unreached, and `--trips` says which city it is measuring. That is not a default and could not be one: every minute count is a legitimate Budget, so *no* minute count can mean *unset*, which is session F's own finding about placeholders applied to the number that finding was written beside. **It is also the only city whose cost distribution is uncensored**, so this is a precondition of the ratifier rather than a convenience: a Budget in force censors the distribution it is a percentile of, and a value chosen now would be measured back out of a run it had already shaped. ⚠ **A SECOND READING IS IN, 2026-08-14, and it fires one of the two refuting observations this row names.** Over a Day at **256,000 Citizens** — 64× the golden fixture — the Census reads `moderate 0 · unsavoury 0 · beyond ceiling 0`, and the same at 4,000, 16,000 and 64,000. **Every job in a quarter-million-person city is taken on the *fast* rung.** That is *a fast rung never left means the rungs are three names for one state*, in those words. The cause is geometric rather than a mis-set value: the modal commute is **1–2 minutes** at 16,000 and **4–8 minutes** at 256,000, against a fast rung of **20**, because the paved extent scales with `√population` and a generated city is 2.5 km across at 16,000. ⚠ **So the reading refutes the rungs *as authored against this map* and not the three-rung shape**, and the two must not be collapsed: `adr/0095`'s argument is that a single threshold makes a cliff out of `adr/0017`, which nothing here touches. **`adr/0095`'s fourth revisit trigger — reopen the rungs *on the distribution* when 5c lands — is what this discharges into**, and the distribution now exists at four populations |
| ~~**`lots_per_segment`**~~ **SET 2026-08-11 at 5 and moved to D1 on 2026-08-19**, where it is an unratified number in use rather than a gap — 5a-bis created the `[lots]` table it lives in, and this row should have moved on that day: D2 says *nothing is built on any of these*, and `LotSubdivider.Face` has read it ever since. ⚠ **Its named ratifier changed on the move**, because the one stated here is defeated by the build rather than merely unrun — see D1 | — | — |
| **The IDM's tuning parameters** — desired headway, comfortable braking, acceleration exponent | The Lane model, which is Phase 2 | **NEW, from session D task 0.** `adr/0016` says they *"need tuning parameters in the Ruleset, not in code"*, citing `FAST ITERATION` — which settles *where they live* and leaves them unset and unratified. Hash-bearing by construction: they set emergent travel time on every Microscopic Segment. Two of the three are **published constants of a named model** (Treiber, Hennecke & Helbing 2000), so the first question is whether any of them is ours to choose at all, and the answer may retire rows rather than fill them — `adr/0059`'s direction |
| ~~**How the IDM is *spelled***~~ **RETIRED 2026-08-11, the day it was opened, by S5's L5** — and retired rather than filled, which is `adr/0059`'s direction. The row asked whether the IDM's divisions are taken as written or against precomputed reciprocals, because L1 measured **1.63–1.75×** between the two and a reciprocal moves the State Hash. **The premise was false: the speed was never behind the hash.** `IntegerMath.FloorDiv` evaluated its modulo unconditionally, so every `Fixed.Div` was **two** 64-bit divisions; reordering two conditions is **bit-identical by construction** and recovers **1.50×** of it. The approximate form is refused — 1.21× over free, and a measurably different city within 64 Ticks — and the *exact* multiplier-and-shift form is refused too, for the opposite reason: a **dead heat** with the free correction at **+8 bytes a Vehicle**. **No number is chosen and none is owed**, so this is not a §D2 row at all and never was. **The lesson is about how it got here**: it was opened by an argument from a measurement whose instrument had a defect in it, one sitting earlier the same day | — | — |
| ~~**Money's unit**~~ **RETIRED 2026-08-18, the day it was opened, by [`adr/0115`](../docs/adr/0115-moneys-unit-is-fixed-by-the-smallest-fraction-the-design-multiplies-by.md)** — and retired rather than filled, which is `adr/0059`'s direction and the **fourth** row in this section to lose its quantity, after `commute_peak_factor`, the Sight Horizon and the Zone Rule sample. ⚠ **There is no number and no key.** The unit is fixed by the smallest fraction the design multiplies money by, and that fraction is already chosen at **10%** in two places — `02 §5.6`'s `clamp(demand / supply, 0.9, 1.1)`, chosen against UrbanSim's ±25% *"so players see prices drift rather than snap"*, and `02 §4.1`'s percentage apply count, `FloorDiv(readout × percent, 100)` at `RuleEngine.cs:763`. Both **floor**, so a quantity `Q` moves only where `f·Q ≥ 1`: **a price of 9 units is frozen** and the tâtonnement is a permanent no-op on it. ***A design that has chosen its fractions has already chosen its unit.*** ⚠ **`adr/0065`'s *"money's unit is a Ruleset choice"* does not describe a key** — it describes the scale being implied by the money quantities a Ruleset writes down — so nothing is declared, nothing can be authored wrongly, and there is no value for `adr/0052` to want a ratifier for. ⚠ **What survives is a *discipline* and an instrument, not a value**: any money quantity a fraction is ever taken of must be at least ten smallest units, and wants materially more; and milestone 10 task 5 builds the count of percentage applications that **floor to zero**, which names a machine, a world and a quantity where this row could name only a time. ⚠ **The finding that makes it worth an ADR is not the arithmetic**: `FloorDiv(readout × 15, 100)` is **zero** below 7, so under a coarse unit a percentage tax collects nothing from the poorest and the stated rate from everyone else — a **regressive** outcome nobody chose, in the system [`adr/0024`](../docs/adr/0024-money-is-conserved-and-the-city-has-a-balance-of-payments.md) calls *"the most politically loaded mechanic in the design"* and requires the game take no position on. ***An arithmetic artefact that lands on a distributional outcome is a design position taken by accident***, and it is worse than a wrong number, which is at least visible in the file. *Original row follows.* — what one unit of the smallest denomination is worth, relative to a price | `06` **milestone 10** (conserved Money and the treasury), and then **13**, the price surface | **NEW 2026-08-18, from scoping [`0033`](0033-conserved-money-and-the-treasury.md)** — and its age is the finding. [`adr/0050`](../docs/adr/0050-crossing-an-ownership-boundary-is-a-trade-and-payment-is-implicit-in-the-scope.md) declares it owed in its own consequence list — *"**Money's unit must be fine relative to prices.** Prices are integers, so the smallest expressible price is 1 and a coarse unit gives the economy no resolution. This is a sizing decision owed before the first priced Ruleset"* — and [`adr/0065`](../docs/adr/0065-a-bin-holds-a-long-and-unbounded-names-a-ceiling-whose-approach-is-a-defect-rather-than-a-refusal.md) adds that *"money's unit is a Ruleset choice"*, so it is **authored and hot-reloadable** rather than compiled. ⚠ **It has sat in no ledger since `adr/0050` was written**, so it has had no ratifier and no owner for the whole of that time. ⚠ **The only owner either ADR names is a *time*** — *before the first priced Ruleset* — **and a date is not a ratifier** under this document's own amendment: a ratifier names a machine, a world **and a quantity**. Hash-bearing by construction, since it denominates every balance in the save. **Named ratifier owed with the number**, and the natural quantity is the one `adr/0050` itself names as measurable — *"the largest money amount a single trade ever presents to a Bin"* |
| **The founding balance** — what the treasury holds when a *player* starts a city, and whether it is a gift or a loan | ~~`06` **milestone 10**, which is where the treasury Bin first exists~~ ⚠ **NOT milestone 10, as of 2026-08-18** ([`adr/0116`](../docs/adr/0116-the-treasury-opens-empty-and-a-founding-balance-is-a-ratio-this-milestone-holds-neither-side-of.md)) — **the first playable build**, because the number is a **ratio** and milestone 10 holds neither side of it | **NEW 2026-08-18, from scoping [`0033`](0033-conserved-money-and-the-treasury.md)**, and ⚠ **this row is worse than the one above, because the corpus wrote its own ratifier down and no ledger picked it up.** [`01 §3`](../docs/01-player-experience.md) settles the mechanism — *"**the city is founded with money and the player did not ask for it.** Money's only door is the Outside Connection, so a city that exports nothing earns nothing and a founding balance of zero is a game that cannot start"* — leaves open whether it is *"a **gift** or a **loan to be serviced**"*, and then states its own standing verbatim: *"it is world-creation state that enters the treasury Bin, so it is hash-bearing and needs a named ratifier under `adr/0052`; the ratifier is the first real play session and the two refuting observations are the ones just named."* ⚠ ***A number that states its own ratifier inside a design document is still unratified, because this ledger is what schedules the ratification*** — `plans/0012` **Cause 1** in its one-copy form, a fact with no second copy at all. **Playtest**, with the refuting observations `01 §3` already names. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-18 by [`adr/0116`](../docs/adr/0116-the-treasury-opens-empty-and-a-founding-balance-is-a-ratio-this-milestone-holds-neither-side-of.md), and the owner moved rather than the value.** `01 §3`'s two ends — *"enough to get started and not enough to win"* — are **both denominated in what things cost**, and there is no construction cost, no wage, no price surface and no gate. A figure chosen in milestone 10 would be **a numerator with no denominator**, which is `plans/0012` **Cause 5**'s ***a number that is one half of a ratio says which half***, and it would be quoted as *the founding balance* by everything downstream. ⚠ **Milestone 10's treasury opens at zero and that is a DIFFERENT quantity**, derived from what task 5's circuit needs — tax flows in before the transfer pays out, and an empty treasury is what makes `02 §4.2`'s *"pays whom it reaches and reports where it stopped"* branch reachable on the first sweep. **It shares this row's legitimate range**, which is session F's ***a placeholder whose value sits inside the range of legitimate answers cannot announce itself***; ***where two quantities share a range, only their consumer tells them apart***, and milestone 10 has no `Govern`, no construction cost and no player. ✅ **Gift or loan is settled as *free to stay open*, and checked rather than assumed**: a loan is the same balance with `Govern`'s borrow lever pre-pulled, borrowing is already settled as a player action that **adds** money (`adr/0024` as corrected by `adr/0035`), and **a debt is not negative money** — [`adr/0003`](../docs/adr/0003-deterministic-integer-simulation.md): *"the design genuinely never holds negative money… the treasury empties and its Rules wait"* — so a principal never enters the conservation sum and milestone 10's invariant reads identically under either branch. ***An axis left open is free only when both branches need the same representation, and that is checkable rather than assumed.*** |
| **μ, the choice model's sharpness** | `02 §5.4`'s choice model, which is not built | **It is not only a sharpness knob**, and this constrains whatever value is eventually picked: Q16.16 underflows `exp` to exactly zero below −11.09, so in `μ·V` **doubling μ halves the horizon at which options stop existing**, and the two effects compound. A finer table does not move it; only a wider representation would |

### D3 — Not unratified numbers. Moved out, and recorded here so they are not re-added.

These five were inflating the list and none of them is a design constant awaiting a decision.

| Row | What it actually is | Where it belongs |
|---|---|---|
| **Tick↔second and Tile↔metre exchange rates** — 16 Ticks/s, ≈4 m | **A convention with no fact of the matter.** *Ratios are real; units are invented.* There is nothing to ratify: it is free to change and means nothing on its own. `TICKS_PER_DAY` is **not** one of these, and the Cell's 32×32 Tiles is frozen — only its metre reading moves | `CLAUDE.md`'s constants table, as a convention. Not a debt |
| **The S4 measurement configuration** — `performance`, turbo, one core pinned, DIMMs at 2133 because XMP is off | **Provenance on a measurement**, with a named owner and a scheduled fix. Every K0–K6 figure divides by it; a re-sweep with XMP on is owed | Beside the run, in [`spike-results`](../docs/spike-results.md). Rides with R7's re-capture |
| **The slice 7 evaluation-cost capture** — `powersave`, turbo, unpinned | Same. Every absolute is an **upper bound**, every ratio unaffected, and **both conclusions leaning on absolutes lean the safe way** — a faster machine widens the 188,000 and shrinks the 60% | As above |
| **The S0a capture** — `powersave`, governor needed root | Same. The one verdict leaning on an absolute (the State Hash at 2.08 Tick budgets) needs the machine 2.08× faster to move | As above |
| **The 37k–111k in-flight band** | **A defective derivation, not a number** — it conflates duration sensitivity with peaking, so it is wrong rather than unratified. Re-derive on both axes | **Already tracked** in [`0010`](0010-s2-routing.md) §175 and on [`0000`](0000-board.md)'s correction list. It was being carried in two places, one of which called it a number |

## E — Owed work with no slice and no session

Not questions. Nobody has to decide anything; nobody has scheduled them either, and each was named so
it would be scheduled rather than discovered.

- ⚠ **Four things session T left, none of which blocks anything** ([`0027`](0027-session-t-the-target-speed.md),
  2026-08-16). **Re-measure the whole-map Map Layer recompute at 512 Cells** rather than scaling 1.01 ms
  by 16 — the row's ceiling is stale since [`adr/0089`](../docs/adr/0089-the-map-is-sized-by-how-many-commutes-fit-across-it.md)
  and at the target rung a stale ~16 ms exceeds a whole Tick on its own, though no verdict turns on it.
  **Check whether the walk-search row's 464 routes are commute-derived**, and so take `adr/0094`'s ×4
  like the routing row did — ⚠ **deliberately not applied blind**, since applying a factor to a row whose
  provenance nobody checked is how the volume premise expired unnoticed for nine days. **Apply
  [`adr/0106`](../docs/adr/0106-a-wall-clock-budget-names-a-machine-class-and-a-thread-count-or-it-is-not-a-budget.md)
  to `adr/0037`'s 8–15 ms band and `plans/0004`'s ratio tripwires** — routed to [`0012`](0012-corpus-audit.md),
  because applying a new rule to somebody else's argued decision is a correction with an owner. And the
  standing **`performance`, turbo re-capture** of the reference desktop, which tightens every absolute in
  [`0013`](0013-tick-budget.md) in a known direction and moves no ratio.

- ⚠ **`02 §5.4`'s residential choice model — the most leaned-on unbuilt mechanism in the corpus, and it
  was invisible to the one instrument that exists to catch this.** The logit over sampled candidates,
  the stay-put alternative, and the Hinterland as an ordinary row. **Nothing decides anything here** —
  `02 §5` is grilled and the mechanism is specified in detail — what is missing is a **builder**. Four
  decisions rest on it: [`adr/0102`](../docs/adr/0102-a-housed-departure-is-a-comparison-the-household-re-runs-not-a-threshold-it-crosses.md)
  makes a housed Departure *this mechanism firing*, [`adr/0069`](../docs/adr/0069-placement-is-a-mechanism-of-its-own-and-construction-houses-nobody.md)'s
  `candidates` is unratifiable *"until there is a choice model to score with"*,
  [`adr/0032`](../docs/adr/0032-services-are-delivered-by-trips-not-by-coverage.md) has school quality
  entering residential utility **automatically**, and
  [`adr/0026`](../docs/adr/0026-wages-are-posted-locally-and-never-cleared.md) has wages as a competing
  utility term. **It gates milestone 19 outright.** ⚠ **Why nobody noticed until 2026-08-15**:
  `06`'s *Mechanisms with no milestone* has *Settled by an **ADR*** as its entry condition, which names
  the **format** of a specification rather than its **existence**, so a mechanism settled by a **design
  document** is excluded from the very table built to hold unbuilt-but-specified mechanisms. ***A column
  that asks where a decision is written will silently exclude everything decided somewhere else***, and
  it is `adr/0098`'s blind-spot finding on a second axis — that one was about the *undesigned*, this one
  is about the *designed elsewhere*. `06` now carries the row and the second blind spot. **Sequencing is
  session K's**, and this is a better input than K had.
- ⚠ **The suite's cost is one guard left on in nine tests, and the marking is what is still owed.**
  **NEW 2026-08-19, and the reading is taken rather than owed.** A full `dotnet test` is **9m35s over
  1,592 tests**; per-test durations were captured with `-l "console;verbosity=detailed"` and the
  distribution is extreme — **917 tests carry measurable time and 675 run in under a millisecond**, the
  **top 5 are 42%** of summed CPU and the **top 20 are 69%**, and everything under a second put together
  is **1.4%**. ⚠ **Summed CPU is 2,560 s against 576 s of wall clock**, so xUnit is getting ~4.4× out of
  collection parallelism and ***the wall clock is set by the longest single test, not by the sum*** —
  trimming the 735 fast tests would return nothing at all.
  ⚠ **The folklore was that the long acceptance runs are the cost, and it is wrong.** Every
  100,000-Tick run in the suite is **3–29 s** — Layer 14, Evidence 14, Traffic 28, Placement 4, Zone
  Rule 4, Road 4, Commute 5 — **except one**. `SaveLongRunTests.The_hundred_thousand_Tick_save_run` is
  **6m35s, 69% of the entire wall clock on its own**, and the second and third places are not long runs
  at all: `JobSearchBoxTests` two tests at **327 s and 191 s**, 20% of summed CPU.
  ⚠ **The cause is measured and it is not the saving.** That test's own instrumentation reports **1.55
  ms mean copy and 0.26 ms mean write over 49 saves — 0.09 s of 395 s, 0.02%**. What costs is the
  100,352 `Step` calls, and the reason this run is 15× its siblings is that **every other long-run test
  sets `VerifyDecideWritesNothing = false` and this one does not** — the `O(world)`-twice-per-Tick guard
  S0a found at 76.4 ms a Tick and 95% of a run, which is why `--no-decide-guard` exists at all.
  **Verified by measurement rather than argued: switching it off takes the test 395 s → 26 s, a 15.2×,
  with byte-identical per-Day file sizes and Building counts.** ⚠ **Nine of the top ten are the same
  shape** — `JobSearchBoxTests`, `EmploymentRungTests`, `CarRouteLengthTests`, `VolumeDelayReachTests`,
  `StatisticalTravelTimeTests`, `RouteCacheTests`, `GoldenSessionCoverageTests`, `ReplayTests` and
  `JobAssignmentTests` all step many Ticks with the guard on and none of them is testing the guard.
  ***A guard whose cost is documented in one place and whose call sites are opted out of one at a time
  is a default nobody chose.***
  ⚠ **What is owed is the decision and then the marking, in that order.** The decision is whether the
  default should invert — the guard is `adr/0037`'s read-only-Decide proof and `RuleEvaluationTests`
  already asserts it defaults on, so leaving it on in a *correctness* test and off in a *volume* test is
  the distinction to write down rather than to apply case by case. **Then mark the expensive tier** with
  an xUnit trait so it can be named and excluded on demand, which is `02 §10`'s own *sort by frequency,
  never by build configuration* applied to the suite rather than to the invariants. ⚠ **Marking first
  would have been the wrong order**: a trait applied to whatever happened to be slow would have recorded
  `SaveLongRunTests` as inherently expensive, when 94% of it is a guard nobody meant to leave on.
  ⚠ **And this is not only a convenience cost** — S5's threading re-capture came back bimodal because
  another session's `dotnet test` was running at ~1018% CPU ([`spike-results`](../docs/spike-results.md)
  → *S5 L6*), so the suite's footprint is already a measurement hazard on the board's *the three tracks
  do not contend*.
  ✅ **APPLIED 2026-08-19, and the guard fix found something bigger than the time it saved.** Nine of
  the ten opted out at the site that steps — **9m35s → 4m03s wall, summed CPU 2,560 s → 1,297 s**, 1,592
  green — with `SaveLongRunTests.The_hundred_thousand_Tick_save_run` going **6m35s → 21 s**. ⚠ **The
  tenth was left on deliberately**: `ReplayTests` reaches the world through `Replay.Run`, which builds
  its own `Simulation`, so opting it out means a parameter on a **production** API for a test's speed —
  and at **54 s against a 192 s longest test** it would return **nothing in wall clock**, because the
  wall is now set by one test rather than by the sum. ***A cost worth removing from a sum is not thereby
  a cost worth removing from a maximum.***
  ⚠ **The finding: the guard was sitting in the denominator of a published ratio, and it was 94% of
  it.** `JobSearchBoxTests.The_box_is_not_where_the_pass_spends_its_time` compares a narrow box against
  a wide one and reported **1.06×** for a 5.34× widening — the number `CLAUDE.md`'s Commute Budget row
  cites as the third ground for **not repairing the box**. The guard is an `O(world)` fold twice a Tick,
  **identical in both arms**, and each arm was ~31 s of which ~29 s was the guard. Measured both ways on
  one machine within the minute: guard on **30,938 / 34,320 ms → 1.11×**, guard off **2,248 / 4,129 ms →
  1.84×**, five runs spanning **1.73–1.84**. Solving `(1 − f) + 5.34f` puts box walking at ~**18%** of
  the pass where 1.06× implied **1.4%**. ***A common term that does not move still moves a ratio, by
  diluting it*** — `plans/0012` **Cause 5**'s form for a *measurement* rather than for a quotation, and
  the sibling of the confound that test's own doc-comment warns about, which is a term that **moves**.
  **The harder of the two to see**, because the author watching for a moving term has already thought
  about confounds. The test's bound is re-derived from the spread (1.5 → **2.2**), `CLAUDE.md` carries
  the correction, and the other two grounds for not repairing the box stand — [`adr/0088`](../docs/adr/0088-the-price-of-a-far-hinterland-is-paid-in-your-own-traffic.md):
  a decision given several grounds is load-bearing on whichever survive.
  ⚠ **Still owed: the marking, and one thing the guard fix exposed.** The expensive tier wants an xUnit
  trait so it can be named and excluded, which is now a smaller and better-informed job. And **nothing
  in the suite watches the guard fire** — `RuleEvaluationTests.Deciding_writes_nothing_even_on_a_tick_where_rules_fire`
  watches it **pass**, non-vacuously, which is what licensed the opt-outs, but a guard nobody has seen
  reject is `adr/0064`'s *a guard with no test* one step removed. It is not testable without a seam: the
  thing that would have to write in Phase 2 is `RuleEngine.Evaluate`, and there is no way to substitute
  a writing one. Recorded rather than built.
- **Generation with playability guarantees.** A seed producing no buildable land or no water access is
  a broken map, not a hard one (`adr/0021`).
- **Dial playability floors.** A Hinterland at minimum depth and minimum recovery produces no
  immigration at all, which breaks `01 §3`'s opening outright. Every Mode is a hand-validated point;
  the free sliders need floors.
- **Hazard Region derivation.** Floodplain and Woodland hazard maps are computed at world generation
  and never read during a Tick, so they belong to the generator's output alongside terrain — not to the
  Ruleset.
- **Hysteresis on the extraction frontier.** Forestry Buildings declining and regrowing will flicker
  without it, same as Segment Stress.
- **Evidence for composed fertility.** A farm's panel must decompose its own yield by source, or the
  whole `adr/0022` mechanic is inexplicable.
- ~~**`adr/0033`'s satisfiability invariant** — *no Rule is asleep with all inputs satisfiable*,
  specified in `adr/0033`, `02 §10` and [`0008`](0008-tick-and-replay.md), and built in none.~~
  **DONE 2026-08-10**, as `Invariant.WaiterIsBlockedByTheBinItNames` in the end-of-run tier, and it
  **found a live defect in the committed golden baseline within minutes of being registered** — which
  reversed the sequencing twice and made it one commit with
  [`adr/0063`](../docs/adr/0063-a-wait-list-wakes-on-the-bins-state-and-a-shortfall-is-derived-rather-than-stored.md)'s
  wake predicate rather than the free hash-neutral item it was filed as. It is deliberately **narrower**
  than `adr/0033`'s wording in what it inspects and **stronger** in what it catches: *asleep on a Bin
  that has stopped blocking it* also catches a waiter resubscribed to the wrong Bin, which *would this
  Rule fire* is blind to. **The general lesson belongs to this section rather than to the ADR: an
  obligation specified in three documents and built in none is how `HouseholdHomeExists` came to be
  reported by nothing, and this one paid for itself the day it existed.**
- ~~**S0b — the Tick with work in it.** Blocked on slices 7, 9 and 10~~ **DONE**, three clauses of four.
  **8.72 ms a Tick at 1M — 55.9% of a 15.6 ms budget at 4×**, and it **split the way S0 did**: the
  routing load, pollution decay and `05 §5` role 3 are all unreachable for want of **content** rather
  than machinery, and all three wait on **task 10b**. Its largest finding is not the price — it is that
  a Zone Rule's `sample` is **absolute** where the thing it paces is relative to the size of the city,
  which `adr/0059` has since settled. Numbers in [`spike-results`](../docs/spike-results.md) → *S0b*.
- ~~**Price Phase 3, and price Map Layer diffusion.**~~ **DONE**, and both moved something — the engine
  row went from an *inferred* 60% to a **measured 67%** at 4× (since superseded by an in-situ 41%), and
  **Map Layer diffusion turned out to be the only row in [`0013`](0013-tick-budget.md) with no guess in
  it**. Full body in the archive below → *Closed entries*.

---

## F — Coverage: what has been grilled, and what has not

**Promoted out of the archive on 2026-08-10 and rebuilt**, discharging
[`0012`](0012-corpus-audit.md)'s instruction — *"promote it out of the archive and into the ledger
once it is corrected"*. It had been sitting below the archive banner, which declares its contents
*history and not maintained*, while being **the only per-document account of what has been examined**
and while [`adr/0043`](../docs/adr/0043-a-claim-a-measurement-could-settle-must-not-be-settled-by-argument.md)
cited its 🟢 rows as evidence. **A self-contradicting map filed as history cannot carry that.** The
version it replaces is kept below the archive line, bannered.

**Three defects fixed in the rebuild**, all named by `0012` or by `adr/0043`:

1. **It contradicted itself.** `05 §5` was listed as closed in one sentence and unargued in the next;
   `02 §8` and `§10` were listed as never grilled when session eight closed them.
2. **It stopped at `adr/0043`.** **Twenty-two ADRs — `0038` through `0059` — did not appear at all**,
   which is every decision the *building* has produced. A coverage map blind to two thirds of a year's
   output is worse than none, because it reads as complete.
3. **Its blanket rows were the defect `adr/0043` names.** `adr/0010`–`0022` was thirteen ADRs under one
   mark from two sittings, **two of them since measured false**. Those rows are now **split**, because
   *a status whose granularity is coarser than the claims it covers cannot be checked.*

> **⚠ Read this map for one thing above all.** A 🟢 mark says *a session argued this decision*. It does
> **not** say every sentence supporting the decision was read — that is exactly how `adr/0014`'s
> pre-partition claim and `adr/0020`'s union-find claim passed through 🟢 rows and were later measured
> false. **Nothing in this corpus has ever been typed *arguable* or *measurable* per `adr/0043`**, and
> until that audit runs, ⚫ is a lower bound rather than a count.

**Legend:** 🔴 never grilled · 🟡 partially · 🟢 substantially settled · ⚫ **a supporting claim has been
measured false** (the decision may still stand) · ⚠ **reads decided and has no number** — `adr/0043`'s
named suspects, and the first place to look.

### F2 — ADRs

**144 written, numbered to `0145`; `0028` is reserved and unwritten.** Grouped by what grilled them.

✅ ~~**`0113`–`0118` are missing from this branch and are not free** — they are claimed by the
unmerged `milestone-10-conserved-money` worktree and arrive at merge.~~ **They arrived 2026-08-19,
when that branch merged `main` in**, and the column is contiguous again. The note is struck rather
than deleted because the standing half is what it was written for: a **gap in this column is not
evidence of a missing decision** while more than one milestone is in flight, and the sibling
row-completeness check below cannot tell the two apart. `plans/0000` named this hazard for **plan**
numbers the same week — ⚠ **and that one bit on the same merge**: both branches had scoped into
`plans/0031`, parking first by sixteen hours, so conserved money renumbered to **`0033`** under
`PROCESS.md` → *Numbering*'s never-re-use rule. ***The ADR collision was caught by a filename check
and the plan collision was caught by `ls`***, which is the same fact arriving at two different
instruments.

> ⚠ **These two were renumbered on 2026-08-19, and the claim that failed is worth more than the
> renumber.** Milestone 7's opening sitting took `0112`/`0113` on 2026-08-18 by reading the claimed
> filenames off `git log --all`, which was described as *the only instrument that sees them*. It is
> not: `git log --all` sees **refs**, and the other two branches went on committing afterwards —
> `main` reached `0112` and milestone 10 reached `0118` — so both numbers collided within the day.
> ***A number claimed by reading what other branches have already written is claimed against a set
> that is still growing***, which is the same shape as the plan-number hazard `plans/0000` names and
> not a weaker version of it. What a reservation would need is somewhere all three branches can
> write, and there is no such place.
>
> ⚠ **The check that would catch this must compare *filenames*, not numbers and not content**, and
> the naive versions are both wrong in the same direction. Measured across the three live branches:
> comparing by **number** reports 112 collisions of which 110 are false, because a shared ancestor's
> ADR appears on every branch that descends from it; comparing by **content** reports 22 of which 20
> are false, because *a superseded document gets a banner rather than a deletion*, so an ADR's text is
> expected to move for the whole of its life and every amendment reads as a collision. Comparing by
> **filename per number** reports **2**, both real. The reason it works is a convention this corpus
> already has: **an ADR's filename is the claim**, so the filename is the identity. ***A check whose
> false-positive rate is 91% is worse than no check, because it is the version that gets built and
> then stopped being trusted.*** Filename comparison also needs **no ancestry test**, which is the
> property worth having — a merge-base filter reaches the same two answers here but can only compare
> branches that have already met, and the case the check exists for is two branches that have not.
> Found by hand with `git ls-tree` across worktrees, which is the only instrument that saw it at all;
> measured by `milestone-8-save-load` and recorded in [`0012`](0012-corpus-audit.md).

> **⚠ This count had drifted a fourth time, and it is the half of the map `CoverageMapTests` was not
> holding.** It read *"83 written, numbered to `0084`"* on 2026-08-14 against **96 files numbered to
> `0097`** — thirteen decisions, `0085`–`0097`, including `adr/0089`, `0094`, `0095` and `0096`. **Every
> one of them has a row**: mechanical check 5 has been green throughout and did exactly what it was
> built to do. What it does not check is the **sentence above the table**, so the rows stayed complete
> while the count over them went stale — and a reader who trusts the count concludes the map stops at
> `0084` and that everything after it is unassessed. ***A count of the instruments is itself a fact
> stored in prose*** ([`adr/0093`](../docs/adr/0093-a-description-of-the-build-is-where-to-look-and-never-what-you-found.md)),
> and this is that rule turned on the guard's own header rather than on a guard's name.
>
> **It is now the check's second fact rather than a fourth correction.** The trigger written into that
> test's remarks — *"if it stops at some number a third time, the map wants generating from the
> directory rather than writing"* — was discharged for the rows and left standing for the count.

| `0097` a reach failure is counted on the Citizen, and a stock failure is not remembered at all | 🟢 | **A sitting, 2026-08-13**, run beside `plans/0003` queue item 6, on the rule `adr/0047` filed and `adr/0017` recorded and neither settled. **The debt was one sentence covering two failures** — an option fails because it is **full** or because it is **unreachable** — and `adr/0067` had settled the stock half on **2026-08-10, three days after the debt was filed**, with a cursor that nothing connected to it. ***An answer that arrives after a debt is filed does not find its way to the ledger*** (`plans/0012` **Cause 2**, running in the direction the sweep does not look). **The tell that the debt always meant the other half is its own candidate list**, which offers a demotion, a cooldown and Habit's weight and **not a cursor**. ⚠ **`adr/0047`'s loop is not reachable in the build**: `EmploymentEngine` keys its candidate draw on the **Tick**, so every occasion draws different Buildings — the defect is live as a **recurring search bill** (~32.5 µs a Dijkstra × ~5,400 of 10,000 Citizens, for ever) rather than as a loop, and the loop needs *deterministic* re-evaluation, which is **5c**. **The split is already in the code**: `!HasJob` costs one array read and is not counted; beyond-budget costs a full search and is. *A memory buys a skipped search*, so it attaches to the expensive failure and to no other. ⚠ **`02 §9` was already violated** — *every aggregate figure must be able to name its constituents*, and `jobs beyond budget` had no entity reference behind it. A cooldown is refused on **shape** (*a shelf refills on its own; a road does not move on its own*), a wider box on **geometry** (the box already over-approximates the Budget, so widening returns only candidates that will be refused), and Habit's weight as a **category error**. | `0098` a Citizen travels in their Household's mode, and mode choice is undesigned rather than unbuilt | 🟢 | **Milestone 5c task 5, 2026-08-14.** A `[households] car_ownership_percent` decides who keeps a car; a Citizen of a Household that does drives everywhere and one of a Household that does not walks everywhere. **Nobody weighs a walk against a drive on the day, and nothing in this corpus specifies how they would.** ⚠ **The finding is about `06`'s inventory rather than about modes**: mode choice is absent from every milestone row *and* from the ***Mechanisms with no milestone*** list, whose own opening line is *"every row below is settled by an ADR"* — so it is `adr/0070`'s **undesigned** class, and ***an inventory of unplaced mechanisms structurally cannot list a mechanism nobody designed***. **Fourth consecutive milestone** to find a precondition it had not finished counting. The design answers it one level up: `01 §8` ledger #2 already settled that ownership is a **persistent Household state** and names *every Household owning a car* as the simple assumption until transit exists, so its **exogenous half is now built** and the endogenous half stays open on `01 §8`'s own terms. **Ownership is derived from the Ruleset, never stored** (`adr/0068`, `adr/0064`), and what that buys beyond avoiding a frozen bit is that the owner set is **nested** — both saved alternatives fail in *opposite* directions. `EmploymentEngine` judges a candidate in the seeker's own mode, which is `adr/0008`'s single clock read in the mode the journey is made in. ⚠ **No State Hash moved**, because neither shipped Ruleset states the table — a safety net, not a result. **The car route-length distribution owed since task 4 is measured and not fitted**: median 1 / 4 / 6 / 8 Segments at 1,000 / 4,000 / 8,000 / 16,000 Citizens, and ⚠ **the car cap is the *map* rather than the Budget**, since 50 minutes at 50 km/h reaches 41.7 km. ⚠ **A drive and a walk take the same Streets on this fixture** because `arterial_count = 0`, so ***a comparison run on a fixture that lacks the mechanism under comparison measures the fixture*** |
| `0099` a Leg's cost is a plan, and a drive is priced Segment by Segment as it is met | 🟢 | **Milestone 5c task 6, 2026-08-14.** The volume-delay function, and the machinery `adr/0041` has been waiting on since 5a. A vehicular Leg stores its route; a Traveller holds a cursor onto it; each Segment's dwell is priced by BPR **on entry** from that Segment's volume. **Narrows `adr/0075`** (*a cost and no path* — a walk Leg still stores none, so the economy survives where it was argued) and **`CONTEXT.md` → Traveller** (what a Statistical Traveller still lacks is a position *within* a Segment, which is the whole distinction from Microscopic). ⚠ **The route lives on the Leg and not in `adr/0060`'s route cache**, because a cache evicts and an entry vanishing under a moving vehicle strands it — ***a shared cache is an optimisation and an executing plan is state***. ⚠ **`adr/0041`'s own premise had expired and nothing noticed**: *a vehicle crosses about one Segment per Tick*, stated as following from `TICKS_PER_DAY = 8192`, is **~4.6** under `adr/0094`'s 2048-Tick Day — so the first cut divided a stock by a flow, the function came back **inert at every population** (×1.0000 from 4,000 to 160,000 Citizens), and only a measurement said so. ***A premise licensing one quantity to stand in for another is itself a measurement, and a constant moved in another document can retire it silently.*** The fix is **Little's Law** and introduces no number: a Segment at capacity holds `capacity/Tick × crossing time` = **9.2** Vehicles on a 128 m block, a 14 m spacing, where 42 was a 3 m one. **A second defect on the same axis**: flooring capacity to whole Vehicles/Tick makes anything under 2,048 a Day floor to zero and hit the guard written for an *absent* capacity — ×2.48 at 200 Vehicles/hour and ×1.0000 at 60, which is not a curve any function has. ***A guard written for an absent quantity will fire on a small one.*** ⚠ **The finding that outranks the mechanism: a generated city cannot congest itself** — `v/c` **0.44** at 4,000, 16,000 and 64,000 Citizens alike, because the paved extent scales with √population and ***the same number sizes both the demand and the supply***. Congestion is something a **player** makes (`adr/0090`), and both sides are asserted. Opens **three `adr/0052` numbers** (§D2), all sourced from S2 and unratified |
| `0105` the target speed is 4× at a million Citizens, and a rung dilates rather than being withdrawn | 🟢 | **Session T, 2026-08-16, with the user in the room** ([`0027`](0027-session-t-the-target-speed.md)).
