The two-point form the session recommended — full ladder at 10k, 1× only at 1M — was refused: ***taking options away while a city progresses is weird***, and the `Wait` step is hardest to close in exactly the city that would have lost its fast-forward. **Withdrawal and dilation are different things and only the first is refused.** ⚠ **It fired `adr/0096`'s revisit trigger and that decision survived on one of its two legs** — a Cap and a bill are different objects, so the Microscopic Cap stays at 62.5 ms. ⚠ **The target is ~3× away from the bill** and that is recorded rather than hidden; the only named lever that size is threading, which is session **R**'s |

Bridges are *"a buildability exception plus a rendering variant, **not a system**"*. ***So height's entire net effect on a 24-era city is that some Lots decline to build*** — and `adr/0021` refuses that configuration by name: ***without terraforming, terrain is a wall; with it, a price.*** ***A mechanism whose only built half is the refusal is not a partial delivery of the design; it is the alternative the design refused.*** ⚠ **Terraforming cannot fix it because it does not exist as a verb** — see the open question below. ⚠ **The memory figures corroborate and are DELIBERATELY not the reason** (~512 MiB per-Tile against 86 MiB for a whole 1M world; 8.6% of the map paved): ***deferring for a cost you have not been asked to pay is deferring for the wrong reason***, [`adr/0100`](../docs/adr/0100-moving-the-state-hash-costs-nothing-until-somebody-is-carrying-a-save.md)'s discipline generalised. ✅ **Keeps [`adr/0111`](../docs/adr/0111-a-save-that-re-derives-nothing-needs-neither-a-seed-nor-a-generator-version.md) intact** — height consumed once and discarded regenerates nothing on load. **Opens no `adr/0052` number** |
| `0141` Fertility composes with weights, and only one of them is a number anybody chooses | 🟢 | **`plans/0038` decision 1b, 2026-08-22, with the user in the room.** ✅ **Weights, following `MapLayers.Desirability`** — a tested multiplication reused rather than a second one invented. **Base Fertility is a fraction with `1.0` fully fertile**, so Fertility is a proportion. 🔴 ⚠ **`w_s` is DERIVED and gets no key**: a Cell at Sealing 1024 has every Tile built on and therefore no farmland, an **endpoint rather than a preference**, pinning the term at `base × Sealing / 1024` against a `1024` that is already `CellGrid.TilesInCell`. ***A coefficient with an endpoint is not a tuning knob, and offering it as one invites a Ruleset to state that a fully paved Cell still farms*** — [`adr/0059`](../docs/adr/0059-a-zone-rules-sample-is-a-revisit-period-so-the-ruleset-states-a-duration.md)'s shape, and it takes the decision from **two** unratified numbers to **one**. ⚠ **The bare subtraction was never an implementation**: pollution is *"about 12 in kernel units under a strong source"* against a Sealing count of 0–1024, so unweighted the ratio is **85:1 by representation**. ✅ ***The scale was decided by the readout rather than by the storage*** — [`adr/0022`](../docs/adr/0022-land-is-a-stock-the-city-spends.md)'s *"41% — ground sealed 12%"* specimen falls out with no conversion and no denominator to name. ✅ **Negative is kept and does not clamp**, ⚠ **and the decomposition is not the reason** — Evidence reads the terms; what a clamp destroys is the **ordering between exhausted Cells**, which is what the cyclical land-use arc runs on since Sealing decays. **Opens one §D1 row** |
| `0140` Base Fertility is Ruleset data keyed by terrain type, and the old name invented a field | 🟢 | **`plans/0038` decision 1, 2026-08-22, with the user in the room**, and the question that cracked it was *suitable for what?* — **for farming, and nothing else.** All six occurrences of `terrain suitability` sit inside the Fertility formula, so the quantity is **Fertility's ceiling** and not terrain's contribution to buildability or land value, which are height and water. ✅ **Renamed Base Fertility; it is Ruleset data keyed by terrain type; the stored per-Cell column is the TYPE**, with two values keyed off it — Base Fertility and the Sealing decay rate `CONTEXT.md` has specified that way since it was written. 🔴 ⚠ **[`adr/0124`](../docs/adr/0124-terrain-suitability-is-baked-at-world-creation-and-the-layer-holes-that-need-it-move-to-milestone-24.md)'s baked column is SUPERSEDED and the NAME is what produced it** — read *terrain* and a terrain-derived field is the natural inference, while `02 §2.3` refuses it in one sentence and `adr/0022`, which that ADR cites throughout, argues against it at length. ***A badly named term is a design defect waiting for somebody to reason from the name***, and `CONTEXT.md` had **no entry for it or for Terrain** to reason from instead; it now has both. ⚠ **`adr/0124` sensed a real hole and named the wrong occupant** — the column is owed, for the decay rate. ⚠ **Baking it was also what `adr/0022` forbids for the value beside it**: *"never stored per Tile … storing a rate as state would freeze it into every save."* ***Two values keyed by one column and only one had been thought about as tuning.*** ⚠ **Varying Base Fertility costs one Ruleset key and no storage, and AMENDS `adr/0022` rather than tuning within it** — the shipped demonstration states it uniform. **Opens two [`adr/0052`](../docs/adr/0052-a-hash-bearing-number-is-chosen-with-a-named-ratifier-or-not-at-all.md) numbers**, both owed at milestone 24 against a world with varied terrain, and **leaves the arithmetic open as decision 1b** |
| `0139` milestone 24 is two milestones, because a dial cannot scale a figure nothing authors | 🟢 | **`plans/0038`'s scoping, 2026-08-22, with the user in the room.** It is [`adr/0124`](../docs/adr/0124-terrain-suitability-is-baked-at-world-creation-and-the-layer-holes-that-need-it-move-to-milestone-24.md)'s own revisit trigger firing, ⚠ **on the half that ADR did not expect to be separable** — it offered a *land systems on terrain* seam and the seam was the **dial**. | `0130` the Pool's bound is a duration, and the unhoused channel ships with the gate | 🟢 | **Milestone 11 decisions 3 and 3a, 2026-08-20**, with the user in the room. **The unhoused Departure ships with the gate** — `CONTEXT.md` → Unplaced Pool says *"whoever builds the gate owes the give-up rule in the same milestone"*, and it is right for a reason `UnplacedTable`'s doc-comment could not have known when it routed the counter to *"milestone 9a"*: **`adr/0006` was satisfied because nothing created a Household after world creation**, and the gate removes the reason. ***A minimal list stays minimal until something needs more, and an inflow is something needing more*** — and `adr/0054` named immigration as what it was deferring, so this is its own trigger firing rather than a reversal of it. **Only the unhoused channel comes** — only the *housed* one is a comparison (`adr/0102`), so only it goes to 16 — and it has **exactly one reason**, because `PlacementEngine` is blind and the sole failure is *no room*, which is the **capacity** diagnosis that channel carries. ***A single reason is honest when the mechanism admits one.*** ✅ **The bound is a DURATION**, `adr/0059` one level down: a count of occasions makes the felt waiting time `N × revisit_ticks`, so retuning `[placement]` would silently change how long families look. 🔴 ⚠ **And a count of dwellings *considered* cannot fire in a city with no vacancies at all** — nothing offered, nothing considered, counter never advances — ***which is the exact failure the channel exists to diagnose; a bound that cannot trip in its own headline case is not a bound.*** **The count is recorded as Evidence** — `00-vision.md`'s *"Considered 20 dwellings over 4 months"* is two numbers, one bounding and one describing. ⚠ **A second bound on refusals is owed at 16 and must not be authored now**: nothing refuses anything until acceptance exists, so it would be inert, and that is `w₃` exactly. **Opens one `adr/0052` number** |
| `0129` the Pool waits at the gate, and an arrival's Trip is the move-in | 🟢 | **Milestone 11 decision 2, 2026-08-20**, with the user in the room. `adr/0023` says arrivals *"arrive as Trips originating at an Outside Connection, enter the Unplaced Pool, and house themselves"* — **and `TripTable.Start` requires an origin AND a destination Address**, which an unplaced Household does not have, because the Pool is what will give it one. ***A journey described in prose can name an endpoint the mechanism has to compute.*** So the Household **arrives at a named gate as an entry event, waits in the Pool there, and the Trip is the move-in** — gate → dwelling — which keeps every property `adr/0023` was buying: located arrival, throughput binding on entry, real congestion, and a Departure that leaves through a gate. ✅ **The gate is a column on the Pool membership and the placement is forced**: it is known at arrival because throughput binds there, needed again at placement as the Trip's origin, and the interval between is exactly one Pool spell. ⚠ **A lifetime column on the Household is wrong** — two of the Pool's four entry routes have no gate at all, and *a column meaningless for half its rows is a column describing something else*. ⚠ **`adr/0023` needs NO amendment**: *"people leave the way they came, through a gate"* says they enter through a gate and leave through a gate, not the same one — ***reading a symmetry of shape as a symmetry of identity invents an obligation the record never stated***, and the invented one was about to buy a saved column for life. **Opens no number and moves no hash** |
| `0128` the gate ships before the comparison that walks through it | 🟢 | **Milestone 11's first decision, 2026-08-20**, with the user in the room, closing [`0035`](0035-hinterlands-and-arrival-through-the-gate.md) decision **1**. Three documents specify arrival as `02 §5.4`'s residential choice model — [`adr/0023`](../docs/adr/0023-immigration-arrives-through-the-gate.md)'s opening line, `CONTEXT.md` → Hinterland and `02 §5.4` itself — **and that model is milestone 16**, so the milestone that owns arrival sits five positions in front of the mechanism its own records say arrival is made of. **The milestone splits**: the gate, the Hinterland, the arrival route and the money door ship at **11**; the comparison, the rejected-arrival reasons and the crossover announcement stay at **16**. ⚠ **`06`'s dependency graph is unchanged and was never wrong** — it is assembled from **milestone rows**, and this dependency is stated inside an **ADR**: ***a graph built by reading the rows is a graph of the edges somebody wrote in a row***, and `06`'s note that slots 9 and 11 to 14 *"admit exactly one arrangement"* was derived from four edges without this one. ⚠ **The cost is stated on day one rather than found in a task**: the arrival door has **no autonomous caller** until 16 — a Command and its tests, the shape `World.cs:853` already names as ***the founding door and not the gate*** — so `06`'s row retires the **anchor** half of its risk and not the *where from* half, and ***a test asserting the city grew would be asserting the Command it just issued***. ⚠ **The crude-rule option was refused by name** as milestone 9's **F13**: a hole that throws is safe, one that returns plausible numbers is a working mechanism that says something false. ⚠ **The give-up rule does not travel with the comparison** — an inflow driven by a Command is still an inflow and [`adr/0006`](../docs/adr/0006-no-collection-grows-with-elapsed-time.md) does not care what called it. **Opens no `adr/0052` number and moves no hash** |
| `0127` the land value target never stops moving, so the question is what the lag rests around | 🟢 | **Milestone 9's decision 6, task 5, 2026-08-20**, and the sixth and last of this milestone's scoped decisions. ⚠ **The thing it worried about is not what the field does.** The minimum-step-of-one is safe — `adr/0122` deleted `w₁`, so the target is exogenous, the gap reaches exactly zero and the operator stops, which the **fifty Cells that never move at all** prove directly. What moves the field is that the *target* never settles: the noise term reads a Segment's volume **at that instant**, so desirability swings with the commute. Measured on `rulesets/fouled.toml`: **185 of 262 Cells move per cadence sample**, widest swing **74,373** raw Q16.16, and **no trend** (−567,787 against −563,871 across a four-Day window). ***A ±1 flicker is a swing of ONE; the observed motion is four orders of magnitude larger, so a test that merely asked whether it moves would have confirmed the wrong phenomenon.*** The **arguable** half — *should* land value swing with rush hour — is separated out and filed rather than decided |
| `0126` a Cell samples desirability at its quadrant centres, and a line source's area mean does not converge | 🟢 | **Milestone 9 task 4, 2026-08-20**, and it is the record of a **refuted derivation**. The reduction from a Tile-resolution composition to a Cell-resolution column was expected to be a quadrature whose order converged; measured, the area mean of a line source **does not converge** — the Segments sit on the Cell's own edges, so every refinement adds samples where the field is unbounded and the mean climbs for ever (−252,011 → −296,734 → −323,982 → −337,116 at orders 1, 2, 4, 8). ⚠ **So the sample set defines the quantity rather than estimating one.** What survives is the weaker and true claim: the **ordering between Cells** is stable — 615 of 630 pairs agree between order 2 and order 8, and all 15 that do not are pairs the fine sample puts within 1% of each other — and ordering is the only thing anything reads. `CellDesirabilitySamplingTests` pins **both** halves, the non-convergence deliberately, so nobody re-derives it as convergent |
| `0125` a ratifier that needs a consumer nobody built is not reachable, so the weights get a floor and a debt | 🟢 | **Milestone 9's decision 5, 2026-08-20**, with the user in the room, closing [`0034`](0034-the-land-value-target-and-the-composed-layers.md) decision **5**. `w₂` and `w₃` get **two §D1 entries each**: a **floor** reachable inside milestone 9 — machine, the acceptance run; world, `rulesets/congested.toml`; quantity, that the field varies, that **both** terms are visible, and the **pollution/noise correlation across Cells** — and the **real** ratifier recorded as **owed**, triggered by **milestone 13, the price surface** (`02 §5.6`'s initial prices), the first named consumer. ⚠ **The decisive fact was read out of `src/`: nothing in the city reads land value.** Outside `MapLayers` the only readers of `LayerCellTable.LandValue` are the headless layer dump — a picture — and `RulesetLoader` resolving a name. So the quantity that would refute an absolute scale **does not exist**, since every quantity of that kind comes from a consumer and every consumer is unbuilt. ***A ratifier that needs a consumer nobody built is not reachable.*** ⚠ **Writing one anyway repeats milestone 7 task 8 one milestone later** — the shed radius named a machine, a world and a quantity correctly and was still unreachable, because ***nothing in [`adr/0052`](../docs/adr/0052-a-hash-bearing-number-is-chosen-with-a-named-ratifier-or-not-at-all.md)'s checklist asks whether the named state can occur***; the difference is that here it was seen **before** the number was written. ⚠ **The two entries must not be read as one — the floor REFUTES and never confirms**, and ***a reachable check standing in the place of an unreachable one is how a number comes to look settled***. ⚠ **The fifth-sighting check is inside the floor deliberately**: industry and roads are both placed in proportion to the same population, so pollution and noise may co-vary, and if they do **no ratio is identifiable** and what is owed is a **hand-authored world** rather than a better number — the pre-flight the shed radius never got. ⚠ **`congested.toml` is named because [`adr/0123`](../docs/adr/0123-desirability-ships-without-its-only-positive-term-and-a-caveat-that-must-travel-gets-a-test.md) established only it and `scarce.toml` have any driver**, and a floor run on a world where one term cannot vary is not a floor. ⚠ **And `w₃` is not merely unratified, it is not yet meaningful**: noise is unbuilt, its output units are task 1's free choice, and `w₃` absorbs them exactly — only the product is constrained, so **`w₃` is chosen after task 1**. `w₂` does not share this. **The weights stand unratified from 9 until 13**, and `06`'s dependency graph forces 13's position, so no re-ordering shortens it |
| `0124` terrain suitability is baked at world creation, and the Layer holes that need it move to milestone 24 — 🔴 **HALF SUPERSEDED by [`adr/0140`](../docs/adr/0140-base-fertility-is-ruleset-data-keyed-by-terrain-type-and-the-old-name-invented-a-field.md), 2026-08-22: the placement stands, the baked artefact does not** | 🟢 | **Milestone 9's decisions 3 and 4, 2026-08-20**, with the user in the room, and its **task 8** discharged with them. Fertility, Sealing's decay, Woodland, replanting and **Water Bodies** all leave 9 for **24**, which **absorbs** them. ⚠ **They are placed rather than deferred**: each needs terrain, and [`adr/0021`](../docs/adr/0021-the-map-is-bounded-procedural-and-terrain-never-enters-a-tick.md) gives terrain one source. **15 was never available for Water Bodies** — water is immutable and seed-generated, so a Water Body has exactly one possible producer. ***A row with nowhere else to be is not deferred; it is placed.*** ⚠ **The sitting found an artefact 24 owes that no document named**: terrain suitability **baked at world creation into a stored per-Cell column**. `adr/0021`'s table says terrain **does** *feed land value and desirability* and **does not** *get read by any Tick phase*, and Fertility is composed **at the point of use, inside a Tick** — ***two cells each correct alone constrain the implementation jointly, and neither says so***, which is `adr/0122`'s shape arriving in a second document. The bake is the resolution and is a **deliverable**, not an assumption. ⚠ **Sealing's decay is blocked twice and only one blocker sits where a tuner looks**: `sealing_decay_tau = 0` is a stated absence the headers explain, **and `MapLayers.Step` never calls `DecaySealing` at all**. Not a wrong description and **not** [`adr/0093`](../docs/adr/0093-a-description-of-the-build-is-where-to-look-and-never-what-you-found.md) — `MapLayers`' doc-comment says *not scheduled* and is accurate — but a fact living in one place and needed in another. **Scheduling it is no freebie**: a Layer cadence is hash-bearing under [`adr/0044`](../docs/adr/0044-the-map-layer-diffusion-cadence-is-the-designers-number-not-the-profilers.md) and owes a ratifier, against a world with varied terrain. ⚠ **No Ruleset was edited**: `congested.toml` and `scarce.toml` are golden baseline artefacts whose **comments move hashes**, so the fact went to `06` — ***where a fact can go to a document or to a hashed artefact, it goes to the document.*** ⚠ **And the move falsified `06`'s own note on 24** — *it depends on nothing in the spine and nothing in the spine depends on it* — **rewritten in the same edit**, because ***a row that absorbs work absorbs the sentences that were true while it was empty***, and leaving it would be `plans/0012` **Cause 1** created deliberately. **Opens no [`adr/0052`](../docs/adr/0052-a-hash-bearing-number-is-chosen-with-a-named-ratifier-or-not-at-all.md) number and records one owed by 24** |
| `0123` desirability ships without its only positive term, and a caveat that must travel gets a test | 🟢 | **Milestone 9's second decision, 2026-08-20**, with the user in the room, closing [`0034`](0034-the-land-value-target-and-the-composed-layers.md) decision **2**. The composition is **two terms** — `− w₂·pollution − w₃·noise` — with amenity and shoreline **absent from the formula rather than defaulted**, and `MapLayers.Desirability` keeping its name and ceasing to throw. ⚠ **The scoping listed the unbuilt terms together and they split**: ***absent in the world and absent in the build are not the same absence.*** Noise where nobody drives is zero **and zero is true** ([`adr/0098`](../docs/adr/0098-a-citizen-travels-in-their-households-mode-and-mode-choice-is-undesigned-rather-than-unbuilt.md): a city before the motor car is a legitimate city); shoreline is zero and **true of every world that exists**, since nothing in `src/` mentions a Water Body at all. **Only amenity is a false zero**, so the decision was only ever about one term. ⚠ **And it is the only *positive* term**, so what ships is **bounded above by zero** — a **disamenity field** whose maximum is clean empty ground far from everything. ***That is not a hole that fails loudly; it is a working mechanism that says something false, and it is the opposite failure from the one the named-hole discipline was built for.*** It ships because the producer **is** the milestone. ⚠ **Amenity's blocker was misrecorded and the correction is small and specific**: scoping said it *needs Businesses and the Provider List*, both of which exist — Businesses shipped at milestone 10 — and `BusinessTable` holds `building`, `balance` and `building_next` and **no kind**, so *distinct Business types* has nothing to be distinct in. ***A blocker named by listing what a mechanism needs is not the one found by opening the table.*** ⚠ **Noise is buildable and its machinery already exists** — `RoadSegmentTable.VolumeForward`/`Backward`, maintained by `TripEngine` **independently of `[traffic]`**, and `LoadOf`, which converts the standing count to a flow by dividing by free-flow time — but **only two of eight Rulesets have any driver**, so the term is inert in six and the acceptance run must be pointed accordingly. **A Ruleset base value was offered and refused**: a number standing in for an absent term can be ratified against nothing. ✅ **The caveat gets a mechanical check rather than prose alone** — a corpus test refusing any document to say desirability is composed without naming amenity's absence, extending the **disqualifier registry**'s idiom from a figure to a claim, **written by task 2 and not before**, because until the claim is true the test passes vacuously and ***a vacuously-passing obligation is an unread one***. **Deleted at milestone 15**, when the absence ends. **Opens no [`adr/0052`](../docs/adr/0052-a-hash-bearing-number-is-chosen-with-a-named-ratifier-or-not-at-all.md) number**; leaves **two** weights owed |
| `0122` land value is not a term in its own target, and a term on both sides of a lag is a gain | 🟢 | **Milestone 9's first decision, 2026-08-20**, with the user in the room, closing [`0034`](0034-the-land-value-target-and-the-composed-layers.md) decision **1**. `02 §2.4` composed desirability with `w₁·land_value` as its opening term **and**, in the next paragraph, moved land value toward desirability — so the stored field was a first-order lag on an affine function of itself, `w₁` was a **gain of `1/(1 − w₁)`** on the other four terms rather than a fifth weight, and at `w₁ ≥ 1` every Cell diverged. ***A term that appears on both sides of a lag is a gain, and calling it a weight hides which way the loop runs.*** ⚠ **The dramatic failure is the unlikely one.** A designer authoring five weights is unlikely to type a one; what would actually have happened is that they author `w₂` for pollution and the city uses `w₂/(1 − w₁)` — ***the number tuned is not the number in force***, and the field says nothing, which is `plans/0012` **Cause 5** with the clause never written rather than merely detached. ⚠ **Three documents claim the momentum is a stabiliser and each describes a loop it cannot see** — `02 §2.4`, [`0009`](0009-map-layers.md) §7, and `MapLayers.Step`'s doc-comment, which states outright *"it cannot oscillate"*. All three are true of a lag chasing a target **somebody else supplies**, and none was ever tested against one that moves when the field moves, because `MapLayers.SetLandValueTarget` **has three test callers and none in `src/`**. ***A sentence whose truth rests on a producer being unbuilt is falsified by the commit that builds it, and that commit edits none of them.*** **Deleting `w₁` restores two and does not reach the third** — the ±1 dead-band question is [`0034`](0034-the-land-value-target-and-the-composed-layers.md) decision **6** and survives `w₁` either way. ⚠ **The formula had three homes and the scoping session recorded two**; the third is `MapLayers.Desirability`'s doc-comment, the copy a *programmer* reads, **invisible to every check in `tests/Borough.Tests/Corpus/`** because those are document-to-document by construction. ***A count of where a claim lives, taken by reading the documents, is a count of the documents.*** **Prestige feedback is *undesigned* and not refused** ([`adr/0070`](../docs/adr/0070-an-unbuilt-mechanism-is-not-a-design-constraint.md)): if it returns it returns as its own mechanism with a stated gain, a bound refused at load and a ratifier, never as the least remarkable item in a list of five. **No bound and no load-time check are written**, because policing a deleted term is machinery with no subject. **Opens no [`adr/0052`](../docs/adr/0052-a-hash-bearing-number-is-chosen-with-a-named-ratifier-or-not-at-all.md) number and moves no hash** — `w₁` never had a value, no Ruleset declares a desirability weight, and `Desirability` throws rather than composing. It **closes** one: the weights this milestone must author are **two**, not five |
| `0121` the commit gate is the assertion tier, and a long test runs post-submit on a machine that is not yours | 🟢 | **Test tiering, 2026-08-19** ([`0032`](0032-test-tiers.md)), with the user in the room, and the decision `plans/0032` said would have to be an ADR. **Three lanes**: a commit is gated on `--filter "tier!=instrument"` — **42s over 1,690 tests, Release, reference machine, nothing else running** — the instruments and the long play states run **post-submit on a runner**, and the whole unfiltered suite stays a **milestone**'s gate. ⚠ **The Definition of done changed by not one word**: it is titled *for any milestone* and was being honoured at every commit, so ***a gate applied more often than it was written to be is not extra safety; it is an unpriced tax on every commit in between.*** ⚠ **The instrument that forced it is 34m22s of a 36m22s full Release run**, and by `plans/0032`'s own discriminator — *what would you do on the day it failed* — the answer is **paste a number into a document**, so the gate was spending nearly all its wall clock on a check whose failure would not stop the commit. ⚠ **The first draft moved them to the milestone boundary and that was worse**: a milestone is a long time to carry an unknown regression, and post-submit also gives the **long play states** a lane they have never had. ***A check that can take an hour is only affordable when nobody is waiting for it***, which is why a notifying gate may be long and a blocking one may not. ⚠ **The sharp edge is [`adr/0106`](../docs/adr/0106-a-wall-clock-budget-names-a-machine-class-and-a-thread-count-or-it-is-not-a-budget.md)**: a hosted runner is **not** the reference i5-10400 and its class is not stable between runs, so the lane may report that an instrument **broke** and may **never** supply a figure a document quotes — ***a number produced on an unnamed machine is not the number it looks like***, which is `plans/0012` **Cause 5** arriving through a new door. ⚠ **The band — *past five minutes a test stifles iteration, ten is the ceiling* — is the operator's preference and no measurement settles it**, so [`adr/0043`](../docs/adr/0043-a-claim-a-measurement-could-settle-must-not-be-settled-by-argument.md) does not reach it and recording whose preference it is beats deriving it from something that looks more objective. ⚠ **The lane has never run**, so under [`adr/0070`](../docs/adr/0070-an-unbuilt-mechanism-is-not-a-design-constraint.md) it is *unbuilt* until its first green run and nothing may lean on it yet. **Opens no [`adr/0052`](../docs/adr/0052-a-hash-bearing-number-is-chosen-with-a-named-ratifier-or-not-at-all.md) number** — no figure here enters the city |

| `0112` the saved set is the hashed set, so a save can compute its own State Hash | 🟢 | **Milestone 8 task 10, 2026-08-18** ([`0030`](0030-save-load.md)), closing the milestone's own open question by finding its premise false. A version-1 save carries the **State Hash**, folded from the **copy** rather than from the world, so it costs the simulation thread **nothing** — and [`adr/0087`](../docs/adr/0087-a-save-is-copied-at-save-cadence-not-read-from-a-past-that-no-longer-exists.md)'s *never on the simulation thread* is **honoured rather than overturned**. ⚠ **Task 6 had recorded this as unbuildable and every sentence of its reasoning is true**: `HandleColumn.Fold` folds the target row's monotonic id and a handle's bytes do not contain one. It does not follow — `Rows.cs:72-73` declares `id` and `generation` as `Saved`, so the id is in the copy, in another table's block. ***A value absent from a column's own bytes can still be present in the copy.*** The two walks were always the same walk: [`adr/0086`](../docs/adr/0086-a-save-has-no-schema-of-its-own-and-the-field-declaration-is-the-format.md) made the declaration the format and it was already the hash, so **the save file is the hash's input written down**. ⚠ **The finding outranks the mechanism, and it is [`adr/0093`](../docs/adr/0093-a-description-of-the-build-is-where-to-look-and-never-what-you-found.md) on a surface that ADR does not list: the false claim was pinned by a *test*.** `A_fold_over_the_bytes_is_not_the_state_hash` is still green and was always about something else — it folds the buffer flat, byte after byte, which nobody would want. ***A test name is a description of the build, so it says which symbol to read and never what is in it***, and **a negative test is the most quotable kind of all, because it reads as a closed door** — three documents cited it and none opened it. ⚠ **The cheap answer would have spent a revisit trigger**: folding the live world was defensible on `adr/0087`'s own arithmetic and would have taken the save's blocking half to **~42 ms at 1M**, the size that ADR's own trigger names as *visible*. ***An argument that a cost is affordable is not a reason to pay it when it can be moved.*** **Consequences**: the header is **nine fields and 60 bytes** (amending [`adr/0111`](../docs/adr/0111-a-save-that-re-derives-nothing-needs-neither-a-seed-nor-a-generator-version.md) on the count alone), **zero is not a sentinel** so there is no unverified save, **the copy is part of the format rather than an optimisation**, and `05 §4` **invariant 6 is a property of every load**. **Opens no `adr/0052` number** — it stores a measurement of the world rather than choosing anything |
| `0111` a save that re-derives nothing needs neither a seed nor a generator version | 🟢 | **Milestone 8 task 4, 2026-08-17** ([`0030`](0030-save-load.md) D6). The version-1 save header is magic, format version, a native-order byte-order sentinel, the **world key**, the Ruleset content hash, and **four world-creation constants** — and **no generator version and no world seed**, which amends [`0086`](../docs/adr/0086-a-save-has-no-schema-of-its-own-and-the-field-declaration-is-the-format.md)'s table of three and `05 §7`'s copy of it. ⚠ **The two are one requirement rather than two**: a seed is consumed only by `WorldKey.FromSeed`, every call site derives and discards, **`World` retains no seed at all**, and no load path calls a generator — so `adr/0021`'s *seed 42 produces different terrain* cannot occur in a version-1 save. ⚠ ***A placeholder inverts the guard***: `adr/0021` **pins** that number, so a terrain build must refuse every pre-terrain save — which an absent version achieves through the format version and `generator_version = 1` defeats by agreeing with itself. **What replaces the third row is a class**, a world-creation value living in the binary rather than in a table, of which the generator version is the unbuilt member and four are built, **each saying *baked into the save* in its own file with nowhere to be baked into**. ⚠ **The live exposure is the Input Log's** — a replay re-runs `SyntheticCity` and `RoadGenerator` from `log.Seed` on every read — so ***a version number guards the artefact that re-derives, not the one that restores***, routed to `InputLogCodec.Version` on the day per `adr/0073`. ⚠ **And the State Hash cannot notice a missing world key**, since `World.Key` folds nothing: a loader that dropped it hashes identically at the load and diverges on the next Tick, which makes it a property of the *test* — only the run-N-more form of the Factorio test can catch it |
| `0110` scratch is a third `Disposition`, because `Derived` is a claim a scratch column does not make | 🟢 | **Milestone 8 task 1, 2026-08-17** ([`0030`](0030-save-load.md) D3). `Disposition` gains **`Scratch`** — neither saved, hashed, nor rebuilt — because `layer_cell.pollution_pass` is a phase-local intermediate and `Derived`'s contract is a **claim** (*a pure function of saved state*) it never made, so the old declaration was **false rather than loose**. ⚠ **The alternative was an exemption list inside the rebuild audit, and this corpus refused that shape once already in the same file**: `Reference` exists because *a list of fields shares its blind spot with the bug it exists to find*. ⚠ **`adr/0070`'s objection — one column is not a general mechanism — is a *prediction*, and the experiment has run**: `Declaration.cs:127` says *only one column needs it* of `Reference` and it is now **seven columns across five tables**, so the only evidence available points the other way and the sentence carrying the prediction is filed to [`0012`](0012-corpus-audit.md). **What makes it better than an exemption is that it is an obligation**: nothing may read scratch outside the phase that wrote it, asserted by filling it with garbage and requiring the hash trace not to move. ⚠ **It creates a way to dodge the audit** — redeclare a failing column — and the defence is that the garbage fill then fails instead, which is a property of the tests rather than of the declaration and is recorded as such |
| `0100` moving the State Hash costs nothing until somebody is carrying a save | 🟢 | **Settled with the user in the room, 2026-08-14**, beside the `Blocking` rename. **The State Hash test classifies a change and does not price one** — `CLAUDE.md`'s *optimisation versus design change* is a taxonomy, and the corpus had been supplying a cost by implication because the re-record is visible and mechanical. While nobody holds a save the cost is **one command, printed by the failing test**, so hash movement may never be cited as a reason to defer, narrow or split work. ⚠ **Written because a sitting proposed exactly that twice in one session** and the user reported having said it *"a million times"* — an unwritten rule is one that gets re-litigated at full price every time. What survives is **attribution**, **collision** and **incidental movement**, all three of which are about legibility or scheduling rather than economics. Its evidence is the rename it was written beside: fifty-two sites, and **the State Hash did not move at all** |
| `0104` a Skill Tier is earned by attendance, and the credential stays a wall | 🟢 | **Grilling `04 §7`, 2026-08-15, with the user in the room.** **A city produces its own skilled workers**: when a Household's Life Stage advances and a new Young Household forms, its adults' tier is read off the **Education** Need that Household accumulated while it had children — so **Sorting is the *fast* channel rather than the only one**. ⚠ **`§7` denied a mechanism the corpus had acquired twice over**: `adr/0011` gave the city children, `CONTEXT.md` → Life Stage already stores *a child's schooling tier*, `CONTEXT.md` → Unemployment already lists *their children escape* as a destitution exit, and `adr/0103` — written the same morning — gave a Household an **Education** Need with **no downstream consumer at all** if nobody is educated here. ***A description of the build is where to look and never what you found*** (`adr/0093`) landing on a **design document**: `§7` described the demographic model and what it described had been superseded twice by ADRs that never came back to edit it. **The mechanism introduces no machinery** — the Need is already the accumulator (`adr/0102`), so there is **no new duration and no new counter**, and the lag is structural: a school pays off one Life Stage later. ⚠ **The cut is a threshold and is licensed exactly where `adr/0102` refused one**, on `CONTEXT.md`'s own ground that a tier is *a category boundary … because a category is not a quantity* — ***a cut is dishonest over a gradient and honest over a category***. **The wall at 2 → 3 is kept and the softness taken at 1 → 2**: if time alone reached the top tier a patient player would never build a school, so failing your schools costs the city its **Office economy** and not its workforce. ⚠ **A tier 0 was floated and refused** — the segments stay **three**, because `CONTEXT.md` → Skill Tier's *thinness is a property of the number of segments* is load-bearing (`adr/0026` built shrinkage-to-anchor for exactly that), nothing would read tier 0 differently from tier 1, and a permanent underclass is a **second wall** in a design that permits one. ⚠ **A claimed cost was withdrawn in the sitting**: the schools → tier 3 → Office → exports loop is **already damped**, by `adr/0032`'s own aside that *good schools raise land value, which prices out the Families the school was built for* — **third time in one sitting that a gap was already answered elsewhere in the corpus**. Opens **two `adr/0052` numbers** as one §D2 row, ratifier **20's long run** |
| `0102` a housed Departure is a comparison the Household re-runs, not a threshold it crosses | 🟢 | **Grilling `04 §6`, 2026-08-15, with the user in the room** — the seven-step chain, never previously examined. A Household leaves because a **Hinterland scored better**, and three mechanisms do three jobs: the **Need** accumulates, the **comparison** decides, the **cadence** decides only when it looks. ⚠ **The decision was already in the corpus, in the list of banned words** — *"Tax tolerance"* says *"tolerance is emergent: a Household compares the city against a Hinterland using the same utility function everyone uses, and leaves or does not"* — so `04 §6` step 7 had been describing a second, incompatible mechanism. ***A banned term carries the decision that replaced it.*** **`sustained degradation` named no duration**: a threshold is `adr/0053`'s Failure Pressure, whose argument is that abandonment **has no actor**, and a Household has one — and it would have rebuilt the cliff `adr/0095` removed two days earlier, three documents away. ⚠ **The tempting repair — the duration survives as the re-evaluation period — is refused by `CONTEXT.md` → Zone Rule's own sentence** (*the sample is when the city notices, not when the Building fails*) and `01 §6`'s *cadences bound perception, not response*. The accumulator needed no re-adding because **it is the Need**. **The failed-occasion trigger is kept and is safe *because* the Need smooths** — the trigger moves *when* you look, not *what* you read — so the two decisions hold each other up and neither is safe alone. **Opens no `adr/0052` number and closes one**: a comparison authorises nothing, so ***a mechanism chosen correctly can retire a number rather than choose one*** (`adr/0059`'s shape a fifth time). ⚠ **The housed channel has two producers and not three**: `adr/0097`'s reach count is the **Destitute** channel's, because `CONTEXT.md` → Unemployment already says ***destitution is a reachability failure wearing a money costume*** — so the Commute Budget is **one ladder feeding two channels** by which side of the ceiling a Citizen lands on |
| `0103` a Need is where a frequent private failure accumulates | 🟢 | **Grilling `04 §6`, 2026-08-15, with the user in the room**, and the sibling of `0102`. **Four Needs — Sustenance, Satisfaction, Education, Health — and a membership test rather than a list**: a Need exists for each **exhaustible** thing a Household consumes and can be refused on arrival; everything **standing** is a term in `02 §5.4`'s utility. ⚠ **A Comfort Need for the commute was drafted and refused** on `CONTEXT.md`'s own warning under *"Tax tolerance"* — ***any named tunable that is really an outcome of the choice model should be suspected of the same error*** — since a Hinterland is already authored with a commute figure, so the Need would have been a second copy of a term able to disagree with the first. ⚠ **Volatility was proposed as the test and the user broke it**: an unanswered police call is volatile and is nothing like an empty shelf, because ***one Household does not have enough incidents to form an opinion***. So the axis is **where the signal is** — frequent and private puts the accumulator on the Household, rare and shared puts it on the **place**, and there is **no safety Need**. **`adr/0032`'s *who moves* taxonomy answered it without amendment and gains a second job**, deciding where a failure accumulates as well as who travels; its demotion of the coverage Layer to an **overlay** is the place-side accumulator, already built. ⚠ **Attended splits again on *can it run out***: a park refuses nobody, so recreation is an Amenity and not a Need — which sorts on **Supply**/**Space**, the vocabulary renamed the previous day for an unrelated reason. ***A vocabulary that names the real axis does work in places nobody renamed.*** **Per-service granularity was chosen over one *Provision* Need** on `LEGIBLE CAUSE`, and the cost is stated: Education and Health are the first Needs with **no Good, no Bin and no price**, so `04 §1`'s Good → Need diagram stops being total and their degradation rule is **owed and undesigned** (`adr/0070`) |
| `0096` the Microscopic Cap derives from the design speed's budget, and not from the top rung's | 🟢 | **Session P, 2026-08-13, with the user in the room**, correcting `0094` on the day. **The Cap is priced against 62.5 ms at 1×, not 15.6 ms at 4×** — pricing it at the top rung chooses `03 §3.9`'s **first** degradation row (every player permanently less accurate) to avoid its **second** (this machine dilates and says so), which is that table read backwards; and `01 §1` makes 4× the rung a large city withdraws, so it is the budget of a speed the binding case is not running. ⚠ **`0094`'s 27–58× gap is withdrawn and there was never a gap**: 186,624 is S2 R2's synthetic **fleet**, labelled *not a stressed count* in `plans/0013`'s own table cell, and the supply side was quoted at 15.6 ms while the clock moved. At the design speed it is **~25,400 Vehicles on one core — identical to what the old clock gave at its top rung**. ***A caveat attached to a number does not travel with it***, and `plans/0013` predicted this failure in the paragraph beneath the table. **Two things recorded rather than decided**: a **fallback tier** below Microscopic is foreseen and deliberately undesigned (`adr/0070`), and a **2- and 4-thread** Lane kernel measurement is owed to S5. **`plans/0012` gains Cause 5 and check 6** on this sighting — and check 6, once built, **caught this ADR's own first version calling 186,624 a whole fleet**: it is 2,592 stressed Segments × 72 Vehicles and an **upper bound**, so the gap is **7.3×** at the design speed rather than nothing. ***A caveat is a claim and travels exactly as badly as a number*** |
| `0094` a Day is 2048 Ticks, because Ticks per Day is a sampling rate and not a length of life | 🟢 | **Session P, 2026-08-13, with the user in the room**, grilling `01 §4`. `TICKS_PER_DAY` **8192 → 2048**, a Tick is **42.1875 s**, a Day is **2m08s at 1×**, and a twenty-hour campaign goes from **140 Days to 562** — which `01 §4`'s twenty-hour marker needs, because Replacement Rate takes generations and 140 Days contains none. **`adr/0019`'s conclusion falls and its prohibition survives.** Its *"same city, same population, same roads, **twice the vehicles**"* needs a Tick whose duration is fixed independently of the Day, which is what `0082` deleted: a commute is 1.39% of a Day at **both** constants, so the constant cancels and no balance moves. **The rule that decides what rescales is one question — *what is it denominated in?*** Ticks (kept, ×4 coarser in-world), in-world time (unchanged, ×4 more per real second), Days (unchanged, ×4 faster in real time). **Only Goods quantities rescale, ×4.** ⚠ **One cost is large and one was overstated the same day**: routing ×4 takes `0013` to ~305% at 4× and ~76% at 1×; the sub-step band goes 21–45 → **84–180**, and the Microscopic Cap consequence is **corrected by `0096`** — priced at the design speed the supply side is unchanged from the old clock's top rung, and the 27–58× gap is withdrawn for want of a denominator. **Its finding is that `0082` left two live consequences standing on the premise it deleted** — including a **revisit trigger whose direction is now backwards** — which is `0089`'s *the obligation a deletion creates is a re-derivation* for the third time in two days |
| `0095` a Commute Budget is three rungs and only the last one refuses | 🟢 | **Session P, 2026-08-13, with the user in the room.** **fast to 20 clock minutes, moderate to 40, unsavoury to 50, refused beyond**, and `commute_budget_minutes` becomes the **ceiling**. **A single threshold makes a cliff out of `adr/0017`**, the one rule that is meant to be graded: a city whose commutes creep from 12 to 19 minutes reports **zero** and then reports a cliff, which arrives exactly when a spatial fix has stopped being cheap. **It does not open `adr/0076`'s Fate set** — three gradations of a cost in *time* all sit on the Budget's side of its second clause, so an unsavoury Trip **completes** — and it is the fourth proposal that looked like it might. The **unsavoury** rung is where `01 §4`'s *housed* Departures come from. It makes `candidates` ratifiable in the way 5b-bis task 4 predicted, because the draws now differ in **outcome** rather than in hit rate. ⚠ **It restates `adr/0089`'s ratio**: 5.6–7.8 map-crossings at the fast rung, **2.2–3.1 at the ceiling**, and the ceiling is what governs separability — `WorldCells = 512` survives with less headroom than it was granted |
| `0092` the region view is the map from far away, and a trajectory names the place it is reported at | 🟢 | **Session P, 2026-08-12, with the user in the room.** The **region view** is a **zoom level of one map** — zoom out and the ground stops being drawn and the Settlement graph is drawn at true positions — not a second screen, because SC4's region screen was a different *game state* and `adr/0020` makes this world singular and live. **`01` had never mentioned it and five other places had**, including `00-vision.md`, so it was a view with no owner. Second half: **`01 §6`'s *expandable by District* was not a choice** — at 16.4 km the map is one Settlement (`adr/0085`, S2 R1.5), so the alternative had no instances. Now **Settlement → District, one hierarchy**, with each trajectory reported first at the level its mechanism makes: commute-shed failures at the Settlement, policy and pooling failures at the District. ***A decision taken when the alternative had no instances is re-decided rather than defended*** |
| `0101` a commute is two journeys, and the Day's shape is a property of the job | 🟢 | **Built 2026-08-15**, and it is the first ADR in this map whose *record* was corrected by its own build in three places — the start draw is triangular rather than uniform, a Shift length is drawn in Ticks rather than in the hours it is authored in, and there is a **fifth** hash-bearing number the decision counted at four. It retires `commute_peak_factor`, which is the **third** §D row to lose a quantity rather than gain a value. Its sharpest finding is not about commutes: ***an intrusive index that unlinks by recomputing its key cannot outlive a change to that key's inputs*** — the roster recomputed a bucket from a `Reference.Severable` Workplace handle, so demolition made `Remove` a silent no-op and every re-employment double-linked, an `adr/0006` leak with a quadratic tail. **The old single-partition roster was safe only by luck**, its key being an id nothing can change |

> the sitting that noticed. **The lesson is not *be careful* — it is that a coverage map maintained by
> hand at the end of a sitting is maintained by whoever remembers**, and neither session D nor session N
> task 1 did. If it stops at some number a third time, the map wants generating from the directory
> rather than writing.
>
> **⚠⚠ IT STOPPED A THIRD TIME, and the trigger this box wrote for itself has fired (2026-08-12).** It
> stopped at **`0070`**, missing **`0071`–`0081`** — every ADR from slice 5a, session F, slice 5a-bis
> and the §A sitting, eleven of them, while the header went on claiming *"69 written, numbered to
> `0070`"*. Four separate pieces of work each added ADRs and none added a row. **This is now three
> observations of one mechanism and they agree**: a hand-maintained index of a directory is maintained
> by whoever remembers, and across four sittings nobody did, three times running.
>
> **So the instruction above is no longer a contingency but a debt**, and it is filed as one in
> [`0012`](0012-corpus-audit.md) rather than left here — a box that predicts its own failure and then
> absorbs it is how a corrected document goes on being wrong politely. **The ADR column of this table
> should be generated from `docs/adr/`**; the *State* and *Note* columns cannot be, and that is the
> point of the split — the generator supplies the row, and a missing note then reads as **unexamined**
> instead of as **absent**, which is the distinction the whole map exists to make.

### What the map says now

**The old summary — *we have argued what the city does thoroughly and how it is built not at all* — is
no longer true, and it is worth saying why.** It was written after session five, when the technical
layer had never been examined. Since then ~~**thirty-one**~~ **thirty-eight ADRs have come out of
building rather than arguing** (`0038`–`0059`, plus `0036`, `0037`, the S2 pair, and `0071`–`0073` and
`0077`–`0079` from Phase 2's first two slices), and they are concentrated in exactly the layer the
summary called untouched. **The gap did not close by argument; it closed by code and spikes producing
decisions.**

**What replaces it is narrower and worse.** The remaining 🔴 is almost entirely **one cluster**:
`03 §5` and the research-written ADRs around it — ~~`0005`, `0007`, `0008`, `0009`, `0012`, `0016`~~
**`0005`, `0007`, `0009`, `0012`, `0016`, since session D closed `03 §5` itself and session F closed
`0008`** — which together are the traffic and movement model. That is **one wall, not many small
gaps**, it gates every Phase 2 milestone, and **it is where the largest measured number in the corpus
lands** (the 861.87% diversion figure). Sessions **D** and **F** have run; **E**, **G**, **H** and
**I** are the rest of it.

**The ⚠ rows are the actionable part, and there are ~~three~~ two.** `adr/0016` and `adr/0009` each
read as decided, each carry a quantitative claim, and **none has a number**. `adr/0043`'s own evidence
is that this exact shape has already produced two false claims from 🟢 rows. **Type them before
grilling them** — if the refuting number and the machine can be named, they are measurable and a
session must not close them.

**`adr/0008` was the third, and how it left the list is worth keeping.** Session F did not find its
number; it **refused the quantity** — the per-mode Commute Budget weight — on the ground that a
weighted score makes the compared quantity differ from the **displayed** one. So the ⚠ discharged
because the claim went away, not because it acquired a value. **That is a third disposition this
legend does not have a mark for**, alongside *measured* and *still owed*, and it is `adr/0059`'s
direction: a number deleted rather than chosen.

# Archive — the session record

**Everything below this line is history and is not maintained.** It is the record of how each question
was raised and argued, session by session, and it is kept because the reasoning is worth more than the
conclusions on their own. It is **not** a status surface: where it disagrees with the ledger above, the
ledger is right, and an item that reads as open below but is absent above has been closed.

**The coverage map that used to live down here has been promoted to §F above and rebuilt.** It was
the only per-document account of what has been examined, and `adr/0043` cites it as evidence — which
a section banner-ed *history and not maintained* cannot carry. The superseded version is retained
below, bannered, because its wording is what §F's list of defects is stated against.

### C — what a Zone Rule's sample is denominated in

- **What a Zone Rule's `sample` is denominated in — and therefore what `§5.7` means by pacing growth.**
  **NEW, and measured into existence by S0b rather than argued.** `§5.7` lists sampling first among four
  growth-pacing mechanisms and justifies it with *"cost is constant regardless of Zone size"*. Both
  halves are now measured and they point in opposite directions. **The cost claim is true and then
  some**: raising the sample **117×** at 1,000,000 Citizens — enough to hold a one-Day revisit period
  over 120,001 Lots — cost nothing outside noise (18.36 s against 19.12 s over 2,000 Ticks), so
  `O(sample²)` in `ZoneSample.Draw` is amortised by the interval and cost has never been the constraint.
  **The pacing claim is the problem**: `sample ÷ interval` is an **absolute** throughput, so the period
  in which a given Lot is looked at once is `Lots ÷ (sample ÷ interval)` — **0.12 Day at 1,000 Citizens
  and 117 Days at 1,000,000.** S0b confirmed this is structural rather than a bad guess by collapsing
  the whole occupancy trajectory onto **τ = (sample ÷ interval) × Ticks ÷ Lots**, agreeing to 1.6 points
  across a 1,000× span in Lots *and* a 117× span in sample. **An absolute throughput against a growing
  city is a decelerating city.** The diegetic argument is where the answer probably lives: `CONTEXT` →
  Zone Rule justifies sampling because *a developer does not evaluate every parcel*, which is a sample
  per **developer**, and the model has exactly one developer per Zone Rule at every city size — **a city
  of a million people has more developers than a city of a thousand.** Note that of §5.7's four
  mechanisms only **capital** scales with the city and capital does not exist yet; sampling and the
  **build rate throttle** are both absolute, so two of the four pace in a unit the section never names.
  ~~**Arguable** — no measurement chooses a denominator — but the revisit period it implies is
  hash-bearing and needs a ratifier under `adr/0052` on the day it is written down. Owner: **task 10b**,
  which is the first Ruleset that models a city and therefore the first that could be wrong about this.~~
  **CLOSED the same day by [`adr/0059`](../docs/adr/0059-a-zone-rules-sample-is-a-revisit-period-so-the-ruleset-states-a-duration.md)**
  — a Zone Rule authors `revisit_ticks`, a **duration**, and the engine derives
  `sample = ceil(Lots × interval ÷ revisit_ticks)`, which is `pollution_decay_ticks`'s shape against the
  Lot count instead of the cadence. **It needed no ratifier after all**: the default is `TICKS_PER_DAY`,
  derived the way tau and the arming stagger were, so §D **loses a row rather than gaining one**.
  Implementation is `0014` **task 11**, sequenced **after slice 8** because it moves the three golden
  baselines and slice 8 task 10 re-records the same files.


### E — price Phase 3 and Map Layer diffusion

- ~~**Price Phase 3, and price Map Layer diffusion.**~~ **DONE**, and both moved something. The engine
  row in [`0013`](0013-tick-budget.md) went from an *inferred* 60% to a **measured 67%** at 4×, over a
  whole Tick rather than one phase; the **sort was found and identified** — 121.6 → 198.3 ns per due
  Rule across two decades, a 1.63× rise where `log₂` predicts 1.7× — and it is real but not a wall.
  **Map Layer diffusion turned out to be the only row in the ledger with no guess in it**, because
  residency saturates at ~256 emitters against a city of 120,001 Buildings, and it costs 0.5–15.8 µs
  per Tick amortised on any budget.

The row says *bounds* rather than *picks* deliberately. Note what constrains it today: *sample N and take the best* has **nothing to rank**, because the create predicate is a boolean with no score, so `N` buys throughput and not choice until `02 §5.4`'s choice model exists. **`02 §5.3`'s `N` is not this number** — that one is dwellings a Household considers |

## Where we got to

Settled across the two sessions so far, in the order it happened:

| Decision | Record |
|---|---|
| One clock, no calendar, no aging | `adr/0010`, `adr/0011` |
| `TICKS_PER_DAY = 8192`, 16 Ticks/s reference, pacing lives in the speed ladder | `adr/0019` |
| No hour or minute — a sun arc with named phases, Commute Budget as a wedge on it | `02-sim §1.2`, `01-player §7` |
| One live world; **Settlement** derived from commute range; region-of-tiles rejected | `adr/0020` |
| Bounded procedural rectangle, sparse Chunks, terrain at construction time only, terraforming priced by haul | `adr/0021` |
| Land is a stock the city spends; fertility composed not generated; Woodland the one generated resource | `adr/0022` |

Session three — the density and demographics thread:

| Decision | Record |
|---|---|
| Density is a **cap**, not a designation; capacity not quality; trades Land for Materials | `adr/0025` |
| Two routes to density — **subdivide** vs **stack** — separated by Access Points per capita | `adr/0025` |
| A Building holds many Occupants but **aggregates logistics, never decisions** | `adr/0025`, `CONTEXT` → Building |
| Zoning is **exclusion**, not placement; nothing permitted by default; agriculture is protection | `CONTEXT` → Zone |
| Immigration is **arrivals through the gate** as ordinary Trips — no demand scalar anywhere | `adr/0023` |
| The Outside is **four Hinterlands**, authored in domain units, drained as a stock | `adr/0023` |
| **Money is conserved**, sourced and sunk only at the gate; the city has a balance of payments | `adr/0024` |
| Policy is a lever set, never a prescription | `adr/0024`, `CONTEXT` → Policy |
| **Preference is drawn per Household** from a stage-constrained range, and persists for life | `adr/0027` |
| Failure is **spatial as well as citywide**; notifications fire on named trajectories, never thresholds | `01-player §6` |
| **Office** — one family, all tiers, no freight but business-travel Trips; it is what makes a downtown | `04-economy §1` |
| **Amenity** = walkable variety, which is what rewards mixed use | `CONTEXT` → Amenity |

Session four — pressure and difficulty (`01-player §5`):

| Decision | Record |
|---|---|
| Pressure is **two axes — the Bill and the Clock** — not three layers. Every §6 trajectory maps to one or both | `01-player §5.1`, `CONTEXT` → The Bill / The Clock |
| **Difficulty lives outside the map.** The dial changes the cost of *recovering* from a mistake, never the cost of the mistake | `01-player §5.5`, `CONTEXT` → Intensity Dial |
| The dial's whole surface is **the Hinterland plus one interval** — no new parameters, no branch written on intensity | `01-player §5.4` |
| The dial **never touches an instrument**. Checkable via the Input Log: intensity is an input, notification verbosity is not | `01-player §5.7` |
| A **Shock** is a movement in a Hinterland's authored figures, full stop. Closes ledger #19's "one home for the shock layer" | `01-player §5.2`, `CONTEXT` → Shock |
| A **Disaster** is world-scheduled; the **city sets severity**, because containment is an ordinary Trip that can fail | `01-player §5.2–5.3`, `CONTEXT` → Disaster |
| Catalogue: **Flood, Urban fire, Wildfire** ship. Utility failure blocked; outbreak, earthquake, wind deferred | `01-player §5.2` |
| A **Mode** is a preset plus a lock policy, fixed at world creation. The lock is opted into, never imposed | `01-player §5.6`, `CONTEXT` → Mode |
| Randomisation is **orthogonal to Mode** — the Mode sets a range, the seed picks the point. Same shape as `adr/0027` Taste | `01-player §5.6` |

Session four continued — `03-agent-architecture`:

| Decision | Record |
|---|---|
| **Invariant 6 was inverted.** It required Microscopic segments to agree with the VDF; §3.2's whole justification is that the VDF is wrong there. Rescoped to the **audit** | `03 §4`, `03 §3.5` |
| The **divergence metric** is travel time against the statistical prediction, on audited *unstressed* segments only. It also gives §3.6's blind spot a detector | `03 §3.5` |
| The Microscopic tier gets an **acceptance suite** derived from its own justification — queueing, spillback, hysteresis — with an admission rule and the audit as its discovery route | `03 §5.1` |
| `T_high`/`T_low` are **measured, not chosen** — sweep one Input Log and find where the boundary stops mattering | `03 §3.3` |
| **Walking is always Statistical**, permanently — pedestrian networks do not saturate, so `distance/speed` is exact. Reopened only by transit | `03 §3.7`, `CONTEXT` → Fidelity |
| **One graph with mode masks**, not two networks. It is what makes Severance emergent | `03 §3.7`, `CONTEXT` → Road Graph |
| **Microscopic Cap** (was *Fidelity Budget*) — a world constant, never host-tunable, or one Input Log yields two cities | `03 §3.9`, `CONTEXT` → Microscopic Cap |
| Reaching the Cap is **not a failure mode**. Removed from `01-player §6`; `HONEST DEGRADATION` is met by §7's existing overlay-honesty rule | `03 §3.9`, `01-player §6` |
| **VDF** finally defined — and *why* it is structurally wrong under saturation, which is the two-tier design's whole justification | `CONTEXT` → Volume-Delay Function |
| **Contiguity needs a second trigger.** Stress decides where microscopic simulation *begins*; **downstream blocking** decides where it *extends*, event-driven so it keeps pace with a jam | `03 §3.3` |
| **A segment with a non-empty queue never demotes.** The queue is what demotion would discard, deleting hysteresis at the moment it would be observed | `03 §4`, invariant 3 |

Session five — the services layer, and it went much further than services:

| Decision | Record |
|---|---|
| `Service` was **one verb over four unrelated mechanisms**. The axis that sorts them is **who makes the journey** | `adr/0032`, `CONTEXT` → Delivery Mode |
| Services are delivered by **Trips**; the coverage Map Layer is demoted to an **overlay** | `adr/0032` |
| **School is an Attended Trip**, which finally gives `adr/0010`'s **Sorting** a mechanism | `adr/0032`, `adr/0010` note |
| A drop-off is a **waypoint on the parent's commute**, never a planned itinerary. *A Household chooses providers and modes; it never chooses an itinerary* | `adr/0032` |
| Mode is a **Provider List attribute**; re-evaluation is an Event Wheel countdown **or a failed Trip** | `adr/0032`, `CONTEXT` → Household |
| **TRANSIT SHIPS.** The largest open question in the project, closed | `adr/0029` |
| **Right-of-way is transit's only axis** — shared / reserved / separated. No mode list, no unlock ladder | `adr/0029` |
| Player buys **vehicles**; frequency is emergent from round-trip time | `adr/0029` |
| **Crime is reopened** — thread E's deferral overturned deliberately | `adr/0030` |
| An Incident has a **victim, a place, and a response — and no perpetrator** | `adr/0030` |
| Crime reads **employment, never income** — and unemployment here is a reachability failure the player caused | `adr/0030` |
| Police suppress the **symptom, never the cause**, which is what keeps neglect uncontainable | `adr/0030` |
| Safety presence counts as an **`adr/0027` Taste axis**, so under-provision changes the *population*, not the score | `adr/0032` |
| **Good, Utility and Money are three families of one Resource**, split on transport alone | `adr/0031` |
| **The constraint is chain depth, not list length.** Five Goods → nine Resources, zero added depth | `adr/0031` |
| **Waste is a Good.** Its collection is a Shipment; it can be exported at a price | `adr/0031` |
| Nobody draws a utility network — utilities **ride the Road Graph and pool by District** | `CONTEXT` → Utility |
| **Capacity ≠ Storage.** Money is unbounded capacity; Power is zero storage | `adr/0031` |
| **Schooling is accumulated per completed Trip**, in three levels under three tiers. Primary is a **gate** | `CONTEXT` → Schooling |
| A school sets the tier of **a Household that does not exist yet**; the lag is made legible by showing the **cohort in flight** | `adr/0010` note |
| **In Education** is a state of a Young Household, not a stage. *Life Stage is composition* | `CONTEXT` → In Education |
| The tier wall stands, **re-justified as a category boundary** — learnable skill vs credential | `adr/0026` note |
| **Experience is continuous within a tier**, and it is the design's only **productivity growth** | `adr/0026` note |
| **The city has no central bank, correctly.** Three external anchors do the damping | `adr/0026` note |

Prior art for all of the above is now recorded in [`references.md` §9](../docs/references.md) — SC4's cap, SC2013's road-derived cap, CS's command model, and the Peanut Butter Point as the genre's most instructive failure.

Through-lines worth holding on to, because they decided several arguments each:

- **Ratios are real; units are invented.** The simulation holds only Ticks and Tiles. Anything expressed in seconds or metres is an exchange rate chosen outside the simulation and free to change. The one number that is *not* an exchange rate — `TICKS_PER_DAY` — is a balance constant, because it fixes the ratio of a commute to a life.
- **Scarcity is a gradient, never a wall.** Parking widens the shed (`adr/0009`); Timber lengthens the haul then becomes an import (`adr/0022`); terrain is a price rather than a refusal (`adr/0021`); a drained Hinterland gets expensive, never empty. Anything that fails hard should be suspected.
- **Author in domain units, never in utility units.** The Hinterland argument generalises: `rent §620` is a number a designer can defend and a player can read; `V = 4.7` is neither. Any constant that cannot be stated in something the player already sees is a balance hazard.
- **Prefer a crossover to a threshold.** Two curves meeting is a fact nobody chose; a threshold is always somebody's guess. Regime changes should be detected as crossovers wherever one exists.
- **An authored constant is acceptable when it is the same thing the player is shown.** A magnitude threshold in a config file fails; a named failure mode in a table the player reads passes. Corollary: **duration constants beat magnitude constants**, because durations are scale-free and mean the same thing in a village and a metropolis.
- **An exploit is usually a missing mechanism, not a missing rule.** Five were found and closed this session — density spam, Office monoculture, universal mixed use, public hiring as an unemployment sink, sparse streets for mega-Lots — and *every one* resolved into something the design already implied. Reaching for a rule first is the tell that the mechanism has not been found yet. Where a rule is genuinely needed, the test is: **would you have written it without knowing about the exploit?** If the only justification is "otherwise players do X," it is a patch, and players read patches as arbitrary because they are.
- **Growth changes source; it never stops.** Extraction (spend the Hinterland) gives way to cultivation (house families). There is no population ceiling, only a transition between engines.
- **Every specialisation starves an input only a different specialisation produces.** Five instances have now arrived from unrelated directions: all-high-density starves families and therefore internal generation; all-Office starves the tier-2 pipeline that staffs it; all-wealthy starves the tier-1 labour the skill ladder is built from; all-clean starves Materials and therefore construction; all-urban starves fertility and therefore Food. **This is why balance is the winning play rather than the virtuous one** — nobody wrote a rule, the chains are simply circular. It is also why Policies bite here where SC4's ordinances did not: theirs moved a demand scalar, ours act on the chain that produces the thing.
- **Goods are price-constrained; people are rate-constrained.** Any material shortage can be imported away forever at a cost. A demographic shortage cannot — Hinterlands recover at a rate no amount of money exceeds. **The labour pipeline is therefore the hardest constraint in the design**, harder than Food or Materials, and the one that ultimately forces the balance above.

Added session five:

- **Repeated exceptions on one axis mean the axis *is* the abstraction.** Four things had been excepted from *"it's a Good"* — Money, the utilities, Waste, service capacity — and every exception was on **transport** alone. `adr/0024` spent a whole ADR proving Money is Good-like-but-not-a-Good, and the proof reduced to one boolean. When a rule has accumulated four exceptions, stop writing the fifth.
- **The axis that separates systems is usually *who moves*, not what they provide.** Sorting the Services by delivery rather than by function is what made them tractable, and it immediately exposed that `adr/0026`'s demand-determined staffing rule was never universal.
- **Generalise the mechanism; keep the families named.** `CONTEXT` is domain language, not implementation. *"They all sit in Bins"* is an implementation fact, and flattening nine things into one player-facing list makes the model less legible, not more. `Zone` had already found this shape.
- **The honest model and the cheap one keep coinciding.** The walked school run costs nothing in CPU *and* is what the player should be designing toward. An Incident with no perpetrator avoids an unfounded claim *and* avoids an activity model for offenders. When they diverge, suspect the model before the budget.
- **A category is not a quantity.** *Scarcity is a gradient, never a wall* is the most-repeated rule here, and the tier 2 → 3 boundary is the one place it correctly does not apply — because credentials are not a rung, and no amount of experience makes a surgeon. A wall is defensible exactly when it is a category boundary and indefensible when it is a balance constant wearing one.
- **For a lagged consequence, show the pipeline, not the output.** Schooling's effect arrives a full Life Stage after its cause, which looked like a `LEGIBLE CAUSE` catastrophe. It isn't, because the cohort is in flight and its state is readable *now*. The counter-intuitive part: **accumulation is what makes the lag legible** — a conferred model would have nothing in flight to show.
- **Deferrals must be overturned deliberately, never routed around.** Crime was reopened by naming thread E's decision, arguing it, and recording the reversal. The failure mode is a design that quietly grows the thing it decided against, one defensible exception at a time — which is also why the five-Good *count* was replaced by a depth *rule*: a quota cannot say why Waste is fine and Steel is not.

**The honest summary, updated after session five:** we have argued *what the city does* thoroughly and *how it is built* not at all — and the gap **widened**. Session five added transit vehicles to the traffic model, ~50% more Trips to the peak, a new event source, a District-level flow solve, four Resources, and a Rule-engine coupling that `CONTEXT` → Rule does not currently permit. Every one of those assumes a technical layer nobody has stress-tested.

**The deferral of `05` at the top of session five was correct and must not be repeated.** The argument for going to services first was that `05`'s job is to size a budget against the *complete* inventory of systems, and the inventory was missing a tier. That inventory is now materially complete. `05` is owed, and so is `02 §4`.
